RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 13, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Ms.
Ash and Mr. Collins.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  T here will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Case, and pursuant to support received by Mr.  Pierson
and Mr. Brown, agenda item #15 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Dr. Rinne,
and pursuant to support received by Mr.  Pierson and Mr. Brown, agenda item #25(c) was removed
from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 22, 2009.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
 [CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JULY 6, 2009.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.

easterly 10 ft. of Lot 2, Block One, Bike Trail Addition, Cheyenne, Laramie County, WY, containing
approximately 21,532 sq. ft., or 0.49431 acres, more or less (generally located southeast of east 2  St.nd
and Russell Ave. Intersection, extending to the west side of Morrie Ave.) to Frontier Refining, Inc.,
which sale will benefit the economic development of the municipality pursuant to Wyo. Stat. §15-1-112
(b).  Estimated appraised value of said property to be sold is $77,500.00.  There being no comments
or discussion and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.

“A RESOLUTION ACKNOWLEDGING RECEIPT OF AND APPROVING THE ‘ EAST
PERSHING BOULEVARD CORRIDOR AND INTERSECTIONS PLAN’ PREPARED BY AYRES
ASSOCIATES FOR THE CHEYENNE METROPOLITAN PLANNING ORGANIZATION.” Frank
Cole, Co le S hopping Ce nter, expressed co ncerns wi th po tential f uture i ncreased t raffic o n E ast
Pershing; designing the proposed roundabout to accommodate semi-trucks delivering goods to Cole
Shopping Center, and residential parking issues on Pershing Blvd., west of the Converse intersection,
and recommended reviewing plans to further widen Pershing.  John Fafoutakis spoke in opposition to
a roundabout traffic design at the Pershing/Converse intersection area, citing pedestrian crossing and
traffic safety concerns.  G ene M acDonald, Ayers and Associates, provided information o n modern
roundabout traffic designs and safety features resulting in decreased vehicular accidents, pedestrian
safety, and slower t raffic s peeds. H e e xplained t he d ifference b etween t raffic c ircles a nd m odern
roundabouts, a nd d iscussed c orridor p lanning e lements involving nearby residential p roperties a nd
right-of-way and access issues.  He submitted a spreadsheet (on file with City Clerk’s office) containing
the potential cost of property acquisitions relative to additional right-of-way to realign and widen E.
Pershing Blvd.  U pon inquiry, Mr. MacDonald reviewed the elements contained in engineering and
traffic data that were studied relative to modern roundabout vs. standard intersection designs.  Pacomio
Sandoval expressed concerns with a roundabout located in Rawlins and driver navigational difficulties,
and s uggested t he i tem be  p ostponed a nd t hat C ouncil m embers travel t o R awlins t o vi ew t he
problematic intersection.  Mr. Brown moved to adopt, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
MacDonald reviewed the physical placement of pedestrian access/crossings in modern roundabouts,
stating t hey a re u sually “ pulled b ack” f rom t he c ircle; a ddressed p ossible e ncroachment o n s ome
residential pro perties t hat s hould b e addres sed duri ng f inal des ign wo rk, an d reviewed po tential
incorporation of turn lanes, and effect of the project on sidewalks/driveways in the area.  Comments
followed on a modern roundabout concept, widening of the roadway and working with residents who
may be affected, West Pershing Blvd. issues, and non-feasibility, due to cost, of the City purchasing
several properties in the area.  Upon inquiry, Mr. MacDonald advised the design plan is 35% complete
and explained, due to unknown factors and details, by the time a final design is accomplished it often
times has multiple changes from the preliminary design.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present.  U nder oth er City business, Ms. Case commented that she did not
consider this agenda item to be a routine item and that it should not have been placed on the Consent
Agenda.  (#5146)

Consideration of Request for Proposal for a contract between the City of Cheyenne, Cheyenne
Fire & Rescue D epartment a nd N oel G riffith & Associates, a rchitect for p rofessional services for
conceptual f ire s tation d esign, s ite a ssessment, p rogram d evelopment, p roject bu dgeting a nd
promotional services (2007-2010 Optional 1% Sales & Use Tax).  Mr. Valdez moved to approve in an
amount not to exceed $63,480.00, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upo n inquiry regarding a rece nt legal
notice published in the newspaper referencing Cole Fire Addition, Frank Cole, developer, advised he
did not have knowledge of the legal notice and that the notice could pertain to other properties within
the Addition (not the City’s fire station site off Dell Range), which also includes Wal-mart and Sam’s
Club properties.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the
exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no.” (#5419)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-30-09 for furnishing 10,000 Recycle Refuse Carts for the C ity of
Cheyenne Sanitation Department (Solid Waste Management Account).  Motion (per Consent Agenda)
carried (Otto Environmental Systems, Eloy, AZ; amount not to exceed $456,100.00).
Consideration o f Bid #R-29-09 f or furnishing o ne new Rubber Tired Loader from Ho nnen
Equipment of Mills, WY for the Sanitation Department.  M r. Valdez moved to a ccept the bid from
Honnen Equipment in an amount not to e xceed $148,141.00, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  D iscussion
followed o n t he nu mber o f bi ds r eceived, bi d s pecifications, r ecommended bi d s election, a nd
communication received from one of the bidders involving equipment specifications.  Upon inquiry,
Dennis Pino, Solid Waste Director, advised 3 bids were received and provided information relative to
equipment specifications, the type of machinery the City uses and can provide maintenance for through
Fleet Maintenance, and loader bucket size and equipment safety features.  C omments continued on
discretionary f actors i nvolving t he bi d s election r ecommendation p rocess, e quipment c ost, a nd
suggestion of rejecting all bids and re-bidding for the equipment.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Pino stated if all
bids were rejected, this would set back utilization of the equipment by a month and they would have
to reorganize the time frame for projects.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body present with the exception of Mr. Pierson voting “no”. 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-32-09 for furnishing one new cab & chassis with collection body, from
Lariat International, Cheyenne for the Sanitation Department -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-1-10 for furnishing one new truck with lube body, from Peterbilt of
Wyoming, Cheyenne for Fleet Maintenance Department -- referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-2-10  for furnishing one new cab & chassis truck from Peterbilt of
Wyoming, Cheyenne for Forestry Department -- referred to Finance Committee.
ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
[CA] Acknowledgment of the “2007 Cheyenne Greenway Extension 35% Design” report. Motion
(per Cconsent Agenda) carried.  
Announcement was made of the Appointment of James E. Elias as Public Works Director for
the City of Cheyenne, to be confirmed July 27, 2009.
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John F afoutakis co mmented o n cer tain ar eas i nvolving p rojected Ci ty r evenue sh ortfalls;
prioritization of projects, and past expenditures for certain projects, and expressed concerns with taser
use by law enforcement officers nationwide and the U.S. justice system.  During comments on taser
use and the justice system, Mr. Brown raised a point of order that comments being made did not pertain
to City business with Mayor Kaysen requesting Mr. Fafoutakis to bring his comments to conclusion.
Mr. Brown stated he had received communication from a constituent regarding a sanitation bill
relative to fees assessed for the curbside recycling pilot program and that he would be reviewing the
matter with Public Works staff.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 7:03 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
July 27, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Kristy Anderson, Deputy City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White,
City Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  T here will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence o n t he agenda.)  U pon request by Mr. V aldez, and pursuant t o su pport received by Mr.
Collins and Mr. Brown, agenda item #11 was removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Ms.
Case, an d pursuant t o s upport received by Mr. Brown an d M r. Co llins, agenda item #22(a)  was
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Upon request by Ms. Broyles, and pursuant to support received
by Mr.  Brown and Mr. Pierson, agenda item #14 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JULY 13, 2009.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
 [CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JULY 20,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Collins  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Mr.
Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC
Services ($30,596.20), and vacated Council Chambers.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote Mr. Brown
returned to Council Chambers.

OF POWDERHOUSE ROAD, NORTH OF AND ADJACENT TO LIFE CARE CENTER).” Kelly
Hafner, Benchmark Engineers, representing developer, advised the annexation is in conjunction with
the z one c hange a nd p lat re quest.  L inda Cross, C entury Pa rk Associates, d eveloper of Life Care
Centers of America, provided a h andout (o n file with City Clerk’s Office) with samples o f similar
projects that have been developed in other states and advised they intend to build an assisted living
facility on the property.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on
third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Comments in favor of annexation followed with
discussion h eld on  i ncremental a nnexations a nd having proper i nfrastructure i n p lace p rior to
development.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#3867)

APPOINTMENTS:
Appointment of James E. Elias as Public Works Director for the City of Cheyenne.  Mr. Collins
moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, James Elias introduced himself and thanked
the Mayor and City Council members for the privilege to be considered for the appointment.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who
did not vote (Mayoral appointment).  Mayor Kaysen announced Mr. Elias would receive his oath of
office at 8:30 a.m. in City Council Chambers on July 28, 2009.  He expressed appreciation to Vicki
Nemecek for serving as Interim Public Works Director.

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-08-10 | CLIP_ID: 105 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 105

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 10, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
RECOGNITION OF CITY EMPLOYEES FOR THEIR INVOLVEMENT WITH “HONOR
FLIGHT WYOMING.” Mayor Kaysen expressed appreciation to Mandy Liley, Blake Suter and Greg
Martinez, Taco John’s Ice and Events Center staff, for their assistance with the inaugural honor flight
May 19-20, 2009 involving World War II Veterans, noting the Ice and Events Center served as the
departure site. He presented Ms. Liley, individually, and Mr. Suter and Mr. Martinez, as part of the
team, with Certificates of Appreciation issued to them by the Honor Flight Organization.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  T here will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.).  M r. Collins moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.
Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JULY 27, 2009.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
 [CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST 3,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Spiker  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez. Mr.
Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC
Services ($227.04), and vacated Council Chambers.  Mayor Kaysen declared a conflict of interest due
to a voucher payable to him as a reimbursement ($321.05), relinquishing duties of the chair to Council
President Collins, and vacated Council Chambers.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote Mr. Brown and
Mayor Kaysen returned to Council Chambers with Mayor Kaysen re-assuming duties of the chair.
Mayor Kaysen o pened a  P ublic Meeting  t o r eceive c omments a nd input o n t he Cheyenne
Transit Program Fiscal Year 2010, 49 U.S.C., §5307 Federal Operating Grant; required changes for
complimentary p aratransit b us op erations a nd s ervice h ours; a nd m inor r oute m odifications to
accommodate A merican Re covery an d Re investment A ct (E conomic Stimulus F unding) f unded
improvements for up to approximately 53 bus pullouts/shelters and/or benches at designated areas.  Joe
Dougherty, Transit Director, provided information on the following: Purpose of the public meetings
for t his i tem as  wel l as  t he next agen da i tem; pro gram f unding am ounts an d us es; pro gram
requirements; s ervices, f ares an d h ours of s ervice; pro posed ro ute ch anges an d ef fective dat e;
complimentary paratransit routes and service hours; different rider/user categories and f unding
assistance eligibility requirements, and proposed effective date for changes in operations and service
hours (Oct. 1, 2009).  He further explained user growth observed in the paratransit system, future and
existing bus stop locations and styles, and pullout options.  He advised the program has received grant
funding to provide up to 42 locations with bus pullouts, shelters and/or benches, and stated construction
will begin this fall and is anticipated to be completed next summer. There being no further comments
or input and purpose of the meeting having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public meeting.
Mayor Kaysen opened a Public Meeting to receive comments and input on the submission of
a grant application to the Federal Transit Administration, an Operating Agency of the United States
Department of Transportation, as authorized by 49 U.S.C., §5307, for approximately $881,984.00 in
Federal Funds.  Joe Dougherty, Transit Director, advised he was available to answer any questions.
There being no comments or input and purpose of the meeting having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed
the public meeting.
Mayor Kaysen opened a Public Hearing to identify the need and opportunity, and to explore
funding and alternative solutions for development of the Saddle Ridge Park.  Teresa Moore, Parks and
Recreation Dept., provided information on the proposed park, stating grant funding is being pursued
through the Wyoming Business Council, under its Business Ready Communities - C ommunity
Enhancement program, for grant funding to assist with building the neighborhood park, located within
Saddle Ridge subdivision in east Cheyenne, as an urban quality amenity.  She stated the developer has
donated 14 acres for the project of which seven acres are proposed for park development at this time
and as part of the grant application.  Ms. Moore advised the park will serve residents and workers in
the workforce housing and business area, and will be located in the vicinity of the Cheyenne LEADS
business park and the new Saddle Ridge school, and also near the Greenway path system, making the
area an attractive amenity to businesses and individuals looking to locate in Cheyenne, enhancing the
area’s quality of life and promoting recreational values.  She indicated the developer is also donating
assistance with site grading and utility stubs.  Ms. Moore clarified the grant, if approved, is a 50/50
matching grant request for $285,820.00 for utilities, pathways, plazas, restrooms, shelter, landscaping,
and two pedestrian bridges to cross drainage ways, and stated the City’s match will come from private
donations, in-kind labor, 5th penny funds, community facility fees, and value of the donated land.  Ms.
Moore stated another grant request has been submitted for $105,000.00 from the Recreation Mill Levy
administered th rough th e school d istrict, w hich, if a pproved, w ill c over th e City’s p ortion for site
grading, playground, 2 multi-purpose sports fields and a park sign. There being no further comments
or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.

“A RESOLUTION ADOPTING AN AMENDMENT TO THE FUTURE LAND USE PLAN
MAP IN PLANCHEYENNE, THE MASTER PLAN FOR THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND THE
COMPREHENSIVE PLAN FOR PORTIONS OF LARAMIE COUNTY, AMENDING THE LAND
USE PLAN MAP CATEGORY FROM OPEN SPACE AND PARKS TO COMMUNITY BUSINESS
FOR A PPROXIMATELY 28 A CRES A T T HE S OUTHEAST QUA DRANT OF  T HE
INTERSECTION OF WINDMILL ROAD AND DELL RANGE BOULEVARD.”  Mr. Brown advised
there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to
adopt, seconded by Mr. Collins. Dr. Rinne moved to postpone to Sept. 14, 2009, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  It was advised postponement would allow time for further clarification on issues that have
been r aised i nvolving th e p roperty.  D iscussion f ollowed i n s upport a s w ell a s n on-support of
postponement; purpose of the resolution, which is to change language in the PlanCheyenne document;
PlanCheyenne as a plan only and not a codified document; future options involving sale of the parcel
and use of proceeds, and open space use relative to the community business category.  Upon inquiry,
Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director, advised open space is a potential use of community business
type property and that a plan change to community business may not necessarily change the current use
of the land. Discussion followed on open space vs. possible business use(s) on the property; traffic
concerns affiliated with t he site, o fficial zo ne change category for t he pr operty not being achieved
through approval of the resolution, and the resolution being construed as serving as the first step toward
potential re-zoning, declaring of surplus and disposal of the property.  In response to query concerning
what may be achieved during the postponement timeframe, Mayor Kaysen stated postponement would
provide the opportunity to review areas of concern, and to provide clarification and information from
Planning staff regarding questions that have been raised.  Motion to postpone to Sept. 14, 2009 carried.
Voting “yes” –  all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Mr. Collins
voting “no”.  Due to the one month postponement time frame, Mayor Kaysen advised the item will not
be on the next Public Services Committee meeting agenda; it will return on the Sept. 14, 2009 City
Council meeting agenda for referral back to Public Services Committee.

OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
Mr. Collins introduced a “Cooperative Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming
Department of Transportation for the construction of a West Lincolnway Transit Bus Pull-out between
Westland Road and Interstate 80 (American Recovery and Reinvestment Act funding)”.  Mr. Collins
reviewed the Transit Bus Pull-out amenity and the timeliness involving action on the agreement due
to WYDOT’s current construction schedule for the West Lincolnway reconstruction project.  Mayor
Kaysen referred the item to Finance Committee.  
Due to the timeliness to provide consideration of approval for the aforementioned Cooperative
Agreement with WYDOT, Mayor Kaysen announced a Special Meeting of the Governing Body of the
City of Cheyenne, Wyoming will be held Monday, August 17, 2009 at 12:05 p.m. (or immediately
following adjournment of the Vouchers Meeting (beginning at noon) if that meeting extends past 12:05
p.m.) i n C ity C ouncil C hambers, 2 101 O ’Neil Ave nue, to consider a pproval o f t he C ooperative
Agreement b etween t he C ity o f Ch eyenne an d W yoming Depa rtment o f T ransportation f or t he
construction of a West Lincolnway Transit Bus Pull-out between Westland Road and Interstate 80
(American Recovery and Reinvestment Act funding).  He further stated there will be no other business
conducted at the Special Meeting.  Mr. Pierson advised he would be unable to be present at the Special
Meeting.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 6:50 p.m.

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-08-24 | CLIP_ID: 107 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 107

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
August 24, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms . Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Mr. Pierson.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
RECOGNITION OF POLICE CHIEF ROBERT D. FECHT ON HIS RETIREMENT. Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, read a proclamation into the re cord honoring Chief Fecht for his 19 y ears of
service with the Cheyenne Police Department and proclaiming Aug. 31, 2009 Robert D. Fecht Day.
Mayor Ka ysen expressed a ppreciation to Chief Fe cht and pr esented him with a plaque and the
proclamation.  Several members of the governing body expressed appreciation to Chief Fecht for his
years of service and support.  Chief Fecht thanked the Mayor, Council members, his officers, staff and
co-workers for the privilege of working for the City.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the desig nation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.).  Mayor Kaysen advised Agenda Item #19 was being withdrawn from the City
Council meeting agenda to allow for additional review and research to be conducted.  Upon request by
Ms. Case, and pur suant to support rec eived by Mr. Collins and Mr. Brow n, agenda item #15 wa s
removed from the Consent Agenda.  Mr. Collins moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST 10,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
 [CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST 17,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM SPECIAL MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST 17,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez  moved to approve pa yment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Brown.
Upon inquiry regarding a vouche r pa yable to Robert D. Clary  PE, PC  ($450.00), Ma yor Ka ysen
clarified the expenditure was for services involving a proposed City pocket park site located downtown
on West Lincolnway to ensure adjacent building retaining walls were safe prior for future public access.
It was noted that long-term plans for the site would, hopefully, involve commercial use purposes.
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present. 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing  to receive comments and input on the submission of
an application to the Wyoming Business Council, under Business Ready Communities: Community
Enhancement Program, for a grant to develop Saddle Ridge Park.  Todd Feezer, Assistant Director of
Parks and Recreation, provided information on the proposed park; explained proposed grant request
in the amount of $285,820.00 and the City’s proposed grant match mechanism, and noted funding has
also b een s ought and a pproved i n t he a mount o f $ 105,125.00 t hrough t he r ecreation m ill l evy,
administered through the school district, to assist with the project, which will serve as an urban quality
amenity locate d within Saddle Ridge subdivision in east Chey enne.  H e stated the  developer has
donated 14 acres for the project of which seven acres are proposed for park development at this time
as part of the grant application.  Mr. Feezer advised the park will serve residents and workers in the
workforce housing and business area in the vicinity of the Cheyenne LEADS business park, and will
also be near the Greenway path system and new Saddle Ridge school.  He indicated the developer is
also do nating a ssistance wi th s ite g rading a nd uti lity stu bs, a nd de scribed u ses o f g rant f unds, if
approved.  Ap preciation was expressed to staff for their creativity in seeking this type of Business
Council grant for park development.  There being no further comments or input and purpose  of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing.
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments and input on the submission of an
application to the Wyoming Business Council, under the Business Ready Communities: Business
Readiness Project, for a grant to develop a manufacturing building in the Cheyenne Business Parkway.
Scott Sutherland, Vice President, Cheyenne LEADS, advised LEADS is seeking the City’s assistance
in applying for grant funding that, if received, will provide a manufacturing speculation building that
LEADS can promote to manufacturing companies interested in a Cheyenne location.  He explained a
community need for this type of shell building exists; that it will not be a building for warehouse
purposes, and that it will provide for potential economic development.  He stated the grant, if approved,
requires a match that would be provided by LEADS through property valuation.  Upon inquiry, Mr.
Sutherland indicated the facility would be approximately 40,000 sq. ft. in size and provided information
on the estimated number of employment opportunities that may be available based upon past inquiries.
There being no further comments or input and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen
closed the public meeting. 
“AN ORDINANCE SUBMITTING TO THE QUALIFIED PROPERTY OWNERS OF REAL
PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT
AUTHORITY DISTRICT OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, EXCLUDING REAL PROPERTY USED
EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, AT A SPECIAL ELECTION ON TUESDAY,
OCTOBER 20, 2009, THE FOLLOWING QUESTION: ‘SHALL A SPECIAL ASSESSMENT OF
TEN (10) MILLS BE LEVIED UPON THE ASSESSED VALUE OF REAL PROPERTY WITHIN
THE B OUNDARIES OF THE  CHEYENNE D OWNTOWN DEVEL OPMENT AUTHO RITY
DISTRICT, EXCL UDING REAL  PROP ERTY USED EXCL USIVELY FO R RES IDENTIAL
PURPOSES, FOR A PERIOD OF FOUR (4) YEARS PURSUANT TO THE PROVISIONS OF WYO.
STAT. § 15-9-217( b) AND (c) FOR THE  PURP OSES OF (1)  ADMINISTRATIVE COSTS; (2)
LANDSCAPING OR MAINTENANCE OF PUBLIC AREAS; (3) PLANNING OR MANAGEMENT
OF DEVELOPMENT OR IMPROVEMENT ACTIVITIES; (4) PROMOTION OF PUBLIC EVENTS;
AND (5) ACTIVITIES IN SUPPORT OF BUSINESS RECRUITMENT AND DEVELOPMENT?’”
Christian Cherek, DDA Director, advised she was available to answer any questions.  Reporting for
Finance Com mittee, M r. Valdez mov ed to  a pprove on  se cond r eading, seconded b y Mr . Co llins.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  
“AN O RDINANCE CREATI NG SECTI ON 10.24.170, DRI VING WHI LE USI NG
CELLULAR TELEPHONE PROHIBITED; EXCEPTIONS, OF CHAPTER 10.24, RULES OF THE
ROAD GENERALLY, OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”  John Fafoutakis commented in support of violations constituting a
secondary offense instead of a primary one if the ordinance were approved, and cited concerns with
ordinance enforcement, othe r distractive driving scenarios, and governing body infringement of
individual rights and liberties.  Major Dorr spoke in favor of laws that assist with keeping people safe.
Pacomio Sandoval spoke in favor of drivers utilizing hands free devices when using cell phones while
driving, and commente d on passag e of la ws due to indivi duals’ irresponsibility and enf orcement
concerns.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading,
seconded by Mr. Collins. Discussion followed on use of hands free devices, safety concerns with use
of cell phones and texting while driving, enforcement issues and concerns, proposed costs related to
community signage if signs were posted to provide notification to the public of ordinance restrictions,
and public education and awareness. Upon inquiry,  Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised if the
ordinance is approved and signage was to be installed  at entryways into the Cit y, approximately 20
signs would be posted at an estimated cost of $120/sign.  It was advised public awareness and education
would be provided through media releases, public service announcements and City website postings.
Upon inquiry regarding enforcement, Captain Jeff Schulz, Police Dept., advised the ordinance would
be enf orced on a priority  basis as with other or dinances, and provided inf ormation on offe nses
involving careless driving, explaining that citations written for careless driving involves the manner
of operation of a vehicle vs. what a driver may be doing within the vehicle.  Regarding local statistics,
Captain Schulz provided information pursuant to 2008 accident reports involving drivers who admitted
to using cell phones.  Discussion continued on safety issues; driving practices; hand held vs. hands free
cell phone usage by drivers; governing body authority to regulate driving, and protection of the public
and other drivers.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –  all members of the governing body present with
the exception of Mr. Valdez voting “no”. 
 
“AN O RDINANCE REPEAL ING SECTI ON 9.0 8.030, GAMB LING-PROHIBITED-
EXCEPTIONS, AND SECTI ON 9.08.040, VI OLATIONS BY OWNER OR OCC UPANT OF
PREMISES WHERE GAMBLING CONDUCTED, OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE,
CHAPTER 9.08, OFFENSES AGAINST PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY, OF THE CHEYENNE
CITY CODE.”  Mr. B rown moved to a pprove on second reading, seconded by Mr. Collins. Upon
inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, provided information on a recent amendment to state statute which
would a llow c ertain t ypes o f g ambling/gaming a ctivities b y pa trons o f b usinesses u nder c ertain
circumstances, and in which the business does not receive any direct profit from the activity and where
no person is participating directly or indirectly in professional gambling.  Captain Jeff Schulz, Police
Dept., provided information on past acceptable practices and changes due to an Attorney General’s
opinion that resulted in state law changes, and discussed enforcement concerns with the current city
ordinance as written.  Discussion followed and included: Repeal of the ordinance not reflecting that the
City is legalizing gambling activities; state law definition of gambling; what constitutes a bona fide
social relationship; whether advertising casino or poker events by bars was in violation of state law;
interpretation and enf orcement of state law  if the ordi nance is approved, and diffe rent ty pes of
gambling/gaming activities and scenarios.  U pon inquiry, Mr. White expressed his understanding,
pursuant to law enforcement practices, that, for instance, a bar can not advertise a gambling event, such
as a poker night, but could allow patrons to participate in the activity if it meets a bona fide social
relationship as referenced in state statute, and advised some activities could require further review due
to “gray areas” of interpretation, especially those involving a business and the issue of receiving direct
vs. indirect profits.  Upon inquiry, Captain Schulz clarified conducting “pools” (example: involving
professional sporting events) at bars is a gray area as to whe ther it constitutes a social r elationship.
Discussion continued on different types of gambling activities as affiliated with a social relationship,
such as raffles, pools, and casino nights raising money for charities; conducting gambling as a business;
advertising for charitable events, and legal vs. illegal practices involving businesses or entities where
the business or entity  holding or  sponsoring the g aming activity does or doe s not take or  receive a
portion of the gaming proceeds.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” –  all members of the governing body
present with the exception of Mr. Spiker voting “no”.

MUNICIPAL PROPERTY AUCTION AT THE TIME CONVENIENT FOR THE CITY.”  Motion (per

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
September 14, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Absent:
Ms. Ash and Ms. Case.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Rededication ceremony of the memorial honoring Vietnam Veterans from Laramie County who
never returned from Southeast Asia and Wyoming MIA’s not returned.  Mayor Kaysen recessed the
meeting in Council Chambers for the ceremony, which was held at Constitution S quare, l ocated
immediately eas t o f t he M unicipal Building.  F ollowing cl osure o f t he ce remony, M ayor K aysen
reconvened the meeting in Council Chambers at 6:17 p.m.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  M r. Collins moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON AUGUST 24,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
 [CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON September
8, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Collins  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.
Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez each declared a conflict of interest (business interests) due to vouchers
payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services ($85.00) and radio station KYOY ($140.00) respectively, and
vacated Council Chambers.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present
in Council Chambers. Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Brown and Mr. Valdez returned to
Council Chambers. 
“AN ORDINANCE SUBMITTING TO THE QUALIFIED PROPERTY OWNERS OF REAL
PROPERTY WITHIN THE BOUNDARIES OF THE CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN DEVELOPMENT
AUTHORITY DISTRICT OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING, EXCLUDING REAL PROPERTY USED
EXCLUSIVELY FOR RESIDENTIAL PURPOSES, AT A SPECIAL ELECTION ON TUESDAY,
OCTOBER 20, 2009, THE FOLLOWING QUESTION: ‘SHALL A SPECIAL ASSESSMENT OF
TEN (10) MILLS BE LEVIED UPON THE ASSESSED VALUE OF REAL PROPERTY WITHIN
THE B OUNDARIES O F THE C HEYENNE D OWNTOWN D EVELOPMENT A UTHORITY
DISTRICT, E XCLUDING R EAL P ROPERTY U SED E XCLUSIVELY FO R R ESIDENTIAL
PURPOSES, FOR A PERIOD OF FOUR (4) YEARS PURSUANT TO THE PROVISIONS OF WYO.
STAT. § 15-9- 217(b) AND (c) F OR THE PURPOSES OF (1) ADMINISTRATIVE COSTS; (2)
LANDSCAPING OR MAINTENANCE OF PUBLIC AREAS; (3) PLANNING OR MANAGEMENT
OF DEVELOPMENT OR IMPROVEMENT ACTIVITIES; (4) PROMOTION OF PUBLIC EVENTS;
AND (5) ACTIVITIES IN SUPPORT OF BUSINESS RECRUITMENT AND DEVELOPMENT?’”
Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded
by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#3869)
“AN OR DINANCE CR EATING SE CTION 10.24. 170, DR IVING W HILE USI NG
CELLULAR TELEPHONE PROHIBITED; EXCEPTIONS, OF CHAPTER 10.24, RULES OF THE
ROAD GENERALLY, OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY
OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING.”  J ohn Fafoutakis, Eric Miller and John Palmer spoke generally in
opposition to the proposed ordinance, and expressed concerns on use of cell phones for text messaging
while dr iving, l ocal go vernmental co ntrol, o rdinance enforcement, di stractive dr iving scen arios,
violations constituting a primary vs. secondary offense, and current traffic laws in place that might be
utilized instead of the proposed ordinance.  U pon inquiry, Capt. Jeff Schulz, Interim Police Chief,
provided in formation o n c areless d riving v iolations r esulting in  c itations.  R eporting fo r P ublic
Services C ommittee, Mr. B rown moved t o a pprove o n t hird a nd final re ading, s econded b y M r.
Collins. Mr. Pierson moved to amend by indicating that violations of the ordinance be enforced as a
secondary o ffense, s econded b y Mr. V aldez.  M r. F afoutakis, M r. P almer, M r. M iller a nd D avid
Hallam spoke in support o f t he amendment.  R everend Michaela Pena spoke in opposition to t he
amendment.  Discussion included the purpose of violations constituting a primary offense, enforcement
of secondary vs. primary offenses, concern with passing laws due to irresponsible drivers, o ther
distractions a ffecting s afe d riving p ractices, n otification to th e p ublic a nd c ommunity visitors of
ordinance restrictions, and balancing of rights and freedoms for safety purposes.  M otion to amend
failed.  V oting “yes” – Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez. Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr.
Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.  Comments  followed regarding enforcement as a
primary offense and effect on local residents, statistics supporting cell phone usage as the cause of
many accidents, protection of drivers and passengers, community signage, and ordinance provision
allowing u se o f cell phones w ith hands free d evices w hile d riving.  U pon inquiry, Captain S chulz
advised the ordinance, if approved, will be enforced like every other traffic ordinance on the books,
and commented on probable cause scenarios as noted by officers involving violations.  Mayor Kaysen
clarified signs will be posted around t he City and entry ways into Cheyenne alerting residents and
those visiting of the cell phone ordinance, and stated exact sites have not yet been determined.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr. Pierson
voting “no”. (#3870)
 
“AN OR DINANCE REPE ALING SE CTION 9.08. 030, GA MBLING-PROHIBITED-
EXCEPTIONS, A ND SE CTION 9.08. 040, VIOLA TIONS BY OW NER OR  OC CUPANT OF
PREMISES WHERE GAMBLING CONDUCTED, OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE,
CHAPTER 9.08, OFFENSES AGAINST PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY, OF THE CHEYENNE
CITY CODE.”  Mr. Brown reported there was no recommendation on the item from Public Services
Committee.  Mr.  Br own moved t o app rove o n t hird an d final r eading, seconded b y Mr.  Co llins.
Discussion f ollowed on  th e m ost r ecent c hange i n s tate l aw c oncerning g ambling a nd s tatute
interpretation; enforcement concerns with current City code; wording of state law involving “bona fide
social relationship”; concerns with gambling being conducted in businesses within City limits; City
ordinances being more restrictive t han state statutes; enforcement o f t he current o rdinance, which
makes football pools and charity fund-raising events, such as casino nights, illegal; finding a balance
between al lowing certain types o f gam bling v s. t otal rest riction; i nterpretation o f s tate s tatute;
allowable advertising of gambling events and activities per st ate law; revising the current ordinance
to allow charities to conduct fund- raisers, and protecting residents.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Dan
White clarified there are no dollar limits on gambling betting amounts specified in state statute.  He
advised o versight wo uld b e pro vided b y l aw enforcement; pro vided h is un derstanding o f wh at
constitutes a “bona fide social relationship” relative to a business sponsored gambling activity and, in
response to a question referencing the City’s enforcement authority, stated the gambling statutes are
included in the “Crimes and Offenses” section of state statute and that penalties could be enforced by
the police department.  He further reviewed gambling state statutes and advertising restrictions.  Mr.
Spiker moved to postpone for 60 days, seconded by Mr. Collins.  John Fafoutakis spoke in support of
postponement; Donn Edmunds commented on undesirable effects of commercial-type gambling and
spoke in support of revising the current ordinance, and Eric Miller spoke in opposition to gambling
generally and in support of postponement. Upon request for clarification of the exact date involved
with the motion to postpone, Mr. Spiker modified his motion, with concurrence by Mr. Collins as
second to the motion, to indicate postponement would be to Nov. 23, 2009.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk
Carol Intlekofer clarified if an allowable gambling activity takes place within the dispensing room of
an establishment that holds a City liquor license, the hours of operation for the dispensing room must
be complied with pursuant to City Code.  Further discussion followed on enforcement of the current
ordinance vs. state statutes, professional gambling not allowed under state law, and review o f t he
current ordinance for possible revision during the postponement period.  Upon inquiry, Captain Jeff
Schulz, Interim Police Chief, advised police officers will continue to enforce the ordinance as it has
been until the proposed ordinance is either approved or disapproved.  Motion to postpone to Nov. 23,
2009 carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Mr.
Brown voting “no”. 
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:05 pm.

and Cheyenne Area Centralized Pharmacy); Meals on Wheels of Cheyenne, Inc.; NEEDS, Inc.; Peak
Wellness Ce nter, In c.; Re tired and  Senior Vo lunteer Pr ogram; Safe Ha rbor, A  Children’s J ustice
Center; Safehouse/Sexual Assault Services, Inc.; Southeast Wyoming Foster Grandparent Program
(Helping Community and Helping Youth); Special Friends of Cheyenne; Stride Learning Center, and
Wyoming Senior Citizens, Inc. (2007-2010 1% Sales Tax).  Mr. Valdez moved to approve, seconded
by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend by substitutes, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Upon inquiry,
City A ttorney D an W hite e xplained h e h ad r ecommended a mending th e G overnmental I mmunity
clause (Section #9) for each of the agreements to follow state statutes, and that wording in the proposed
substitutes wo uld indicate t he C ity of Cheyenne res erves an d ret ains all defenses an d immunities
available under the Wyoming Governmental Claims Act, Wyo. Statutes §§ 1-39-101 through 1-39-121.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Upon inquiry,
Mayor Kaysen clarified each agreement contains specific amounts for services and each are for a 2
year period.  Upon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, advised funding for human services agreements
are pursuant to 1% sales tax money allocated through the 1% resolution, and noted that the previous
agenda item is funded through the general fund.  She explained the funding amount for t hose
agreements involving general fund money had not been increased from the prior year amount.  Vicki
Nemecek, Asst. Public Works Director, clarified the dollar amounts for each individual contract for
this agenda item may have changed based on the Human Services Advisory Council recommendation,
but the overall total dollar amount of $440,000.00 per year for the agreements had remained the same.
Upon inquiry regarding a clerical error relating to a  funding amount in one of the agreements, City
Clerk Carol Intlekofer clarified the error, that has since been corrected, was contained in a spreadsheet
that ha d be en p repared t o l ist a ll of t he e ntity a gencies a nd f unding i nformation, a nd t hat t he
information within that respective agreement was correct.  Main motion as amended carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present. (#5436 - #5458)
Cooperative A greement b etween th e C ity of  C heyenne, W yoming D epartment of
Transportation, and Laramie County for Speer/High Plains Road Interchange Enhancements (2007-
2010 1% Sales Tax, State Land & Investment Board, American Recovery & Reinvestment Act).  Mr.
Valdez moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  M r. Valdez moved to amend to remove the
funding sources from the entitlement of the agenda item, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Upon inquiry, Tom
Mason, Metropolitan Planning Office Director, confirmed the City is not financially responsible for
the enhancements, noting t he agreement will provide for enhancements for which t he County will
provide the funding.  He explained that when WYDOT places enhancements in a project they require
local government to work with the County and State to provide assistance and maintenance, and that
the County has agreed to pay the funding match and the City has agreed to provide the maintenance.
Upon further inquiry, he advised the interchange is located about 3 miles (I-25 south) from the City
limits. Discussion included the City providing maintenance, following project completion, relative to
the site’s location in the County.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Mason stated maintenance would only be on the
enhancement d etails i nstalled f or s treet l ighting, c oncrete r epair, i f n eeded, a nd s idewalk s now
removal.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  Main
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#5459)

[CA] Consideration of Bid #M-3-10 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Corporate

Consideration of Bid #M-6-10 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and T heatrical

Mr. Valdez reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance Committee.  Mr. Valdez
moved t o approve in an amount not to exceed $483,218.00, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Valdez
moved to amend that the total bid amount not exceed $337,397.56, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion
to amend carried.  Vo ting “ yes” – all members o f t he go verning b ody prese nt.  M ain motion as
amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.  (#5462)
Consideration of Bid #M-1-10 for a contract between the City of Cheyenne and Reiman Corp.,
for the East Cheyenne Greenway, Phase IIIb, Part 1 Project (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).
Rick Potter, Cheyenne Branch Manager, S&S Builders, referenced  a letter dated September 10, 2009
(on file with City Clerk’s office) that had been submitted to members of the governing body by S&S
Builders requesting reconsideration of its proposed awarding of the project to Reiman Corp.  M r.
Potter discussed wording of the City’s bid documents relative to an arithmetic error discovered in the
bid submitted by Reiman Corp. during the tabulation of bids; bid evaluation process, and potential
effect on future bids submitted pursuant to bid document wording that states all proposals must be
totaled and in the case of errors or discrepancies, the unit or lump sum prices will govern.  Due to the
mathematical error reflecting an irregularity in the bid proposal submitted by Reiman Corp., Mr. Potter
stated that S&S is the “true low bidder.”  Randy Reed, attorney with Dray, Thomson and Dyekman,
representing S&S Builders, reviewed the arithmetic (addition) error involving the cost of traffic control
(ie: $44,000 instead of $400) in the proposal amount contained in the Reiman bid, indicating the sum
of the entire unit items was not the same as the bottom line amount; advised if enforcement of unit
prices i s f ollowed t he S&S bi d w ould be  t he l owest r eceived; d iscussed bi d d ocument w ording
involving u nit p rices i f d iscrepancies a re d iscovered, a nd s tate l aw r equirements; c ommented on
maintaining the integrity of the competitive bidding process, and spoke in favor of rejecting the bid
submitted by Reiman and in support of awarding the bid to S&S.  Mr. Valdez reported the agenda item
had been introduced and discussed under other items for the agenda at the Finance Committee meeting
and provisions of Title 2 of City code had been met.  Mr. Valdez moved to accept the bid of Reiman
Corp. in an amount not to e xceed $457,082.10, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion followed on
maintaining the integrity of the City’s bidding process and adhering to policy, and the arithmetic error
discovered by staff in the Reiman bid.  Upon inquiry, Rudy Apodaca, Purchasing Director, reviewed
the bid tabulation process, at which time the error was discovered, and provided information on the
line item amount error vs. the total amount listed on the cover page for the Reiman proposal.  Upon
inquiry, D an White, City Attorney, a dvised t o a void a ny perceived c onflict o f interest d ue t o his
previous p rivate business relationship with Re iman, t he law firm o f Hirst &  A pplegate L LP was
retained to review the bids involved.  Mandy Good, attorney with Hirst & Applegate, clarified the law
firm was retained by the City as an independent party to review the matter, and stated Hirst &
Applegate d oes no t r epresent n or have  a ny c onflict o f i nterest i nvolving Re iman Co rp. o r S &S
Builders.  Ms. Good responded to query concerning the transposition of the dollar amount submitted
by Reiman which involved the traffic control line item, and advised she did not believe the City could
be h eld l iable d ue to th e d iscrepancy r elative to a warding th e b id to R eiman in the amount of
$457,082.10, which is the amount listed on the front page of Reiman’s bid package.  She cited case
law in support of her statements relative to approval of bids following bid opening and acceptance by
the City, and indicated the company selected by the City would have a time frame in which to accept
the bid in the amount approved or the company could forfeit its bid bond.  She further advised that
after r eview o f c ase l aw, i t w as he r f irm’s l egal opinion t hat R eiman’s bi d p roposal m eets t he
requirements for consideration of awarding them the bid, and discussed material errors vs. m inor
defects involving bid proposals.  U pon inquiry, Mr. Apodaca p rovided further information on the
Reiman bid; dollar error involving the amount of $44,000 listed on the unit pricing page for traffic
control vs. the apparent amount of $400.00 that was included in the front page total, and stated the
front page dollar amount listed is what is considered the “Invitation to Bid” and that is the number read
at bid openings (copies of the Invitation to Bid page (front page) of Reiman’s bid package submitted
and o n file with t he City Clerk’s o ffice).  F urther discussion followed o n bid do cument language
referencing “unit or lump sum prices,” “total bid” and “invitation to bid”; entry error of the number
listed in Reiman’s bid sheet; errors or discrepancies in bids; preserving the integrity of the City’s bid
process; state statute provisions; case law; best interests of the City and its constituents, and unit bid
amounts vs. total dollar amount contained in Reiman’s proposal. In response to inquiry, Mr. Apodaca
explained the following choices available to the governing body:  (a) accept Reiman’s proposal as low
bidder, (b) accept S&S Builders’ proposal as low bidder, or (c) reject all bids and re-bid the project,
and stated bidders are not allowed to change elements of their bids following the bid opening.  During
discussion Ms. Good responded to inquiries and provided information on bid documents allowing the
City to reject all bids; bid specification language; case law t hat supports t he fiduciary duty o f t he
governing b ody t o p rotect t he i nterests o f t he p ublic a nd t axpayers, a nd a mbiguity o f w ording
contained in Section 6. e. of the bid document.  Further discussion continued on the bid process and
wording of bid documents.  Mr. Reed referenced Section 6. e. (bid proposal requirements) of the bid
document, stating that all dollar amounts should be added and should correctly total the amount of the
bid proposal, and that the sum of the numbers is what must be considered.  Discussion continued on
the total of unit prices vs. total amount listed on the front page of the bid and bid document wording;
being more “tighter” on bid procedures than state law requires; delay of the Greenway project if all
bids are rejected, and fiduciary responsibility of the governing body.  Upon inquiry, Rich Bolkovatz,
Reiman Corp., acknowledged that Reiman did make an addition error and stated Reiman is not asking
the City to award them the bid;  that the City should look at the best interests of the City.  Upon query,
Ms. Good advised the integrity of the bidding process is upheld if the amount listed on the front page
of Reiman’s bid proposal is the proposal bid amount accepted by the City, explaining that Reiman is
not being allowed to change their bid information as submitted.  Discussion continued.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Apodaca explained when the error was discovered, staff brought the issue to the attention of the
City’s legal staff and there was no communication with Reiman until after that time, which was after
the bid opening.  Mr. Potter reviewed the different types of bids submitted for City projects, stating
some i nvolve a  l ump s um bid a nd o thers i nvolve a  t otal bid a mount.  C omments c ontinued a nd
included verification through Mayor Kaysen that  the motion on the floor was to accept the bid in an
amount not t o exceed $457,082.10.  S ara Vasquez, Purchasing Dept., discussed t he t otal amount
reflected on the itemized bid sheets vs. the front page of the bid proposal relative to future purchasing
tabulation pro cedures.  M otion t o accept t he bid from Reiman Corp. in an amount not t o exceed
$457,082.10 failed.  V oting “yes” – Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  V oting “no” – Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Brown moved to approve the bid
submitted b y S &S Builders in an  amount n ot to exceed $4 77,619.66, seco nded b y Ms. Br oyles.
Pursuant to comments involving acceptance of the bid in a “not to exceed” amount relative to unit
prices, Mr. Potter provided information on unit prices; change order process per directive by the City
to add services under a contract, and consideration of engineer’s estimates of project materials as being
accurate w hen preparing bid p roposals.  M r. Apodaca clarified a  c ontract modification would be
submitted for approval if any changes are proposed to be added to bid services after the contract had
been awarded.  M r. Pierson moved t o adjourn into executive session to discuss an item involving
possible litigation, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of information on the intended
purpose o f t he e xecutive session with Mr. P ierson clarifying his motion is specific t o t he c urrent
agenda item.  Motion to move into executive session failed (2/3rd’s affirmative vote required).  Voting
“yes” – Mr. Collins, Mr. P ierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. S piker and Mayor Kaysen.  V oting “no” – Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, and Mr. Valdez.   Discussion followed on the pending motion, rejecting all bids,
and potential legal ramifications.  Motion to accept the bid submitted by S&S Builders failed (due to
a tie vote).  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne. Voting “no” – Mr.
Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Pierson moved to reject all bids, seconded
by Mr. Spiker. It was clarified there were other bidders who had submitted proposals for the project.
Mr. Pierson modified h is motion t o reject all b ids t o include “and t o re- issue t he b id as  s oon as
possible”, with concurrence by Mr. Spiker as second to the motion.  Upo n request, Mayor Kaysen
recited the names of all bidders who had bid on the project.  Motion to reject all bids and to re-issue
the bid as soon as possible carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with
the exception of Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.
APPOINTMENTS:
Appointment of Holly A. Hansen, and Ronnald J. Jeffrey to the  Laramie County Community
Juvenile Se rvices J oint Powe rs B oard.  M r. C ollins moved t o a pprove, s econded b y M r. S piker.
Motion carried.  V oting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of
Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (Mayoral appointments).
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
Dave Hallam commented on the east/west crosswalk located near Cole Elementary School and
lack of a no rth/south crosswalk for use by students, and provided a map (on file with City Clerk’s
office) showing the current crosswalk  and school location.  He requested a north/south crosswalk be
added for safety purposes. Upon inquiry, Doug Vetter, City Engineer, provided information on prior
requests for an additional crosswalk in the area, noting the difficulty in implementing crosswalks in
every direction relative to traffic issues, and reviewed area signage.  M ayor Kaysen requested Mr.
Vetter communicate with the school district’s committee concerning the request.  During comments,
Tom Mason, MPO Director, advised the MPO and Parks and Recreation Dept. are currently reviewing
walking routes for all schools and a pedestrian plan.  Mr. Hallam also questioned rates assessed on
water/sewer bills he receives for tenant properties.  Mayor Kaysen advised he would review the matter
with Board of Public Utilities’ representatives and a response would be provided to Mr. Hallam.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 10:14 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

 September 26, 2009

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-09-28 | CLIP_ID: 115 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 115

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
September 28, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.  Absent: Ms. Case, Mr.
Pierson and Mr. Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Mayor K aysen welcomed and i ntroduced t he following d elegates r epresenting Cheyenne’s
Sister City Voghera, Italy in attendance at the meeting: Dr. Professor Daniele Salerno, Dott. Carolo
Gianotti, Carlo Riccardi and Anna Riccardi.  He advised Voghera has been a Sister City since 2002.
Dr. Salerno reviewed the City of Voghera’s history, its location within Italy, its culture, terrain, notable
cuisine, and agricultural, industrial and transportation crossroad similarities shared between Voghera
and Cheyenne.  As a western similarity, he noted that Mr. Riccardi is the owner of the Cowboy Guest
Ranch.  I n recognition of t he Sister City relationship, Dr. Salerno presented plaques depicting t he
insignia of Voghera to Mayor Kaysen and Council President Patrick Collins.  In return, on behalf of
the City of Cheyenne and its residents Mayor Kaysen presented the delegation with a rustic framed
rope depicting the word Cheyenne for placement at Voghera’s City building.
City of Cheyenne employees were recognized by Mayor Kaysen for receiving the following
awards at the 2009 Wyoming Planning Association Conference:  Cheyenne area Metropolitan Planning
Organization, Urb an P lanning Pr oject o f t he Ye ar f or t he C heyenne W ayfinding Pl an, pro ject
coordinator Sreyoshi Chakraborty; Jeanie Anderson, New Planning Professional of the Year in the
State of Wyoming, and Western Planner Magazine’s Sheldon D. Gerber Merit Award for Excellence
recognizing the City of Cheyenne and Project Coordinator Matt Ashby for excellence in environmental
planning in the Project of the Year Category for the Belvoir Ranch Master Plan.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Mr. Collins moved to approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr.
Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON SEPTEMBER
14, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES F ROM VOUC HERS MEE TING OF  T HE GOVE RNING BODY  ON
SEPTEMBER 21, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Spiker moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr.
Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to vouchers payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC
Services ($114.89 and $1,894.96) and vacated Council Chambers.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers. 
“AN O RDINANCE AMENDING V ARIOUS SEC TIONS OF  TIT LE 5, BU SINESS
LICENSES A ND R EGULATIONS, CHAPTER 5.68,  BO DY A RT E STABLISHMENTS A ND
OPERATIONS, OF THE CO DE OF  T HE CIT Y OF  CH EYENNE.”  R eporting f or F inance
Committee, Ms. Ash moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry,
Gary Hickman, Cheyenne-Laramie County Health Dept., Division of Environmental Health, advised
the or dinance a mends h ealth r eporting r equirements for b ody a rt e stablishments a nd p rovides for
licensing of body artists.  He explained National Environmental Health guidelines were reviewed and
business o perators and art ists s upport t he pro posed amendments an d requirements, an d indicated
Environmental Health will help guide the establishments in providing a bloodborne pathogen written
exposure control plan, which is also a requirement through OSHA.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Hickman
explained ho w Environmental Health will permit current licensees and body artists at renewal and
City Clerk Carol Intlekofer clarified current operators will have until Jan. 1, 2010 to meet requirements
of the ordinance through the City Clerk’s office; new applicants for a license or permit would have to
meet the requirements of the ordinance upon its approval and publication.  Upon inquiry regarding
whether the term “pre-sterilized” contained in the definition of “body piercing” had the same meaning
as “st erilized”, M r. H ickman c larified i ndividuals c an br ing i n t heir o wn s tuds w hich ha ve t o be
sterilized by the operator, or they can purchase pre-sterilized studs from the establishment.  Dr. Rinne
moved t o d elete “p re-“ f rom t he t erm “p re-sterilized” i n t he t hree a reas referenced w ithin t he
definition, seconded by Mr. Collins.  In response to query concerning whether the ordinance changes
conflict with OSHA requirements, Mr. Hickman advised he did not believe there were any conflicts
as OSHA requirements involve bloodborne pathogens, but explained he would need to research this
to know for sure.  Following comments, motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing bo dy p resent.  M ain motion a s a mended c arried.  V oting “yes” –  a ll m embers o f t he
governing body present. 
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING TITLE 5, BUSINESS LICENSES AND REGULATIONS,

“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING TI TLE 17,  Z ONING, CH APTER 17.08 , Z ONING
DISTRICTS E STABLISHED A ND SE CTION 17.08. 040 CH EYENNE CIT Y M AP OF  T HE
CHEYENNE CITY CODE, BY CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM LR-2 LOW
DENSITY R ESIDENTIAL-DEVELOPING D ISTRICT TO  M R-1 M EDIUM D ENSITY
RESIDENTIAL-ESTABLISHED DISTRICT FOR LOTS 1- 8, BLOCK ONE; LOTS 1- 8, BLOCK
TWO; L OTS 1- 4, BLOCK T HREE; GOODW ATER A DDITION, CH EYENNE, W YOMING
(LOCATED EAST OF AND ADJACENT TO VAN BUREN AVENUE, SOUTH OF DELL RANGE
BOULEVARD).” Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on second

smaller lot size enabling more units on the property.  She advised MR-1 does allow for multi-family
units but would require Board of Adjustment approval if apartment units were proposed.  Comments
followed on concerns with zoning from LR-2 to MR-1 and potential of apartment buildings being built
rather t han t own ho mes o r d uplexes, r esulting i n i ncreased d ensity.  J ohn S teil, S teil S urveying
Services, agent for the applicant, clarified the applicant is proposing to build town homes, and advised
the change in zoning classification will make the project more feasible, and noted MR-1 zoning is in
the nearby area.  He also noted there will be no problem with height requirements. He explained a MR-
1 zone will  a llow smaller town homes to be built t han what the LR-2 zone requires, allowing the
building of more town homes. Upon inquiry, Ms. Pafford provided information on density and parking
requirements relative to when a replatting action may be involved, minimum setback requirements and
distances between buildings.  Comments followed on property layout for the town homes, setbacks,
garages facing the street-side of the property, and PlanCheyenne relative to product materials utilized.
Mr. St eil advised the buildings will be staggered, not o ne long ro w o f buildings.  M otion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception of Ms. Broyles voting
“no”. 
“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING T ITLE 17,  Z ONING, CH APTER 17.08,  Z ONING

regarding architectural and landscaping requirements for a PUD vs. CB zone, and proposed property

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 12, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Ash, and pursuant to support received by Dr. Rinne
and Mr. Pierson, agenda item #19(a) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins moved to
approve the remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body.

28, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.

5, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Collins moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr.
Brown declared a c onflict o f interest (business interest) due t o a v oucher payable t o Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services ($30,437.50) and vacated Council Chambers.  M otion carried. Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote,
Mr. Brown returned to Council Chambers.

Committee, Mr. Spiker moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion

“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING TITLE 5, BUSINESS LICENSES AND REGULATIONS,
CHAPTER 5.04, BU SINESS LICENSE S GE NERALLY, S ECTION 5.04. 030, T YPES OF

BOULEVARD).” Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on third

“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING TI TLE 17,  Z ONING, CH APTER 17.08,  Z ONING
DISTRICTS E STABLISHED A ND SE CTION 17.08. 040 CH EYENNE CIT Y M AP OF  T HE
CHEYENNE CITY C ODE, BY  CH ANGING THE  Z ONING CL ASSIFICATION F ROM PUD
PLANNED UNIT DEVELOPMENT DISTRICT TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS DISTRICT FOR
ALL OF BLOCK ONE, CATHEDRAL COMMONS, CHEYENNE, WYOMING (LOCATED AT
THE NORTHE AST CO RNER O F T HE I NTERSECTION OF  YELL OWSTONE ROA D A ND
GARDENIA DRIVE).”  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr.
Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#3875)
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 12.04.010 OBSTRUCTION ON STREETS, AND
SECTION 12.04.080, CONSTRUCTION AND REPAIR-PERMITS REQUIRED-FEES-EXCEPTION,
OF CHAPTER 12.04, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND DRIVEWAYS GENERALLY, OF TITLE 12,
STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC PLACES, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING.”  M r. Spiker moved t o approve on second reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Upon
inquiry regarding the proposed ordinance regulating the placement of storage containers and other
types of obstructions in the public right-of-way, Nathan Beauheim, City Engineer’s office, advised
issuing permits for placement of containers or obstructions in front of a neighboring property was not
specifically addressed in the ordinance, but that a proposed policy in conjunction with the ordinance
is being formulated, and stated there was no grace period provided for in the ordinance. Upon inquiry,
City Attorney Dan White clarified the violation for penalties affiliated with the ordinance would be`
pursuant to the general penalty provisions of city code and briefly reviewed the provisions.  Discussion
followed r egarding the allowance of  a  g race p eriod, p ermit e nforcement, p ermit f ee, a nd c osts
generally for rental of a storage container.  M r. Valdez moved to a mend the permit fee in Section
12.04.080, Section B, from $7.50/day to $5.00/day, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Beauheim requested
a typographical error be corrected in Section 12.04.080, Section B, on the same line as the fees, to
amend the word “of” to “a”.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Beauheim addressed staffing cost estimates to enforce
permit time frames, companies providing storage containers, types of containers that will be affected
by the ordinance, placement of landscaping materials in the right-of-way, certain containers creating
traffic ha zard conditions, a nd i ssues w ith c urrent e ngineering r ight-of-way p ermits no t ha ving
expiration dates and compliance with the Clean Water Act involving drainage of materials into the
storm sewer system.  Further discussion followed.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White reviewed the purpose of
the ordinance, stating it is to address storage of personal property on City rights-of-way including
landscaping materials and large storage containers, and stated debris washing into the storm sewers
from c ertain s tored materials v iolates w ater r egulations a nd th at th e or dinance w ill h elp w ith
environmental issues.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, clarified the difference in size
between c ommercial d umpsters a nd rol l-off c ontainers, a nd M r. B eauheim advised t hat t hey a re
working with Sanitation to enable that office to also issue permits.  Dr. Rinne raised a point of order
indicating comments being made were not pertinent to the motion on the floor with Mayor Kaysen
advising comments should remain germane to the motion to amend the permit fee. Motion to amend
to reduce the permit fee to $5.00/day, including a clerical correction to change the word “of” to “a”
per Mr. Beauheim’s request, carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body.  Ms. Ash
moved to amend to permit a 3 day grace period where no fees would be assessed (i.e. first 3 days of
permit te rm would involve no fee), seconded by Mr. Pierson. Following discussion and a point of
information on the motion being raised by Mr. Collins, Ms. Ash modified her motion for a 3 day grace
period to clarify that individuals would have 3 calendar days to place items in the right-of-way before
having to obtain a permit with payment of fees.  Mr. Pierson concurred with the modification as second
to the initial motion to amend.  Motion to amend, as modified, carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Mr.
Collins, Mr. P ierson, Dr . Rinne, Mr. S piker, Mr. V aldez and Mayor Kaysen.  V oting “no” – Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles and Ms. Case.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Beauheim advised a permit would probably not
be issued for a scenario involving blocking of a sidewalk, but if a temporary obstruction was needed
to be placed in a certain location for removal of debris which might block a sidewalk, then provisions
would be required t o provide for passage o f pedestrians.  M artin Matsen, Development Director,
provided information o n current landscaping regulations re lative t o private vs. public right-of-way
property, stating the nuisance ordinance may apply in some instances; advised a temporary use permit
may be needed for placement o f landscaping materials o n private property, a nd referenced zoning
setback requirements. Main motion as twice amended carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.   Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Dr. Rinne
and Mayor Kaysen.
