CHANGE ORDERS/CONTRACT MODIFICATIONS:
Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract No. 5350 between the City of Cheyenne and A.V.I.
P.C. for construction administration of the Carey Ave. and Pioneer Ave. Rehabilitation Project (2007-
2010 1% Sales Tax).  Bruce Perryman, AVI representative, briefly reviewed the history of the Carey
Ave./Pioneer Ave. Rehabilitation project which encompasses 13 blocks in the downtown area, and
explained the project has evolved to become more complex with lots of intricacies in the design due
to the challenges involved with the downtown area, which include sensitivity to businesses during the
rehabilitation t o b e ab le t o m ake a nd recei ve del iveries; s anitation pi ck-up se rvices; em ergency
response accessibility; driveway approaches and ADA sidewalk components; traffic features, etc.  He
discussed street reflective cracking and rutting conditions, and issues involved with an initial design
prepared by one engineering firm if transferred later to a different engineering firm.  He advised the
project will include storm sewer system upgrades, and described special materials that will be used to
enhance street durability.  Mr. Spiker reported there was no recommendation on the item from Finance
Committee.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Perryman
advised the City has all of the design plan information their firm has generated.  Comments followed
on the increase in contract price from its initial inception, best use of taxpayers’ funds, and lack of
opportunity for o ther firms t o submit bids relative t o construction management services under t he
modification. Upon inquiry, Doug Vetter, City Engineer, provided information on other c ontract
modifications that are anticipated; explained it is not unusual to have contract modifications due to the
scope o f a pro ject co nstantly ch anging, an d v erified t he o riginal co ntract was  co mpetitively b id.
During query on the project’s funding sources, Mr.Vetter provided additional information on funding
of projects of this type generally, and discussed how and why it had been determined that outside
construction management was needed for t he pro ject; efficiency an d co st s avings o f h aving A VI
provide construction management of a project it designed, and potential use of excess funds from the
depot plaza operations/restroom building project toward this project.  Mr. Spiker clarified his motion
to approve should have also included the dollar amount (not to exceed) of $366,845.00.  Mr. Collins
concurred with the clarification as second t o t he motion.  C ity Treasurer Barb Dorr reviewed t he
funding sources for the project as follows:  5  Penny 1% sales tax, State Loan and Investment Boardth
(SLIB) grant, traffic signals and miscellaneous concrete budget line items and Board of Public Utilities
(BOPU) funds.  During inquiry concerning transferring of funds from one project to another, Ms. Dorr
reviewed the process for transferring money and Mr. Beauheim clarified several line items involving
allocated sales tax funds are being combined and that th e use and purpose of the funding has not
changed.  Concerns were e xpressed r egarding t he i nitial c ontract bi d d ocuments no t i ncluding
provisions f or co nstruction m anagement; f uture b id do cuments co ntaining des ign an d po tential
construction management services in the scope of services for these types of projects, and the process
utilized t o t ransfer f unds w ithin t he bu dget.  M r. V etter p rovided information o n s treetscaping
elements, which are the same as the Downtown Development Authority’s (DDA) streetscape design,
and explained the streetscape components were initially separated out as an alternate to t he project.
Laura Page, City Engineer’s office, reviewed project funding sources and amounts, and verified the
streetscaping elements were an alternate in the initial construction bid.  Mr. Vetter stated future plans
are to change the bid process for projects of this type to include construction management oversight
as an option, stating that for some projects City staff may be able to p rovide these services.  U pon
inquiry regarding use of 1% sales tax traffic signal monies for the project, Mr. Beauheim advised other
areas will not be sacrificed as t he downtown traffic signals have been included in the line item as a
priority, and Mr. Vetter clarified staff could look at working further with the consultant to ascertain
any areas that may lower project costs through partnering of services.  Mr. Spiker moved to postpone
for 2 weeks (to Oct. 26, 2009) t o allow t ime for staff t o visit further with AVI on services t o be
provided, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry regarding the effect a postponement would have on
the project construction time frame, Mr. Vetter advised if the construction contract (Clerk’s note: Refer
to rel ating agen da i tem l ater i n m eeting co ncerning a co ntract wi th Re iman Co rp. f or pr oject
construction services) is awarded then two weeks should not affect the construction process, and stated,
with regard to sharing project management responsibilities, that potentially, based upon the expertise
needed and staffing levels available, City Engineering staff may be able to assist AVI in sharing some
responsibilities.  Upon further inquiry, Mr. Vetter confirmed it is not uncommon to change the scope
of a project the size of this one due to when estimated project costs are forecasted vs. construction cost
environments at the time of construction.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms.
Broyles and Mr. Spiker.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Main motion to approve in an amount not to exceed $366,845.00 carried.
 Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles and
Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#5350)

APPOINTMENTS:
Re-appointment of Judith Lane to the Cheyenne Housing Authority Board.  Mr. Brown moved

Announcement was  m ade o f t he appo intment o f K adi Dav is, J oshua He rr, T aylar Per ez,

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-10-26 | CLIP_ID: 119 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 119

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
October 26, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Case, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins
and Ms. Ash, agenda item #10 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Brown moved to approve
the remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON OCTOBER
12, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON OCTOBER
19, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Mr.
Valdez declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to KYOY Radio
Station  ($250.00) and vacated Council Chambers.  Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the
governing bo dy present in Co uncil Chambers.  F ollowing announcement o f t he vote, Mr.  Valdez
returned to Council Chambers. 
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 12.04.010 OBSTRUCTION ON STREETS, AND
SECTION 12.04.080, CONSTRUCTION AND REPAIR-PERMITS REQUIRED-FEES-EXCEPTION,
OF CHAPTER 12.04, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND DRIVEWAYS GENERALLY, OF TITLE 12,
STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC PLACES, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING.”  Edward Mel ko ques tioned the permit requi rement i nvolved wi th t he o rdinance;
expressed concerns with materials for disposal due to home repair projects (ex: roofing); contractor
costs passed on to customers; length of permit time and potential construction or inclement weather
delays affecting the time frame, and requested further clarification of ordinance requirements.  Frank
Cole spoke in opposition to the ordinance, stating wording of the ordinance is confusing as written.
He q uestioned w hether a  p ermit w ould be  r equired r elative to new s ubdivision c onstruction, t he
difference b etween s treets a nd s idewalks a nd p ublic ri ghts-of-way, a nd w hether d riveways w ere
considered a public street.  Upon inquiry, Nathan Beauheim, City Engineer’s o ffice, pro vided
information o n the proposed permit and when it wo uld be required; clarified if a s treet in a n ew
subdivision is not yet constructed, the right-of-way permit would not be required at the time original
infrastructure is be ing p laced, a nd explained t he d ifference be tween a  r ight-of-way e ncroachment
permit vs. the new permit involving obstructions.  City Attorney Dan White further clarified that under
existing provisions of City Code Section 12.04.010 it is unlawful, except as otherwise provided by
code or other city ordinance, to erect, maintain or in any way cause an obstruction of any character in
or upon any street, alley, or sidewalk, and stated the permitting process is a way to legally allow people
to store something on a street, alley or sidewalk for a period of time while a job is being completed.
Doug Vetter, City Engineer, provided distinction between a right-of-way encroachment permit stating
the permit is intended primarily for construction purposes and provides for inspection and compliance
of those facilities, w hether it  is a n e xisting fa cility o r b rand n ew, a nd t he p roposed n ew p ermit
affiliated with the ordinance which would be for storing items in the right-of-way and does not include
a street that is not built yet.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Beauheim provided a brief history on construction and
encroachment permits that had been combined into the engineering encroachment permit to alleviate
a number of different permits having to be issued.  Repo rting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez
moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend
by substitute #2, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  City Attorney Dan White clarified the substitute provides
for the following amendments: The word “property” is inserted into Section 12.04.010(H)(line 4) after
the word “personal”, subsection B(1) i n Sect ion 12.04.080 has been eliminated, and an o rdinance
effective date has been inserted in Section 3 following the word “publication” by adding“ ... but not
sooner than January 1, 2010".  Comments followed on the time frame that would be allowed under
a permit for storing containers in the right-of-way, and clarification of the size of allowable containers.
Motion to amend failed.  Voting “no” – all members of the governing body.  Discussion followed on
whether  temporary storage of landscaping materials in front of a residence could occur without having
to obtain a permit, and wording of current City Code prohibiting obstructions as reviewed earlier in
discussion by Mr. White.  Mr. Pierson moved to postpone for two weeks (to Nov. 9, 2009) to further
review ordinance wording to address storage of temporary landscaping materials, seconded by Mr.
Valdez.  Mr. Melko spoke in support of postponement and requested consideration be given to allow
storage of siding and roofing materials for construction jobs.  Comments followed on the intent of the
ordinance, which is to address storage of materials and containers in public rights-of-way; potential
cumbersome permit process affecting residents; dedicated streets that are not yet built; protecting the
usage of public rights-of-way for pedestrians, bicyclists, drivers, etc., and when a permit and fee may
not be required.  M otion to postpone carried.  V oting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
Mayor Kaysen advised the ordinance will be reviewed at the next Finance Committee meeting to be
held on Monday, Nov. 2, 2009 at 11:00 a.m. in Room 122 of the Municipal Building.

parking l ot a reas, realignment o f t he G reenway, t rees i n t he a rea a nd m oving f orward w ith t he

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-11-09 | CLIP_ID: 121 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 121

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
November 9, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil C hambers.  Pr esent w ere: M AYOR - Richard L . K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS:  Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Members of Boy Scout Pack 219 led those present in reciting the pledge
of allegiance.
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Dr. Rinne, and pursuant to support received by
Ms. Ash and Mr.  Collins, agenda item #12 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved to approve the remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON OCTOBER
26, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES F ROM VOUC HERS MEE TING OF  T HE GOV ERNING BO DY ON
NOVEMBER 2, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mayor Kaysen declared a c onflict o f interest due t o a v oucher payable t o him for t ravel
reimbursement ($46.37), relinquished duties of the chair to Council President Collins, and vacated
Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by
Mr. Brown.  In relationship to a voucher payable to Arbor Solutions for removal of trees infected
with mountain pine beetles, Forestry staff was commended for their community efforts.  M otion
carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Following
announcement of the vote, Council President Collins returned duties of the chair to Mayor Kaysen
upon his return to Council Chambers.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING SECTION 12.04.010 OBSTRUCTION ON STREETS,
AND SEC TION 1 2.04.080, CO NSTRUCTION AND REPAIR-PERMITS REQ UIRED-FEES-
EXCEPTION, OF  CH APTER 12.04,  ST REETS, SI DEWALKS A ND DR IVEWAYS
GENERALLY, OF TITLE 12, STREETS, SIDEWALKS AND PUBLIC PLACES, OF THE CODE
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WYOMING” (Postponed from Oct. 26, 2009).  F rank Cole
commented on the definition of right-of-way relative to construction of new streets and sidewalks,
cost to developers, and permit requirements and fees.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez
moved to a pprove on third and final reading, seconded by Ms.  B royles.  M r. Valdez moved to
amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry, Nathan Beauheim, City Engineer’s
office, reviewed changes contained in the substitute ordinance, stating the substitute creates a limited
exception for residential property owners to store materials, such as those utilized for landscaping
projects, in the right-of-way for 72 hours prior to a permit being required, and provides a daily,
weekly and monthly permit fee schedule.  He explained the difference between the proposed storage
right-of-way permit and an encroachment permit, and affiliated fees.  Upon further inquiry regarding
established vs. new street c onstruction a nd materials stored in t he r ight-of-way, Mr. Beauheim
provided information on new subdivision construction, encroachments and preserving the City’s
rights-of-way, and dedicated vs. established rights-of-way.  Upon inquiry, City Attorney Dan White
expressed concern with using the word “established” based on plat wording indicating rights-of-way
areas are dedicated for use by the public when a plat is filed, and explained the proposed ordinance
would not apply to vehicles but would affect personal property being stored in the rights-of-way.
Mr. Cole commented on construction material stored in the rights-of-way for property that has not
yet been developed, and restrictions being imposed upon developers.  Doug Vetter, City Engineer,
provided information on  th e intent of  th e or dinance, w hich is to l imit s torage of  c ontainers or
materials in the rights-of-way, and when an encroachment permit would be required.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Beauheim reviewed typical right-of-way distances from residential lot lines, and Mr. White
clarified the ordinance would prohibit a homeowner from placing materials in their driveway close
to t he curb line and explained the concern with run-off of certain materials into the storm sewer
system.  Discussion followed on a grace period involving the time frame for material storage in the
rights-of-way, and time frame flexibility for residents to incorporate landscaping projects relative
to a permit/fee.  Mr. Brown moved to amend the substitute as follows, seconded by Mr. Pierson:
Under Section 12.04.010, Subsections I., 3. and J., 3. strike the words “seventy-two (72) hours” and
replace with “30 days” so the subsections will read “Does not exceed a period of 30 days”.  Mr.
Beauheim expressed concern with the proposed amendment relative to any complaint that may be
received within the 30 day time period, and reviewed the process envisioned for a construction
project (ex: roof shingle replacement), indicating the intent of the ordinance is to create incentives
for individuals to place dumpsters on private property and if this can’t be done a permit would be
issued involving use of the right-of-way.  Discussion included the purpose of extending the permit
time to 30 days, environmental issues regarding run-off from materials, and concerns with blocking
sidewalks to users.  Upon inquiry, Martin Matsen, Development Director, provided information on
nuisance and zoning regulations, including setback requirements, and enforcement areas involving
storage of materials on private vs. public property, and Mr. Beauheim further explained when an
engineering encroachment permit is required.  Mr. Vetter advised the encroachment permit would
apply to work or construction being done in the right-of-way and if materials are not being used for
that purpose then a right-of-way permit for storage of materials would be needed.  Motion to amend
the substitute failed.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.  Voting “no” – Ms.
Ash, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen. Discussion
continued on encroachment permits, with Mr. Beauheim advising various sections of City Code
relate to t hese types of permits and explaining a developer is required to ob tain a permit prior to
construction, and concern with driveways being included in the proposed ordinance.  Mr. Collins
moved to eliminate references to driveways as noted throughout the substitute ordinance, seconded
by Mr. Pierson. During ensuing comments and upon inquiry, Mr. White reviewed existing City Code
Section 12.04.010 wording which does not prohibit obstructions of driveways and explained if the
amendment is adopted then materials could be placed in the area between the sidewalk and curb line
(i.e. driveway) which would potentially still be introducing materials into the storm water system
that are undesirable.  Discussion continued on Clean Water Act concerns, blockage of streets and
driveways, and allowable storage o f materials, equipment o r o ther t angible personal property if
within 100 feet of a personal residence as provided for in the ordinance.  Mr. Vetter p rovided
information on the typical footage involved in right-of-way areas involving sidewalks, curbs and
driveways.   Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles and Ms. Case voting “no”. Discussion followed on current
City Co de Sect ion 12. 04.010 pro visions and i ntent o f t he i nitial o rdinance t o rest rict s torage
containers within rights-of-way.  Mr. Beauheim requested the word “driveway” be reinserted into
Section 12.04.010, Subsections F. and G., and Section 12.04.080, Subsection A. as those sections
provide the authority to i ssue encroachment p ermits and are specific that a  permit is needed to
construct a driveway.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend by reinserting the word “driveway” into Section
12.04.010, Subsections F. and G., and Section 12.04.080, Subsection A., seconded by Ms. Ash.  Mr.
Cole ques tioned t he difference in fee s chedules for an  en croachment permit v s. a ri ght-of-way
permit with Mr. Beauheim providing an explanation of the fees.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Ms. Case voting
“no.”  Discussion on the proposed substitute ordinance as amended followed and included initial
intent of the ordinance and current restrictions on obstructions pursuant to Section 12.04.010 of City
Code.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend language in Section 12.04.010, Subsections I.3. and J.3. from
“Does not exceed a period of 72 hours” to “Does not exceed a period of 10 days,” seconded by Mr.
Brown.  M otion to amend carried.  V oting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the
exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no.”  Motion to amend by substitute as amended (three amendments)
carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson and Mayor
Kaysen.  Voting “no” - Ms. Case, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Main motion as amended
by substitute failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins and Mr. Pierson.
Voting “no” – Ms. Case, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  [Clerk’s note:
Passage of an ordinance on 3  and final reading requires a minimum of six affirmative votes.] Underrd
other City business later in the meeting, Ms. Broyles questioned the current penalty amount and
enforcement of  violations for storage c ontainers p resently located in r ights-of-way w ith Mayor
Kaysen advising a response to members of the governing body would be provided.
“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING TI TLE 17,  Z ONING, CH APTER 17.08,  Z ONING
DISTRICTS E STABLISHED, AND SE CTION 17.08. 040 CHEYENNE CIT Y MAP OF T HE
CHEYENNE CI TY CODE , BY CHA NGING T HE Z ONING CLA SSIFICATION FROM P
PUBLIC DISTRICT TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS DISTRICT FOR LOT 2, BLOCK ONE,
WINDMILL ROAD, SECOND FILING, (A PORTION OF THE NE1/4 OF SECTION 28, T.14N.,

for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown advised there was no recommendation on the item from

Discussion included concerns with unknown potential type of development involving future use of
the property; recommended property use pursuant to the PlanCheyenne document; process that may
be utilized to determine possible transfer of property ownership, and comment that the ordinance
was a zone change action only.  U pon inquiry to staff, Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director,
clarified any sale of the City land would come before the governing body for approval, and Brandon
Cammarata, Ur ban Pl anning Of fice, r eviewed recen t m odification t o t he l and us e m ap
(PlanCheyenne document) to reflect a community business use for the parcel, advising the property
could remain as an open space use, and stated the land use map was modified, as approved by the
governing body, as a preparatory aspect to any change in use of the  property, explaining any future
commercial business property use pursued would need to be consistent with allowable uses.  Martin
Matsen, Development Director, advised the parcel’s current zone classification is a Public District,
which includes parks as one of the uses, and provided information on the approval process and
requirements that may be imposed for any change to a Community Business District use depending
upon the level of development.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
with the exception of Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#3876)

LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
[CA] Juvenile Accountability Block Grant Contract between the State of Wyoming, Department
of Family Services,  Division of Juvenile Services and the City of Cheyenne for partial funding of
Municipal J uvenile C ourt s taffing a nd p retrial s ervices (J uvenile A ccountability B lock G rant).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $25,500.00).  (#5480)
Contract between the City of Cheyenne and WINhealth partners for City employee health
coverage.  Mr.  Val dez m oved t o appro ve, sec onded b y M r. Spi ker.  Upo n i nquiry, Ri ch
Wiederspahn, Human Resources Director, pro vided information o n co ntinuing health insurance
coverage r elative t o t hose individuals who leave employment with t he City pursuant t o federal
COBRA requirements (not an expense to the City) and stated new federal provisions are not yet
reflected in the current employee handbook.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend by substitute, seconded
by Mr. Spiker. Mr. Wiederspahn advised the substitute reduces the proposed increase in fees for the
next contract term from 10% to 9.5%, and expands chiropractic care coverage and vision and eye
wear coverage.  Upon inquiry concerning budgeting of the fee increase, Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
stated the fiscal year budget includes an 8% increase, that the increased insurance rates are based
on a calendar year, and that the difference in funding needed for 6 months (through the end of the
fiscal year) is anticipated to be covered due t o savings realized from new/replacement employee
hiring policies.  Appreciation was expressed to staff with regard to application of retiree pl an
increases being the same ratio as for active employees.  Upon query on industry increases for health
insurance coverage and amendments to the employee handbook, Mr. Wiederspahn responded that,
on average, information he has received is that the industry percentage increase varies anywhere
from 12-15%, and clarified the employee handbook is in the process of being updated and modified.
Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Pursuant to further
comments on federal changes to COBRA requirements, Dr. Glode, Medical Director, WinHealth,
advised Congress has a mended t he law for a  look-back p eriod a s w ell a s a  p rovision for filing
earlier, which r esulted in making t he application pr ocess more flexible, and co nfirmed COBRA
provides for up to 18 months of coverage.  Comment was made in opposition to WinHealth’s HMO
business structure, employee complaints, and level of coverage provided under the WinHealth plan.
Main motion as amended by substitute carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.  (#5481)
[CA] Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Police Department, Victim Witness Program and
the State of Wyoming, Division of Victim Services for ARRA VOCA funding for Fiscal Year 2010
(American Recovery & Reinvestment Act).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to
exceed $58,096.00).  (#5482)
Lease agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Union Telephone Company for wireless
rooftop communication system to be located at the Parks Maintenance Building, located at 4500
Dell Range Blvd. -- referred to Finance Committee.  Mr.  Valdez advised the item was reviewed
under other items for the agenda at the Finance Committee meeting and provisions of Title 2 of City
Code had been met.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Valdez moved
to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Upon inquiry, Teresa Moore, Parks and Recreation
Department,  explained the substitute contains changes to insurance requirements pursuant to City
Attorney and Risk Manager reviews; stated the agreement involves placing 6 cell phone t owers
(approximately 5' in height) on the roof of the Parks Maintenance facility to increase the cell phone
service range provided by Union Telephone, and reviewed generally the initial lease and renewal
terms of the agreement reflecting potential total revenue to the City in the amount of $231,637.00.
City At torney Dan  W hite an d M ayor K aysen pro vided addi tional i nformation o n t he i nitial
agreement term with renewal options and potential total revenue amounts.  Motion to amend carried.
Voting “yes” – al l m embers o f t he go verning b ody.  Upo n i nquiry, J im M alone, r ight-of-way
engineer for Union Telephone, clarified the towers are planned to be placed on the south end of the
building for radio wave propagation and the “pointing” to other towers being constructed within the
area for service coverage. Comment followed on possible placement of the towers closer to the north
end of the building from an aesthetic standpoint.  M r. Malone advised a structural survey of the
building had been conducted, and it had been determined that the south end was the optimum place
on the building to place the towers to provide coverage.  M ayor Kaysen requested Mr. Malone
convey the inquiry regarding placement of the towers on the north rather than the south end of the
building to Union Telephone to assess the proposed location of the towers relative to the comment
involving visual aesthetics.  M ain motion as amended by substitute carried.  V oting “yes” – all
members of the governing body. (#5483)

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-11-23 | CLIP_ID: 123 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 123

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
November 23, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Members of Boy Scout Troop 112 led those present in reciting the pledge of allegiance.
  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dor r, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  T here will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Brown
and Ms. Ash, agenda item #14 a. was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins m oved to
approve the remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON NOVEMBER
9, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON NOVEMBER
16, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. S piker moved t o approve payment o f vouchers as presented, including t hose vouchers
carried over from the Nov. 16, 2009 Vouchers meeting of the governing body, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Mr. Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-Tech
HVAC Services and vacated Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker moved to divide the question to allow for
a separate vote o n t he voucher payable t o Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, seconded by Mr. Valdez.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Mr.
Spiker moved to approve payment of the voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, seconded
by Mr. Collins.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers.  F ollowing an nouncement o f t he v ote, Mr . Brown r eturned t o Co uncil Ch ambers.
Discussion followed on vouchers payable to the following: VISA/UMB, professional license dues to
Wyo. Bd. o f CPAs for police dept. employee, employee travel related expenses, and business lunch
(food) meeting expenditure involving cert ain Council members and City Attorney staff; Behavioral
Interventions, equipment rental fee containing sales tax; First Presbyterian Church, reimbursement for
window re pair; C heyenne L ight, F uel a nd P ower, p ortion of  p ayment du e involving s treet l ights;
Laramie Co. Treasurer, reimbursement due to overpayment received on specific purpose optional tax
amount; Knife River, overlay expense for VA parking lot in conjunction with Henderson Flood Control
project, a nd Verizon Wi reless, c ell p hone c harges l isted f or P arks & R ecreation D ept.  D uring
discussion on the business lunch meeting expense (payable to VISA/UMB), Ms. Broyles indicated her
name was listed in the voucher explanation and clarified that she was not a participant in the meeting
and t hat h er n ame s hould b e removed with M ayor K aysen stating h er co mment wo uld b e n oted.
Responding to inquiries and providing information available on the aforementioned vouchers during
discussion w ere: B arb D orr, C ity T reasurer; L t. M ark M unari, P olice D ept., D oug V etter, C ity
Engineer, and Mayor Kaysen.  Motion to approve remaining vouchers as presented carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body.
“AN ORDINANCE REPE ALING SE CTION 9.08. 030, GA MBLING-PROHIBITED-
EXCEPTIONS, A ND SE CTION 9.08. 040, VIOL ATIONS BY  OW NER OR  OC CUPANT OF
PREMISES WHERE GAMBLING CONDUCTED, OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE,
CHAPTER 9.08, OFFENSES AGAINST PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY, OF THE CHEYENNE
CITY CODE” – referred to Public Services Committee (due to postponement from Sept. 14, 2009).
“AN OR DINANCE A MENDING TI TLE 17,  Z ONING, CHAPTER 17.08,  Z ONING
DISTRICTS E STABLISHED A ND SE CTION 17.08. 040 CHEYENNE CIT Y M AP OF  T HE
CHEYENNE CITY CODE, BY CHANGING THE ZONING CLASSIFICATION FROM LI LIGHT
INDUSTRIAL DISTRICT TO CB COMMUNITY BUSINESS DISTRICT FOR A PORTION OF
LOTS 6-17, AND VACATED 16' ALLEY, BLOCK 405, ORIGINAL CITY, CH EYENNE,
WYOMING ( LOCATED SOUTH OF  A ND ADJACENT T O W . L INCOLNWAY, E AST OF
STINSON AVENUE).” Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on
second r eading, s econded by Mr. Collins.  M otion c arried.  V oting “yes” –  a ll m embers o f t he
governing body.
“AN O RDINANCE A MENDING A  P ORTION OF T HE CI TY O F CHE YENNE’S
SANITATION FEE SCHEDULE PERTAINING TO THE RECYCLE RESIDENTIAL COLLECTION
MONTHLY FEE, AS APPROVED BY ORDINANCE NO. 3851 ON JUNE 8, 2009.”  Reporting for
Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr.
Valdez moved to postpone to Dec. 14, 2009, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Discussion followed on the
purpose of postponement.  Upon inquiry Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised information is being
compiled by staff on recycling program operational costs, and advised a substitute ordinance has been
prepared due to additional discussions on the monthly recycling fee.  Motion to postpone failed.  Voting
“no” – al l members of t he go verning body.  M r. Collins moved to amend by substitute o rdinance,
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Elias explained the substitute removes wording related to a discounted
fee based on residential water usage as proposed in the initial ordinance, and provides options for use
of smaller recycling containers,  every other week pickup service that would be available to residences
and churches, and a 95 gallon container commercial collection monthly fee.  He further explained 95
vs. 60 gallon containers that would be available; that residents’ “opt out” to decline a container would
still involve a monthly fee based on the resident’s current sanitation container size being utilized, and
that the overall intent of the program is to encourage recycling efforts to reduce the amount of materials
being disposed of in the landfill.  Discussion included written comments and questions on the recycling
program and current monthly fee as presented and submitted by Do n Erickson during t he Finance
Committee meeting; current economy and effect on operational costs relative to the recycling market
and program costs; staff information on services, equipment and costs being researched and compiled;
overall program master plan; usage of recycling blue bins located throughout the City and collection,
transporting and disposal costs; fairness issues involving different sized households; container sizes;
mandatory f ee r elative to f inancial b urdens f or those on  f ixed i ncomes; c urrent ti pping f ees f or
transporting solid waste to the Colorado outlet, and single stream recycling programs involving a fee
vs. voluntary recycling efforts.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend the proposed substitute by revising the
following fee areas, seconded by Mr. Valdez: Private Residences and Churches Every Other Week
Services – Re cycle res idential collection m onthly f ee (60 gal lon co ntainer) f rom $3. 00 t o $2. 00;
combination 60 gallon trash/60 gallon recycle containers from $16.00 to $15.00, and combination 60
gallon t rash/95 gal lon recy cle co ntainers f rom $18. 00 t o $17. 00.  Di scussion o n t he pro posed
amendment followed a nd included initial fees for t he curb side recycling pilot pro gram in t he Sun
Valley area, and input from Mr. Elias concerning the proposed change in fee amount affiliated with the
60 gallon trash/95 gallon recycle containers.  Pursuant to Mr. Elias’ comments, Mr. Pierson corrected
his previous motion to amend to exclude the proposed change to the combination 60 gallon trash/95
gallon recycle containers amount, with concurrence by Mr. Valdez as second to the motion.  Comments
continued o n r ecycle c ontainer s izes; p rogram c osts b eing c ompiled b y P ublic Wo rks s taff;
comprehensive recycle plan; budget expenses for program operations vs. cost of service, and revenue
stream affiliated with continuation of a recycling program.  M otion to amend the substitute carried.
Voting “yes” –  Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. P ierson, Mr. S piker, Mr. V aldez a nd Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and Dr. Rinne.  Discussion followed on the structure
of the ordinance relative to the order in which the fee areas were listed (it was noted that pursuant to
the amendment, the listing order of amounts would be revised); number of 60 gallon recycle containers
currently available and acquisition of additional containers; benefits of a curbside recycling program;
past and future educational efforts to inform the public of program components, and level of interest
from commercial businesses in participating in the container program.  Motion to amend by substitute,
as amended, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case
voting “no.”  R equest was made that more green bins be placed throughout the community.  M ain
motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of
Ms. Case voting “no.”
“A R ESOLUTION A UTHORIZING TH E M AYOR A ND TH E C ITY C OUNCIL TO
CONDUCT A M EETING WI TH T HE BOARD O F D IRECTORS O F T HE D OWNTOWN
DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY AND OTHER INTERESTED PARTIES TO DISCUSS FUTURE
DEVELOPMENT NEEDS OF THE GREATER CHEYENNE DOWNTOWN AREA.”  Mr. Brown
moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Case.  Discussion included allowing transition time for the DDA
Board t o c omplete it s p rocess in s electing a  n ew director a nd a cclimating n ew members r ecently
appointed to the Board; recent failure of the DDA District mill levy special election ballot question;
input by t he governing bo dy t o t he ne w d irector a nd D DA B oard m embers o f e xpectations a nd
communications, and DDA goals.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend to add the following sentence at the
end of the Now Therefore Be It  Resolved clause, seconded by Mr. Spiker:  T his meeting shall take
place after a new DDA director is employed and working for 60 days or more and, therefore, becoming
accustomed to their job duties and current situations.  Comments in support and non-support of the
proposed amendment followed.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins,
Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Case
and Mr. Brown.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend the same clause within the Resolution by inserting the
words “and managers” following the word “owners”, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  D iscussion on the
proposed amendment followed and included the scope of the Resolution; involvement by DDA District
stakeholders on issues affecting managers of businesses in which owners of the business properties are
non-local; stakeholder communication issues, and that the holding of a public meeting would include
the op portunity for business managers w ithin th e D istrict to  a ttend w ithout r equiring a  c hange to
Resolution wording.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins,
Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Case and Mr.
Valdez.  Following further comments on voter turnout and dissemination of information connected with
the recent DDA District special election, motion to adopt as twice amended failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms.
Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mayor Kaysen.
LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
[CA] Professional Services Contract between the City of Cheyenne, Office of Youth Alternatives,
and Wyoming Department of Family Services, for grant funding for Intensive Support Mentoring for
youth in the Municipal Court System – referred to Finance Committee.
CHANGE ORDERS/CONTRACT MODIFICATIONS:
Change Order No. 1 to Bid #R-31-08 between the City of Cheyenne and Kubat Equipment and
Service Co. for additional work needed to complete installation of the above ground vaulted tank and
fueling system for the Parks Maintenance Building, located at 4500 Dell Range Blvd. (2003-2006 1%
Sales T ax).  M r. V aldez m oved t o a pprove i n t he a mount o f $ 1,895.00, seconded by Ms. As h.
Discussion included timeliness of payments to contractors and, upon inquiry, project information from

Contract Modification No. 2 to Contract No. 5357 between the City of Cheyenne and S&S

Valdez moved to approve in the amount of $32,447.28, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry, Sara
Vasquez, Assistant Purchasing Director, confirmed payment had already been made to the contractor
but pay ment o f ret ainage h ad n ot y et b een rel eased, an d Do ug Vet ter, Ci ty E ngineer, pro vided
information on the work that was needed in the final stages of the project due to unforeseen conditions,
and t he us e o f force accounts.  Di scussion i ncluded t he f ollowing t opics: T imeliness o f co ntract
modifications s ubmitted fo r approval; c oncern w ith f orce a ccount f unds; a dministrative
communications involving City Co uncil an d n otification o f t imely an d/or emergency-type co ntract
modifications, a nd c urrent e conomic c onditions, p roject c osts a nd budgetary c oncerns.  D uring
discussion Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, provided the amount of funding balance of the street renovation
1% sales tax fund, noting an additional amount will be included in the next fiscal year budget due to

Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Dr. Rinne and Mr.
Spiker.  M ayor Ka ysen a dvised he  ha s be en c ommunicating w ith s taff to e valuate p ractices a nd
procedures involving contract modifications, and that a project tracking form has been developed to
share as a communication tool with City Council members.
[CA] Contract Mo dification No . 2 t o Co ntract No . 53 16 b etween t he City o f Cheyenne and J2
Contracting for additional materials and labor for construction of David R. Romero South Cheyenne
Community Park (State Loan & Investment Board) – referred to Finance Committee.
[CA] Contract Modification No. 3 to Bid No. M-5-09 between the City of Cheyenne and RECCO,
Inc. d /b/a Fi ve-R-Co. f or a dditional w ork a nd a menities f or t he Cheyenne Y outh Ac tivity a nd

CONSIDERATION OF BIDS:

Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, advised the potential postponement should not affect

[CA] Consideration of Bid #R- 4-10 for the surplus sale of one Deery Mastic Mixer to include eight

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-12-14 | CLIP_ID: 127 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 127

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
December 14, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items l isted with the desig nation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.) Mayor Kaysen explained a point of information pertaining to Agenda Item
#17(g), stating the item should have been introduced as a resolution instead of a contract and that it
would be referred to Finance Committee as a resolution instead of a contract.  Mr. Collins moved to
approve Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON NOVEMBER
23, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON DECEMBER
7, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr.
Brown declared a conflict of interest (business interest) due to a voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC
Services and vac ated Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker moved to divide the question to allow for  a
separate vote on the voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Mr. Spiker
moved to approve payment of the voucher payable to Ameri-Tech HVAC Services, seconded by Mr.
Collins.  Mo tion c arried.  Vo ting “ yes” – a ll m embers of  the  governing bo dy pr esent i n Co uncil
Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, M r. Brown returned to Council Chambers.  Upon
inquiry reg arding a vouche r pay able to CivicPlus ( $850), City  Clerk Carol I ntlekofer explained
payment is for a one time setup fee for social networking sites Facebook and Twitter in conjunction
with the city’s website to disseminate information to the public. Motion to approve remaining vouchers
as presented carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
“AN O RDINANCE RE PEALING SE CTION 9 .08.030, G AMBLING-PROHIBITED-
EXCEPTIONS, AND SECT ION 9.08.040, VI OLATIONS BY  OWNER OR OCC UPANT OF
PREMISES WHERE GAMBLING CONDUCTED, OF TITLE 9, PUBLIC PEACE AND WELFARE,
CHAPTER 9.08, OFFENSES AGAINST PUBLIC PEACE AND DECENCY, OF THE CHEYENNE
CITY CODE.”  Bryan Grzegorczyk, speaking on behalf of the Laramie County Liquor Association,
stated that idea lly Association m embers would like to have state statutes pe rtaining to ga mbling
adopted without local ordinance provisions, but that the group is in favor of working with the governing
body on proposed ordinance changes.  Also providing comments on the proposed gambling ordinance
were:  Mike Ra ndelman, M ike L anghoff ( co-owner o f T wo B ar B owl), M atthew H avens, M ary
Shappell, Dan Shappell, Kenneth Atell and Donn Edmunds.  Topics presented and discussed included:
Repealing current city code and adopting state law; suggestion of postponement of the ordinance to
provide for input from poker players within the community; support of tournament-style poker games
and non-profit fund raising opportunities; opposition to minimum non-refundable $25.00 permit fee;
concerns with distribution of fees and lack of refunding a permit fee if a game is cancelled; who would
be authorized to examine or review the books and records involving an event for which a permit has
been obtained; permit application process and burden to applicants; advertising of games; poker as a
game of skill; on-line betting and football/sports-type pools; providing flexibility of gaming rules, and
support for changing current city code.  Upon inquiry during audience discussion, Dan White, City
Attorney, stated that most of the time a police officer would be the individual designated to review the
books and records involved with a poker event; advised of requirements under state law that must be
met to constitute a bona fide social relationship, and discussed distribution of gaming proceeds relative
to w hat c ould c onstitute p rofessional g ambling, a dvertising r estrictions, a nd in tent o f th e r evised
ordinance to provide a permitting process for certain types of tournament-style poker events.  Reporting
for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by
Mr. Pierson.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute ordinance dated 12-9-09 which includes an
amendment recommended at the Public Services Committee meeting to change the word “present” to
“participating” in  Se ction 2 . o f th e o rdinance, Se ction 9 .08.030 B ., s econded b y M r. Pi erson.
Discussion followed on who would be required to apply for a permit (i.e. organizer of the event)
pursuant to wording that was changed in the substitute ordinance, and an affidavit requirement by the
property owner/proprietor of the fa cility where tournament-style poker events would be  held.  Mr.
Collins moved to amend the substitute with a revised substitute ordinance, seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Mr. Randelman, Mr. Langhoff, Mr. Shappell and Ms. Shappell questioned the advocacy of the city’s
involvement in gambling;  suggested changing the word “poker” to “gambling”, and discussed allowing
someone other than the applicant of the permit to be present at the games in case the applicant is unable
to be present, and the permit fee vs. participant buy-in amount.  Discussion followed on the purpose
of the orig inal ordinance, which was to repea l current city code a nd adopt state statute pr ovisions
involving gambling, and “compromising” to legally allow for tournament-style poker events as outlined
in the revised substitute.  Motion to amend the substitute with a revised substitute ordinanc e carried.
Voting “yes” –  all members of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Ms. Case
voting “no”. Mr.  Collins moved to amend Section 2. of the ordinance, Section 9.08.030 D. from $25.00
to $5.00 as the minimum amount for the non-re fundable permit fee  or the a mount equal to the
participant’s buy-in, whichever is greater, seconded by Mr.  Spiker. Comments followed concerning
the assessment of a permit fee.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White explained the intent of a permit fee is t o
recoup the city’s cost involving permit processing and issuance; clarified that all funds of the game
must be distributed to game participants, and expressed his belief that the city would not be considered
as participating or endorsing gambling relative to liability concerns.  During discussion, Mr. Pierson
raised a point of order  with Mayor Kaysen concurring that comments should per tain to the cur rent
motion to amend involving the permit fee.  Further discussion followed on regulating poker events
affiliated with a permit vs. those poker events not requiring a permit.  Motion to amend failed (due to
a tie vote) .  Voting “ yes” – Ms. B royles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pi erson, Mr. Spiker  and Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” – M s. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Dr. Rinne, and Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Brown moved to
amend to rem ove sub-section D. in its  entirety.  Motion died due to lack of a  second.  D iscussion
continued on the proposed permit and fee.  Upon inquiry, City Clerk Carol Intlekofer advised upon her
brief review involving staff time and supply cost to process and issue a permit, she estimated the cost
would be approximately $30.  Ms. Ash moved to amend sub-section D. to state the amount of the non-
refundable permit fee shall be equal to the buy-in of the poker event if the buy-in is $25 or greater, and
if the buy-in is less than $25 then no permit would be required, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr. Langhoff,
Mr. Randelman, Mr . Shappell, Mr. Have ns, and Mr. Grz egorczyk commented on the pr oposed
amendment.  Topic s included wh ether other a reas of the ordina nce w ould be af fected by  the
amendment; support for the amendment; suggestion of postponing the ordinance for further review and
input; permitting vs. not permitting games under the buy-in amount, and permit application time frame
relative to each poker event.  Mr. Collins suggested a friendly amendment to the motion concerning
inclusion of affiliated wording in sub-section  B. (3) as well as within any other appropriate areas of
the ordinance.  Following a 5 minute recess to review ordinance areas that might involve additional
changes due to the proposed amendment, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 7:35 p.m.  J ohn
Knepper, Assistant City Attorney, stated there would be three places in the ordinance that would need
to be changed to effectuate the proposed amendment: Sub-section B.(3), and sub-sections C. and D.
Ms. Ash, with concurrence by Mr. Valdez as second to the motion, agreed to include changes to the
areas outlined by Mr. Knepper in her previous motion to amend.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Knepper clarified
the proposed or dinance does not aff ect a ny underlying requirements; that the ac tivity must be a
tournament-style poker game with participants being  at least 21 y ears of age.  Motion to amend as
revised carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case
voting “no”.  Comments followed regarding lack of input on the proposed ordina nce provisions by
interested members of the community, and incidental affiliated monetary benefits, excluding gaming
proceeds, that may be received by certain businesses where poker events may be held.  Mo tion to
amend by substitute as amended by revised substitute ordinance, as amended, carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.  Discussion on the
main motion as amended followed and included comments regarding the minimum 21 years of age
requirement for poker event participants.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by striking sub-section B. (1)
in its entirety, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  (Clerk’s note - sub-section wording is as follows: “ ... The poker
event is a tournament-style poker game, in which all participants are at least 21 years of age, pay a fee
for participation, and all betting is conducted with chips allocated equally among all participants; ...”.)
Mr. Langhoff, Mr. Shappell and Mr. Havens spoke in favor of adopting state law vs. the ordinance, and
made comments either for or against the proposed amendment.  Mr. Brown withdrew his motion with
concurrence by Dr. Rinne as second to the motion. Mr. Valdez moved to postpone for 90 days (to
approximately March 8, 2010), seconded by Ms. Case.  Pacomio Sandoval commented on community
gambling and spoke in support of socialized gambling only.  Tom Sanders, Mr. Shappell, Julie Russell,
Mr. Grzeg orczyk and Ms. Shappell spoke in opposition t o postponement g enerally.  Additional
comments included support for repealing current city code and adopting state statute provisions, and
suggestion that a committee be formed to work on a draft for a future ordinance.  Upon inquiry, Mayor
Kaysen stated if the ordinance as amended is not approved, provisions of existing city code would be
enforced, a nd Mr. White reviewe d pos tponing the proposed or dinance vs. repe aling the exist ing
ordinance regulating gambling.  It was clarified that the proposed ordinance only regulates tournament-
style poker games and that all g ambling activities and eve nts must comply  with state statute.  Mr.
Valdez withdrew his motion to postpone with Ms. Case stating she was not in agreement and would
not withdraw her second to the motion.  Mr. Collins called for the previous question (to limit debate),
seconded by Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Intlekofer explained her understanding of the process
involving the call for the previous question, and stated the motion was non-debatable.  Motion (to limit
debate) carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Motion to postpone for 90 days
failed.  Voting “no”  – all members of the governing body.  Ms. Ash moved to amend sub-section B.
(1) by striking participants must be at least “ ... 21 years of age ...” and replacing with “ ... 18 years of
age ...”, se conded by Mr. Collins. Dis cussion followed o n the par ticipant ag e limit and included
comments of concern regarding correlation of youth drinking and gambling.  Mr. Brown called for the
previous question (to limit debate), seconded by Ms Ash.  Motion failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Mr.
Brown, Mr. Collins, Mr. Valdez and May or Kaysen.  Voting  “no” – Ms. B royles, Ms. Case, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.  Comments followed on the administrative guidelines affiliated with
poker event permit activities, and the proposed amendment to reduce the age of event participants from
21 to 18 years of age.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins, Dr.
Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson a nd Mr.
Spiker.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White advised the administrative guidelines are subject to change and are
not a par t of the ac tual ordinance.  Comments followed on the g overning body’s ability to enac t
regulations more restrictive than state statute.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend Section 3. of the revised
and amended substitute ordinance by striking “but not sooner than F ebruary 1, 2010", e nabling the
ordinance to bec ome ef fective up on approval and publication, sec onded by  Ms. Broy les.  Mr.
Randelman, Mr. Atell, Mr. Langhoff, Mr. Edmunds and Ms. Shappell provided comments.  Topics
presented and discussed included: Opposition to the ordinance, possible future discussions which may
involve ordinanc e amendments, and ty pes of g ambling le gally allowed pe r state statute a nd the
ordinance.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Knepper clarified under the ordinance if poker is played in a public place
it has to be tournament-style poker.  Upon request to clarify amendments made to the ordinance during
the meeting, City Clerk I ntlekofer reviewed the mot ions that had been made  and appr oved during
discussion. Further  comments wer e made  reg arding controls and g uidelines for a llowing soc ial
gambling in the community, and when the ordinance would become effective.  Motion to amend to
provide for the effectiveness of the ordinance upon approval and publication carried.  Voting “yes” –
all members of the governing body.  Discussion continued on the proposed ordinance, current city code,
and adoption of state law provisions instead of the revised substitute ordinance as amended.  Ms. Ash
moved to amend sub-section C. by “ ... authorizing the mayor to appoint agents, including its police
officers, to seek any additional information ...”, se conded by Mr. Collins. M otion to amend carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.  Motion
carried to approve the revised substitute as amended.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning
body with the exception of Ms. Case voting  “no”.  Comments followed r egarding supporting a n
enforceable o rdinance, a thletic s ports p ools, a nd t ournament-style p oker g ames. M ain m otion to
approve as amended by revised substitute ordinance as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members
of the governing body with the exception of Mr. Brown and Ms. Case voting “no”. (#3877)
Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 9:07 p.m.

members of the governing body with the exception of Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case and Mr. Pierson voting
“no”. Comments followed in support of sustaining the residential recycling program and recouping
costs already invested. Main motion as amended carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no.” (#3879)

MEETING: City Council | DATE: 2009-12-28 | CLIP_ID: 131 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 131

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
December 28, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning at
6:00 p.m. in Council Chambers.  Present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS:
Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez.  Also present: Kristy Anderson, Deputy City Clerk; Tammy Fossen, City Treasurer’s Office,
and Dan White, City Attorney.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
Nominations an d e lection o f Ci ty Co uncil Pr esident f or 2010.  M ayor K aysen an nounced,
pursuant to City Code, Title 2, the Mayor conducts the elections for City Council President and Vice-
President but may not v ote, therefore, he would not b e participating in voting for the elections of
Council President and Vice-President.  M r. Collins moved to n ominate and elect Dr. Rinne as City
Council President for 2010, seconded by Ms. Ash. Mr. Spiker moved to nominate and elect Ms. Broyles
as City Council President for 2010, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  There being no further nominations, Mr.
Collins moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Brown.  Motion to cease nominations carried.
Voting “yes” – all members o f City Council. Comments followed regarding an informal straw poll
conducted by council members each year prior to the election of Council President and Vice-President,
and the role of Council President.  Upon request, Dr. Rinne and Ms. Broyles provided comments on
their experience serving o n City Council and how each felt t hey could best represent t he Council.
Motion t o elect Dr. Rinne did not carry. Voting “yes” - M s. Ash, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr.
Valdez.  Voting “no”- Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker with Ms. Case “passing”.
Motion to elect Ms. Broyles did not carry.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and
Mr. Spiker. Voting “no” - Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez with Ms. Case “passing”.
Mr. Pierson moved to reopen nominations, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Ms. Case raised a point of order
regarding her option to pass during voting, advising she did not abstain but wished to vote later. Mr.
Pierson withdrew his motion to reopen nominations, with concurrence by Mr. Collins as second to the
motion.  Upo n request regarding a r uling on breaking a t ie relative to passage of a motion to elect
officers, City Attorney Dan White requested a brief recess in order to research Roberts Rule of Order
further before making a ruling.  Following a 15 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting
at 6:40 p.m.  M r. White advised after reviewing Robert’s Rules of Order the option to pass is only
allowed when there is a roll call vote; the “pass” procedure doesn’t apply in this situation as it is a voice
vote, th erefore a  “ pass” c ould b e d etermined a s a n a bstention or  a  n egative v ote m aking b oth
nominations a 4-5 vote with neither candidate prevailing.  He further clarified pursuant to City Code,
the Mayor conducts the elections, but does not cast a vote.  Upon inquiry on whether it was in order
to make a new motion, Mr. White clarified per Robert’s Rules of Order, council continues voting until
a c andidate i s e lected s o n ominations w ould n eed to b e r eopened. M ayor K aysen c alled f or
nominations. Mr. Pierson moved to nominate Ms. Broyles as City Council President for 2010, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Mr. Collins moved to nominate Dr. Rinne as City Council President for 2010, seconded
by Ms. Ash. Mr. Collins moved to  cease nominations, seconded by Mr. Brown.  M otion to  cease
nominations carried, with all Council members voting “yes”. Mr. Spiker raised a point of order on
whether a f ailed motion co uld be re-introduced at t he same meeting with Mr. White advising per
Robert’s Rules of Order nominations could continue, even for the same candidates, until someone was
elected.  He also clarified “pass” could not be used in this type of vote.   Motion to elect Dr. Rinne did
not carry by a majority vote.  Voting “yes” - Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez.  Voting
“no” - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.  Motion to elect Ms. Broyles
carried by a majority vote.  Voting “yes” - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Mr.
Spiker.  Voting “no” - Ms. Ash, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez.
Nominations a nd e lection of  City Council V ice P resident for 2 010.  M r. Collins moved to
nominate and elect Mr. Pierson, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Th ere being no further nominations, Mr.
Brown moved that nominations cease, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Motion to cease nominations carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of City Council.  Upon inquiry regarding whether a straw poll had been
held for Vice President, it was advised it had not.  Upon further inquiry on how Mr. Pierson had come
to be nominated, it was advised he was nominated for his past experience serving on City Council.
Motion to nominate and elect Mr. Pierson carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of City Council.  
Mayor Kaysen announced that individuals in attendance in the audience that may be present for
the two agenda items introducing an ordinance regarding terms and conditions of employment for non-
uniformed employees and sworn officers would only be referred to Finance Committee at this meeting
with no discussion at this time on either ordinance.  He advised the Finance Committee would meet in
Room 122 on January 4, 2010 beginning at 11:00 a.m.  However, he stated if those employees present
wished to speak under other business at the meeting they were welcome to stay and provide comments
at that time. 
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  T here will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two other
members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its normal
sequence on the agenda.) Upon request by Mr. Collins, and pursuant to support received by Mr. Brown
and Ms. Ash , agenda item #23(e) was removed from the Consent Agenda. Upon request by Mr. Brown,
and pursuant to support received by Mr. Collins and Mr. Pierson, agenda item #23(b) was removed
from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins moved to approve remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded
by Mr. Brown.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON DECEMBER
14, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
[CA] MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON DECEMBER
21, 2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Brown moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr.
Valdez declared a conflict of interest due to a voucher payable to radio station KYOY ($250.00), for
advertising, a nd va cated C ouncil C hambers.  Motion c arried.  V oting “yes” -  a ll members o f t he
governing b ody prese nt in Co uncil Chambers.  F ollowing an nouncement o f t he v ote, Mr.  Valdez
returned to Council Chambers. 
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing for a transfer of ownership for a restaurant liquor license
application f iled w ith t he Ci ty Cl erk’s Of fice f or Wei Sheng Qi u d /b/a Go lden Ho use Ch inese
Restaurant, 1651 Carey Ave., Ste. 8, Cheyenne, WY (License currently held by Yu Zheng d/b/a Golden
House Restaurant, 1651 Carey Ave., Ste. 8, Cheyenne, WY).    There being no comments or discussion
and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the public hearing and referred the
application to Finance Committee.
Mayor Kaysen op ened a  p ublic h earing to d etermine w hether c ompliance of  a nnexation
conditions required by Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402 exists for the parcels commonly known as So uthwest
Properties, situated in a portion of Sections 12 and 13, T. 13N., R. 67W., of the 6  P.M. Laramieth
County, WY. (Ge nerally located e ast o f So uthwest Drive, So uth o f Interstate 80 , West o f BNSF
Railroad, and North of Woodenshoe Road, including Cheyenne Animal Shelter and associated rights-
of-way).  Don Threewit, Planning Services Dept., reviewed requirements and compliance of conditions
contained in Wyo. Stat. §15-1-402, including specific information indicating compliance with Wyo.
Stat. §15-1-402(a)(i) through (vi).  Deb Roden, attorney with Woodhouse Roden Law Of fice,
representing county residents residing in the area proposed for annexation, advised they had the same
objections to this annexation action as they did regarding the Swan Ranch annexation.  M s. Roden
raised concerns with traffic congestion and egress/ingress to the development; geographical a nd
physical isolation of the area from the city and contiguity issues.  M ike Basom, attorney with Dray,
Thompson and Dyekman, representing the developer, advised these issues have been raised before with
other a nnexations w ith t he same platting, p lanning a nd d esign issues.  H e a dvised t his p roperty is
referred to as a natural growth property in the PlanCheyenne document and stated issues could be
worked out within the platting phase of the development. Leo Garcia, spoke in opposition to annexation
based on traffic concerns and commented he does not feel this is for the health, safety and welfare of
the individuals that live in this area. There being no further comments or input and purpose of the
hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed the hearing.

“A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR AND CITY CLERK TO EXECUTE A
STATE HIS TORIC PRES ERVATION OFFICE CERTI FIED LOCAL GOVERNMENT GRANT
AWARD #56-10- 000000.03 FOR A C LASS I I CUL TURAL RESOURC E SURVEY OF  T HE
BELVOIR R ANCH P ROPERTY.” M r. V aldez moved to a dopt, s econded by M r. C ollins. U pon
inquiry, Don Threewitt, Planning Services Dept., advised the total survey cost is $25,626.00, which
includes the following funding: City hard cash match of $5,000.00 through the Urban Planning Small
Grant Match fund, the award of a grant from the State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO) in the
amount of $15,000.000, an in-kind local match of $2,160.00 and staff time (150 hours) and materials
match of $3,466.00 included in the Planning Department budget.  Discussion followed on the survey
providing the opportunity to develop the ranch in the future, deadline time frame for grant application
and c ouncil approval. U pon inquiry, M r. T hreewit a dvised a n e xtension for t he a pplication t o be
submitted had been applied for and received to Jan. 31, 2010.  He further clarified $5,000 had been
previously budgeted and advised this is a “sole source” for services  and will not go out to bid.  Dr.
Rinne moved to amend the last “Be it further resolved” clause to add at the end of the sentence “and
acceptance by City Council,” seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon inquiry regarding whether the Resolution
was time sensitive, Mr. Threewit reiterated the deadline to submit acceptance of the grant was Jan. 31,
2010.  Discussion followed on time frame for submittal of grant acceptance and allowing enough time
for staff to negotiate survey provisions to meet the deadline, occurrence of SHPO grants being awarded,
and concerns with taking on more financial responsibilities during a budget crisis. Upon inquiry, Mr.
Threewitt advised SHPO grants have been awarded annually, however t he City has not previously
turned down a grant, stating he was unsure whether non-acceptance could effect future awards.  He
further reviewed soft match provisions involving volunteer time and staff time, and hard cash aspects
involving the project.  Motion to amend failed.  Voting “yes” - Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Dr. Rinne and
Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” - Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mayor
Kaysen.  Discussion continued on future opportunities to apply for grants for the ranch.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Threewitt, provided information on state grant funding available and the City’s share of awarded
funding.  Main motion failed.   Voting “yes” - Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.
Voting “no” - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Spiker.
