PRELIMINARY PLATS/PRELIMINARY ZONE CHANGE:
Preliminary Plat for Block 140, Original City, 2  Filing, a replat of Lots 1-8, Block 140,nd
Original City, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located at the northwest corner of the intersection of E. 24th Street
and Warren Avenue).  Mr. Brown moved to acknowledge receipt with staff recommendations #1, 2,
3 and 5, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Upon inquiry, Brett Walker, Development Department, explained
the Development office had not approved this area as a dirt parking lot.  Brad Emmons, AVI and agent
for applicant, explained paving of the lot is planned and advised the State is currently using the dirt lot
to alleviate on-street parking.  H e clarified the paving will be done after site plan, zone change and
vacation of alley are completed.  Ian Cattalier, State of Wyoming, Construction Management, explained
the contract was to be done Dec. 30, 2009, but budget constraints had precluded some of the work.
Upon inquiry a bout s everal p arking facilities a lready in e xistence for s tate e mployee p arking, M r.
Cattalier explained plans are for a new office building at this location, but in an effort to maintain dust
control, paving of the parking lot is planned if the building will not be constructed in the near future.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body.  
Preliminary Zone Change from MR-1 Medium Density Residential-Established District to PUD
Planned Unit Development District for Lot 1, Original City Block 140, 2  Filing , Cheyenne, Wyomingnd
(located at the northwest corner of the intersection of E. 24th Street and Warren Avenue).  Mr. Brown
moved to acknowledge receipt with staff recommendations #2, 3, 4 and 6 and Planning Commission
recommendation #7, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon inquiry, Suzanne Norton, State of Wyoming,
Construction Management, explained that due to a study that was conducted, the parking that is planned
is adequate for employees and visitors.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing
body.
[CA] Preliminary P lat for Woodhaven S ubdivision, a  r eplat of  Lots 1  a nd 2 , Block On e, Farson
Addition and all of Block One, Dry Creek Twin Homes, Cheyenne, Wyoming (located east of and
adjacent to Wills Road, south of the Dry Creek Channel and Parkview Drive) -- referred to Public
Services Committee.
LEASES/CONTRACTS/LEGAL:
[CA] Agreement of Acceptance between the City of Cheyenne and the Wyoming Department of
Transportation for 0.16 acres of highway right-of-way adjacent to Nationway between Dunn Avenue
and Logan Avenue for the purpose of building Greenway Pathways (Greenway construction funded
by 2007 Norris TEAL Grant).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.  (#5492)
Lease Agreement between Francis E. Warren Post No. 1881, Veterans of Foreign Wars and the
City of Cheyenne, Parks and Recreation Department, for the city’s use of Pride Park Softball Complex.
Phil Harris, Quartermaster Secretary/Treasurer of Post 1881, explained the lease was initially for the
use of 2 girls ball fields and explained no federal dollars were used for construction of the fields. He
advised the lease was dissolved 2 years ago, and they offered the fields for City use for girls softball
and other youth activities.  Mr. Harris advised Post would like to continue to see the fields used by the
youth o f t he co mmunity with a l ong t erm lease agree ment for use o f t he fields with maintenance
provided by the lessee.  He further advised if the City is not interested in this arrangement, Extreme
Softball Club had indicated they would be interested in entering into negotiations for use of the field.
Lou Simpson objected to the lease agreement because of the City’s current financial situation and costs
associated with maintaining the fields. Upon inquiry, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation D irector,
reported the projected cost to maintain the fields is approximately $20,000 annually and explained there
is a shortage of fields in the community.  He advised current User Agreements involving athletic fields
will expire as of December 31, 2009 and will not be renewed, stating the City is looking at “pay for
play” agreements.  John Palmer questioned maintenance costs involved.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve
in t he amount o f $1.00, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Di scussion followed o n leasing fields to the girl’s
Extreme Softball Club, overall increase in maintenance costs of city parks, investing in future baseball
infrastructure, budget cuts and effect on recreational activities and services, providing a facility for the
girls’ softball leagues, a nd c osts a ssociated w ith maintaining a nd p roviding u pgrades to  th e fields.
Upon inquiry, M r. P arish a dvised t hat t he fields w ould p rimarily b e u sed fo r p ractice a s t he g irls
softball gam es are curr ently pl ayed at  Co nverse f ield. Upo n f urther i nquiry, M r. Harri s pro vided
information on potential negotiations with the softball club and use of the fields.  Major Dorr spoke in
support o f teams from out of town u sing t he fields, bringing r evenue d ollars into t he c ommunity.
Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - Ms. Ash, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor
Kaysen.  Voting “no” - Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker. (#5493)
Following a five minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:47 p.m.
[CA] Agreement with Laramie County to p rovide radio communications equipment to th e City of
Cheyenne, Public Works Department, purchased with funds from U.S. Department of H omeland
Security and administered by Wyoming Department of Homeland Security  (P artial 2007-2010 1%
Sales Tax).  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (City’s match $4,335.27). (#5494)
[CA] Agreement between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming Department of Transportation for a
Transportation Enhancement Activities - Local (TEAL) Grant for continuation of the Greenway System
(Walterscheid Pedestrian Underpass Project)  (1% Specific Purpose Sales and Use Tax).  Motion (per

Professional S ervices A greement b etween th e C ity of  C heyenne a nd A yres A ssociates f or
design and construction engineering services for the E. Pershing Blvd. Reconstruction Project (Phase
IV) (2003-2006 & 2007 - 2010 1% Sales Tax, BOPU).  Frank Cole spoke in opposition to the project.
Mr. Valdez moved to approve, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Concerns were expressed with project costs
and a vailability o f f unds f or c ompleting t he p roject. U pon i nquiry, D oug V etter, C ity En gineer,
explained the budget for this project was formulated approximately 5 years ago involving 5th penny

agreement, construction bid alternates based on funding and possible grant funding availability. Nathan
Beauheim, Assistant City Engineer, reviewed areas that could help reduce project costs, and clarified
funds c urrently b udgeted a nd f unds needed to c omplete th e p roject, p roviding a  s preadsheet on
proposed costs and funding.  G ene MacDonald, Ayres Associates, addressed questions relating to
roundabout issues and what would be most cost effective for the project.  Upon further inquiry, Mr.
MacDonald advised that a portion of the money is allocated for right-of-way acquisition.  Upon inquiry,
Mr. Beauheim clarified money for the project is currently uncommitted. Motion carried.   Voting “yes”
- a ll members o f t he g overning b ody w ith t he e xception o f M s. C ase v oting “ no” ($ 999,856.00).
(#5496)

program bus to be used by Stride’s clients.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (1 year lease/$1.00
revenue to city). (#5497)

CONSIDERATION OF BIDS:
Consideration o f Bi d #M -12-10 f or a co ntract b etween t he C ity o f Ch eyenne an d A ztec
Construction Co., Inc., of Cheyenne, for the Transfer Station Fire Main Loop Project (State Loan &
Investment Board).  M r. Valdez moved to accept the bid in an amount not to exceed $113,724.00,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, explained that use of the
miscellaneous force account would allow immediate action, if necessary, for any unknown problems
that may occur during the project.  Concerns were expressed regarding completion of other projects on
the SLIB list.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Elias explained that projects for solid waste use would go unfinished
if this is paid for out of the solid waste fund.  He further advised this project is part of the single stream
recycling project that was approved under SLIB funding, and provided information on project costs and
funding. Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#5498)
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-3-10 for one new 2011 cab & chassis truck with dump body, spreader,
snow plow and tarpaulin from Peterbilt of Wyoming for the Street and Alley Department (2003-2006
& 2007-2010 1 % S ales T ax).  M otion ( per Consent A genda) c arried ( approved in the a mount of
$171,952.00). 
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-5-10 for two 2010 4x4 Utility Vehicles from Greiner Ford, Casper,
WY for t he Pu blic W orks D ept a nd C onstruction En gineering D ept. (2 007-2010 1 % S ales Ta x).
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried (approved in the amount of $39,304.00).
[CA] Consideration of Bid #R-7-10 for furnishing and installing one new single ram baler and one
new roller c hain c onveyor from JDM Eq uipment, L LC o f Arvada, C O for the Tra nsfer S tation --
referred to Finance Committee.
APP OINTM ENT S:
 Appointment of Philip Griggs to the Public Transit Advisory Board and Kurt Zunker to the Fire
Civil Service Commission.  Mr. Collins moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Motion carried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not
vote (Mayoral appointments).
ANNOUNCEMENTS/ REP ORTS /MOTIONS :
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held January 11, 2010 at 6:00 p.m. in City
Council Chambers to determine whether compliance of annexation conditions required by Wyo. Stat.
§15-1-402 exists for a parcel of land being situated in the  SW ¼ NW ¼ of Section 21 T. 14N., R.
66W., of the 6  P. M., Laramie County, WY (located south of Plain View Road, west of Point Bluff,th
and north of Apache Street).
Announcement was made of the appointment of Brian N. Kozak as Chief of Police for the City
of Cheyenne, to be confirmed January 11, 2010.
OTHER CIT Y BUSINESS:
Claus Johnson, Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA) President, commended Mr.
Harris for coming forth with the lease agreement of the Pride Park Softball Complex, but advised he
does not support the agreement that went through tonight with Post 1881 due t o the city’s financial
concerns.  He advised he feels employees should come first and this could have saved the city $20,000.
He no ted he w as speaking on behalf of c o-workers, stated he d oesn’t feel e mployees c reated t he
financial situation the city is in, but they are part of it, and commented that employees should have been
made a ware of the situation sooner a nd g iven a n op portunity to p rovide input.  U pon inquiry for
clarification on cost saving ideas for the city,  Mr. Johnson provided some cost saving measures the
Parks and Recreation Department has implemented, and suggested a change to the city vehicle take-
home policy. He advised CPEA is in opposition to employees losing longevity pay.
Also s peaking in re gard t o t he C ity’s financial s ituation, Stev e Pe nnock, Sa nitation B oard
representative, discussed cost saving measures that have already been implemented in the Sanitation
Department.  He advised they are willing to do their part, but expressed concerns with loss of longevity
pay. Hawk Leal, Sanitation Department employee, also expressed concerns with losing longevity pay,
stating f urlough d ays a re u nderstandable, a nd p rovided s uggestions f or cost saving m easures f or
Sanitation Department operations. He also expressed concerns over citizens lack of participation in
Sanitation Department programs.  John Palmer, Sanitation Department employee, expressed concerns
on reducing employees pay and discussed wages for Sanitation Department employees.  He  requested
the City look at cutting projects before cutting pay.
Upon inquiry regarding clarification of certain part-time employees in the Public Works Dept.
and Treasurer’s Office, Jim Elias, Public Works Director, explained that the Department is looking at
combining and/or eliminating part-time positions.  Mayor Kaysen explained the part-time position in
the T reasurer’s o ffice had been a  full-time position a nd c hanging t o p art-time status w ill r esult in
savings to the city.
Mayor Kaysen addressed the city employee vehicle policy and clarified department heads had
been asked to evaluate and assess all employees who have vehicles assigned with regard to  “on call”
responsibilities, discussed proposed longevity and deferred compensation reductions, and encouraged
employees to bring cost saving ideas and concerns to him. 
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting was officially
adjourned at 10:05 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 
 
 Kristy B. Anderson, CMC
 Deputy City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

MEETING: Sine Die Meeting | DATE: 2009-01-05 | CLIP_ID: 63 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 63

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
January 5, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in “Sine Die” session at 6:00 p.m. in the
Cheyenne Civic Center. Those present were: MAYOR - Jack R. Spiker; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Mr.
Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Segrave and Mr. Valdez.
Absent: Mr. Laybourn.  Also present were Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Bob Nicholas, Acting City
Attorney. 
Following the pledge of allegiance, Mayor Spiker welcomed those in attendance and stated the
purpose of the session was to c eremonially recognize outgoing Council members for their years of
service on City Council.  He commented generally on governing body and staff achievements a nd
community leadership, and expressed appreciation to outgoing Council members for their time, effort
and dedication to the community, and recognized the support role of families, friends and co-workers.
At Mayor Spiker’s request, City Clerk Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety
honoring o utgoing Co uncil m ember Mr.  Segrav e en titled as  f ollows:  “ A CEREMON IAL
RESOLUTION IN  R ECOGNITION O F TH E H ONORABLE TO M SEG RAVE F OR H IS
DEDICATION AND OUTSTANDING CONTRIBUTIONS TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND
ITS CITIZENS.”  Mayor Spiker presented a framed copy of the resolution to Mr. Segrave and an item
of appreciation on behalf of City Council.  (#5093)
Mayor Spiker expressed appreciation on behalf of the governing body to Mr. Pierson, immediate
past president of City Council, and presented Mr. Pierson with a gavel plaque of appreciation.
Mayor Spiker relinquished duties of the chair to Council President Pierson.  At Council President
Pierson’s request, City Clerk Intlekofer read a ceremonial resolution in its entirety honoring Mayor
Spiker en titled as  f ollows: “ A CER EMONIAL RESOLU TION I N R ECOGNITION OF  T HE
HONORABLE J ACK R. SP IKER F OR HIS DEDI CATION A ND OUTST ANDING
CONTRIBUTIONS TO THE CITY OF CHEYENNE AND ITS CITIZENS.” Council President Pierson
presented Mayor Spiker with an item of appreciation on behalf of City Council.  Following conclusion
of the presentation, Council President Pierson returned duties of the Chair to Mayor Spiker. (#5094)
Mayor Spiker announced the first regular meeting of the new governing body would convene
at 6:30 p.m. in the Civic Center.  There being no further business for the Sine Die session, Mayor Spiker
adjourned the meeting at 6:15 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
PUBLISH: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 10, 2009

MEETING: First Regular Meeting | DATE: 2009-01-05 | CLIP_ID: 64 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 64

   RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
 
Office of City Clerk
 
January 5, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne assembled for its first regular 2009 meeting on
this date at 6:30 p.m. in the Cheyenne Civic Center.  Purpose of the first regular meeting was to
recognize and administer oaths of office to those individuals who were elected, or re-elected, at the
most r ecent g eneral e lection t o s erve a s mu nicipal o fficials, a s w ell a s p rovide f or c ertain
administrative functions.  Ms. Mona Pearl served as mistress of ceremonies prior to the meeting being
officially convened.
Posting of Colors was provided by members of the Wyoming National Guard Joint Color
Guard, and a musical selection was performed by Ms. Patrice Pilivant with assistance by members of
Hands in Harmony.  Father Gary Ruzicka, St. Mary’s Cathedral, delivered the invocation.
The Honorable William U. Hill, Justice of the Wyoming Supreme Court, administered the oath
of elected office to Mayor-elect Richard L. Kaysen,  followed by administration of oaths of elected
office for the following newly elected and re-elected City Council members:  Amber Ash and Jimmy
Valdez, Ward I; Patrick Collins and Jack Spiker, Ward II, and Jim Brown and Don Pierson, Ward
III.
Mayor Kaysen officially called the first regular meeting to order at 6:50 p.m.  Those present
were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles,
Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Also present were Carol
Intlekofer, City Clerk, and Bob Nicholas, Acting City Attorney.  Following the pledge of allegiance,
Mayor Kaysen welcomed those present and provided comments on the community’s progress and
his vision for the City’s future.
ELECTION OF  CO UNCIL PRESI DENT F OR 2009.   M ayor Kaysen an nounced t hat
pursuant to City code, he would not be participating in voting for the election of Council President
and Vice President officers.  Mr. Brown moved to nominate and elect Mr. Collins as City Council
President for 2009, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Dr. Rinne moved that nominations cease, seconded
by Mr. Valdez.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no” and Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Motion to
nominate and elect Mr. Collins carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
ELECTION OF COUNCIL VICE PRESIDENT FOR 2009.  Mr. Pierson moved to nominate
and elect Ms. Broyles, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Dr. Rinne moved that nominations cease, seconded
by Mr. Pierson.  Motion to cease nominations carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing
body with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no” and Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.  Motion to
nominate and elect Ms. Broyles carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body with the
exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote.
APPROVAL OF APPOINTMENT OF CITY OFFICIALS (pursuant to Title 2 of the Code
of the City of Cheyenne) as follows: Guy E. Cameron, Chief of Fire and Rescue Department; Barbara
E. Dorr,  City Treasurer; Robert D. Fecht, Chief of Police Department; Paul S. Galeotos, Municipal
Court Judge; Randy K. Hays, Assistant City Treasurer; Carol A. Intlekofer, City Clerk/Assistant City
Treasurer; Ronnald J. Jeffrey, Juvenile Court Judge; Mark T. Moran, Municipal Court Judge; Rick
L. Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, and Daniel E. White, City Attorney.  Mr. Collins moved
to approve the appointments “en masse”, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Motion  carried.  Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body with the exception of Mayor Kaysen who did not vote (mayoral
appointments).  Justice Hill administered oaths of appointment to the above listed city officials.
Following a musical selection performed by Dr. Joe Harrell, with assistance by members of
Hands in Harmony, City Clerk Intlekofer announced that refreshments would be served in the Civic
Center lobby immediately following the meeting.
There being no further business for t he first r egular meeting, t he meeting w as o fficially
adjourned at 7:15 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 January 10, 2009

inquiry, Dan White, City Attorney, advised he had reviewed Robert’s Rules of Order pertaining
to whether pending business left over upon adjournment of a previous City Council is resumed at
the next meeting of City Council, and stated the language does not apply to City Council as it is
not a legislative assembly or a board, but that it is somewhat unique as it has both legislative and
executive functions.  He cited a past Kentucky Supreme Court ruling that ruled on t he
importance of  a  C ity C ouncil to a ct a s a  c ontinuous b ody to b e a ble to w ork on  un finished
business a nd t o c onduct it s b usiness expeditiously, a nd fu rther n oted in formation fo und in

expeditiously.  M r. White o pined t hat t he City Council is permitted t o p roceed w ith all o f t he
items on the meeting agenda.  C omments followed on the City’s adoption of Robert’s Rules of
Order, an d Ci ty f ire an d resc ue res ponse t o t he pro posed an nexation a rea. Motion carri ed.

officially adjourned at 8:14 p.m.

standard 60 ft. width co ntained i n t he curren t Ro ad, S treet an d Si te, P lanning an d Des ign
Standards.  Ms. Yenco  advised the final plat had been reviewed and adopted before but the time
frame for filing the plat had expired, and that the previous plat reflected a ro adway width of 50
ft.  Discussion followed on including an additional 5 ft. of right-of-way on each side of the street
to ensure t he r oadway w ould meet future w idth compliance.  M r. P ierson moved t o a mend by
modifying the 50 ft. roadway width to 60 ft. with 5 ft. of right-of-way added to each side of the
roadway, s econded b y M r. C ollins.  U pon in quiry, M s. Y enco s tated if t he a mendment is
approved it will not affect adjacent properties until those properties are developed at which time
the additional right-of-way for roadway width would be required.  Mo tion to amend to increase
the roadway width to 60 ft. (additional 5 ft. of right-of-way on each side of the roadway) carried.
Voting “y es” –  a ll m embers o f t he g overning bo dy.  U pon in quiry, Ms. Y enco a dvised t he

“AN OR DINANCE CREATI NG SECTI ON 10.24.170, DI STRACTIVE DRI VING
PROHIBITED; EXCEPTIONS, OF CHAPTER 10.24, RULES OF THE ROAD GENERALLY,
OF TITLE 10, VEHICLES AND TRAFFIC, OF THE CODE OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE,
WYOMING.”  John Fafoutakis and Ronald Nickle expressed concerns with how comprehensive
the ordina nce will be and law e nforcement conc erns; ty pes of dr iver distrac tions; l egislating
citizens’ freedom and rights; message texting, and responsibility of drivers for alertness and safe
driving habits. Mr. Br own moved to appr ove on sec ond reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.   Mr.
Pierson moved to amend by  substitute ordinance, seconded by Mr. Collins.  I t was advised the
substitute removes eating and drinking from the list of distracti on types on page 1 and r emoves
law enforcement personnel from the exemption s.  Mr. Pierson moved to ame nd Section A, Item
5, of the substitute ordinance  to add lang uage after “Observing a television or video display” by
inserting OTHER THAN  GPS NAVI GATION SYSTEMS”, seconde d by  Mr. Collins. M r.
Fafoutakis su ggested a  fu rther a mendment t o Se ction A  to i nclude no n-alcoholic be verages.
Chief Bob Fecht, Police Dept., spoke in support of the amendment, stating the department has a
policy relative to office rs and ope rating in-car computers while dri ving, and explained offe nses
cited under the ordinanc e would constitute a se condary offense, not primary  offense, meaning
there must be other proba ble cause(s) or r eason to pull a drive r over.  Discussion included the
following topics:  GPS  sy stems, seconda ry offe nse violation, and vari ous types of dr iver
distractions.  U pon inquiry for the City Clerk’s record, it w as clarified the proposed substitute
ordinance on the floor was the version dated Feb. 20, 2009 by the City Attorney staff.  Motion to
amend Section A, Item 5 of the substitute car ried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning
body. U pon in quiry re garding GPS navigation s ystems, Mr . N ickle dis cussed B luetooth
communication technolog y rela tive to GPS. Dis cussion continued on other  possible driver
distractions n ot s et o ut w ithin t he o rdinance, d efinition o f d istracted d river, a nd t he p hrase
“including, but not limited to, the following” in Section A.  Mr. B rown moved to further amend
the substitute by striking the word “computers” in Section A, Item #1, seconded by Ms. Broyles.
Upon inq uiry, Ch ief Fecht advised s ince the  ins tallation o f i n-car c ameras a nd c omputers,
officers’ operational accident rates has not chang ed.  Discussion followed on the  purpose of the
proposed amendment, and distrac tions co nstituting a secondary offense.  Mr. B rown withdrew
his motion to amend with conc urrence by Ms. Broyles as second to the motion.  Mayor Kaysen
clarified the motion to amend had been w ithdrawn.  Mr. Collins moved to further a mend the
substitute by striking the phrase “including, but not limited to, the foll owing:” and Items 1, 2, 3,
4 and 5 under Section A, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Discussion on the amendment involved the
overall p urpose of  the  or dinance, w hich is to a void i ndividuals f rom be ing dis tracted w hile
driving. Mr. Brown raised a point of clarification questioning whether the proposed amendment,
if approved, would also requir e an a mendment t o Section F, def inition of terms.  Mr. Colli ns
modified his previous motion to amend, with concurrence by Dr. Rinne as second to the motion,
as follows: Strike the wording “including, but not limited to, the following:” and delete Items 1
through 5 in Section A, and remove Items 1, 2, 3, 5, and 6 in Section F.  Brian McNutt,  member
of Laramie County Amateur  Radio Emerg ency Service s and Amate ur Radio Civil  Emergency
Services (ham ra dio opera tors), expressed c oncerns the ordin ance may pose re garding
emergency reporti ng (i .e. s potters re porting se vere we ather a nd a ssisting wi th c ommunity
emergency and disaster  communications) provide d by members to loca l public safety  agencies
and the Na tional W eather Service. He r equested hand held r adios be e xcluded f rom the
ordinance.  Upon inquiry, Chief Fecht reviewed evidentiary and prosecution issues involving law
enforcement if the ordinance were implemented.  Mo tion to amend carried.  Vo ting “yes” – a ll
members of the governing body.  Upon inquiry regarding the term “section” as used in Section D
(i.e. No motor vehicle shall be halted solely for a violation of this section), Bob Mason, Assistant
City Attorney, advised the word could be changed to “ordinance.”  Mr. Pierson moved to further
amend the substitute ordi nance by chang ing the  word “ section” in Sec tion D to “ordinanc e,”
seconded by  Mr. B rown.  Motion to amend ca rried.  Voting “ yes” – a ll members of the
governing body. Ms. Ash moved to further amend the substitute by striking the phrase “As used
in this section, the following terms are defined:” under Section F a nd the number 4, le aving the
definition of Distrac ted Drive r as the only  wording  under Sec tion F, se conded by Dr. Rinne.
Upon inq uiry, A ssistant A ttorney Ma son s uggested le aving the  int roductory ph rase wi thin
Section F for any future clarifications or definitions.  Ms.  Ash modified her previous motion to
amend, w ith c oncurrence b y D r. Ri nne a s s econd to  th e mo tion, b y le aving th e in troductory
phrase in Section F as it exists and renumbering Item 4, definition of distracted driver, to Item 1
under Section F.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
Motion to amend by substitute ordinance as amended (four amendments) carried. Voting “yes” –
all members of  the g overning body .  Main motion a s amended by  substitut e (a s amende d)

item involving re-appropriation of the budget and prioritizing. Upon inquiry regarding placement

opposition to t he pr oposed e xpenditure.  D iscussion in cluded c reation of the A IPP pr ogram,
Committee role and program funding fo r artwork acquisitions.  U pon inquiry, Barb Dorr, City
Treasurer, e xplained th e b udget f und s pecific to  th e A IPP p rogram, w hich is  n ot a  g eneral
operating fund line item, and explained how transf ers into the AI PP fund are made  (i.e. budg et
re-appropriation) following approval of the annua l fiscal y ear budget.  Comments in support of
the proposal and the AIPP program were made.  Motion ca rried.  Voting “yes” – all members of
the governing body with the exception of Dr. Rinne voting “no”.

“AN O RDINANCE CR EATING SECTI ON 10.24.170, DI STRACTIVE DRI VING
PROHIBITED; EXCEPTIONS, OF CHAPTER 10.24, RULES OF THE ROAD GENERALLY, OF
TITLE 10, VEHI CLES AND TRAF FIC, OF TH E CODE OF THE CI TY OF  CHEYENNE,
WYOMING.”   Re porting for Public Service s Committee, Mr. B rown moved to a pprove on third
and final reading, seconded by Mr. Collins.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded by
Mr. Pierson.  It was advised the proposed substitute adds the phrase “including cell phone usage” at
the e nd of  Se ction A; re organizes a nd re letters/renumbers se veral se ctions, a nd pr ovides th at a
violation would constitut e a secondary, not primary , offe nse.  Discussion included the f ollowing
topics: I nclusion of the ce ll phone usag e phra se, hands- free cell phone usag e, ty pes of dr iving
distractions, o riginal in tent o f t he or dinance, p rimary vs . s econdary of fense sta tus fo r v iolations,
and texting as a  form of dr iver distraction.  Upon inquiry, Bob Fecht, Police Chief, explained the
difference betwee n a primar y and sec ondary offe nse and f actors involved in an offic er
stopping/pulling over a dr iver, the c itation issuance proc ess, and e vidence officers must determine
is sufficient to issue a c itation for a se condary violation.  Moti on to amend by  substitut e fa iled.
Voting “yes”– Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne.  V oting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms.
Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Brown moved to amend by
substitute, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  It was noted the proposed substitute remove s Section D. (i.e.
“No motor vehicle shall be halted solely for a violation of this ordinance”), and reletters the certain
ordinance se ctions.  I t w as c larified th e su bstitute or dinance c hanges the vio lation p enalty to
constitute a p rimary o ffense.  Di scussion fo llowed o n s upport/non-support o f a v iolation b eing a
primary offe nse; ordinanc e sub-se ction C. 2. wording  that excludes person s reporting a n
emergency; vehicle accident statistics involving cell phones used by drivers; hands-free devices and
cost of same , and drive r distrac tion scenar ios.  Upon inquiry  reg arding sub-sec tion C . 2., Dan
White, City Attorney, stated his interpretation of the sub- section means that a per son contacting an
emergency services agency to inform that agency of an emergency would be exempt from the entire
section.  Upon inqui ry, Chief Fe cht re viewed of ficer discre tionary authority  rela tive to citing
distracted dr ivers, and c ommented on th e careless driving  ordinanc e that is curr ently in place.
Further discussion followed on c ell phon e usage; hands-f ree cell phone de vices; sug gestion of
removing ordinanc e Section A.; driving  di straction scenar ios (inside/outside a vehicle) ; office r
discretion in issuing citations; primary  vs. seconda ry offense status, and notifica tion of ordinance
requirements to the public and temporary visitors to the community.  During discussion, a point of
order was raised involving comments being made with Mayor Kaysen advising comments need to
remain on topic.  Mr. Spiker moved to postpone for 2 w eeks (to March 23, 2009), seconded by Mr.
Collins. Br ief discussion followed r egarding action on the most previous motio n to amend by
substitute, r elative to support or  no n-support f or the  su bstitute, p rior to a ction o n th e mot ion to
postpone.  Mr. Spiker withdrew his motion t o postpone with concur rence by Mr. Collins as second
to the motion.  Upon inquiry, Mr. White clarified that if an individual were using a hands-free cell
phone device and is invol ved in an  accident, theoretically it could be conc luded by an officer that
the driver  was still dist racted  pursuant to wor ding c ontained in Section A. of  the proposed
substitute, which states that no person shall operate a motor vehicle while the attention of the driver
is distracted from the safe operation of the vehicle.  Ms. Broyles moved to amend Sec tion B. of the
substitute by ending the sentence after the word “vehicle” and striking the remaining phrase “unless
the telephone  is sp ecifically desig ned to allow hands- free oper ation and is used in a ha nds-free
manner”.  Motion died due to the lack of a second to the motion.  M otion to amend by substitute
carried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the  governing body with the exception of Ms. Ash, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne and Mr. Valdez voting “no”.  M r. Spi ker moved to postpone for 2 weeks (to
March 23, 2009), sec onded by  Mr. Collins. Brief discussion follow ed on the purpose of
postponement and the approved motion to amend by  substitute, which ma kes usage of c ell phones
and oth er driving dis tractions wh ile op erating a  mot or ve hicle a  pr imary of fense.  M otion to
postpone for 2 w eeks c arried.  Vo ting “yes” – all member s of the g overning body  with the
exception of Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Dr. Rinne voting “no”.

“A RESOL UTION RATI FYING THE L ISTING OF  CHEYENNE’ S MOORE HAVEN
HEIGHTS HI STORIC DI STRICT TO THE NA TIONAL REGI STER OF HI STORIC PL ACES
AND AU THORIZING THE A DDITION OF  THE PROP ERTIES CONTAI NED THE REIN TO
THE CITY’S INVENTORY OF HISTORIC STRUCTURES.” Chuck Lanham, Cheyenne Planning
Office/Historic Pr eservation, pr ovided in formation o n th e pu rpose of the  re solution, w hich is  to
ratify the listing of the Moor e Haven Heights structures to t he National Register, and to add the
actual listing of properties to the City ’s inventory of historic s tructures.  Mr. L anham advised there
were some property owners who requested their respective properties be withdrawn from the listing
prior to establishment of the district, and that these would be considered non-contributing structures
without a ffecting the  re st o f t he str uctures w ithin t he dis trict. He sta ted o nce the  dis trict is
established by the National Par ks Service, it is hard to remove properties from the listing.  Mr.
Lanham discusse d the district survey  that was c onducted; explain ed property require ments to be
included in an historic district; clarified there are no federal, state or city restrictions other than City
Code addre ssing demolishing a  structure  within an historic district (i.e. demolition permits);
commented that properties within an hist oric district tend to maintain its value long er tha n
properties in non-historic districts, and noted that sometimes neighborhood residents form their own
association to uphold the historic integr ity of the a rea. Upon inquiry , Mr. L anham pr ovided
information on notification efforts to property owners to advise of public mee tings pertaining to the
proposed h istoric dis trict de signation.  Mr . Lanham a nd City At torney Da n Wh ite re viewed Ci ty
Code demolition application, review  and per mitting procedures, including the a ppeal process, and
zoning re strictions.  Matt Ashby , Urba n Planning Dir ector, provid ed further information on the
National Reg ister desig nation, stating it does not come  with restrictions f rom the fede ral
government but that local jurisdictions have authority to apply additional restrictions on h istoric
districts, such as the City ’s demolition permit process, a nd that other e lements can be implemented
by City Code to provide property  protections.  Upon inquiry , Mr. L anham commented on positive
aspects to property owners who have structures included in an historic district and a previous grant
request approved by the governing body that assisted with funding to conduct the property survey,
and advise d communica tion with the Building  Dept. would be ma de involving a ny unsafe
structures.  Speaking  in favor of  the re solution were:  Kara Hahn, National Reg ister Prog ram
Coordinator, Wy oming Sta te Hi storic Pr eservation o ffice; B etsy B radley, St ate Hi storic
Preservation office  and prope rty owner  within t he proposed district; J eanie Tucke r, Rainsford
Historic District re sident, and Glen G arrett, proper ty owner  within the proposed district .  Topics
presented and discusse d included: Letters sent to pr operty owners in the proposed district advising
of the intent to form an historic district; benefits of being a property owner wit hin an  hi storic
district; grant e ligibility to o wners if  the ir str ucture is l isted o n t he National Re gister o f Historic
Places; en couragement f or c reation o f m ore dis tricts w ithin t he c ommunity; pr operty va lues;
owners’ re sponsibility to understand their  role a nd maintain their prope rty within a district, and
designation of districts providing for a community neighborhood identity.  Mr. Brown advised there
was no recommendation on the item from Public Services Committee. Mr. Brown moved to adopt,
seconded b y M rs. C ase. D iscussion i ncluded c ontent o f t he l etter o f n otification s ent t o pr operty
owners by the Planning of fice; concerns with demolition permit applications  relative to previous
requests involv ing the his toric Gove rnors’ Ma nsion protective a rea; curre nt condition and
maintenance of rental structures located w ithin the proposed district; property  owners’ rights; and
future po tential tr ansfers of  pr operty ownership.  F ollowing a  10  min ute re cess t o a llow t ime to
photocopy and distribute the letter  the Planning of fice mailed to p roperty owner s reg arding
notification of the de velopment of the propose d district, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting  at
8:00 p .m. Up on inq uiry, M r. L anham pr ovided in formation c oncerning re modeling of  str uctures,
adding on  a dditions, e tc.  D iscussion f ollowed o n c ontent o f t he l etter sent to pr operty ow ners;
positive imp act th e dis trict w ould h ave on neighbors;  pr ocedures f or de molishing pr operties;
gentrification and prese rvation of historic buildings a nd neighborhoods; property rights, and future
property ow ner n otification le tters c ontaining mor e de tailed in formation o n p roposed h istoric
district formation.  Motion carr ied.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body with the
exception of Mr. Brown, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker voting “no”.  (#5106)

restaurant, 1651 Carey  Ave. #8.  City  Clerk Carol I ntlekofer offe red a  sug gestion that in lieu of

renewal applications and posting of notices on respective licensee premises have been achieved and
the holding of a public he aring before the g overning body has bee n conducted.  She further  stated
that the Cit y Attorney has advised tha t this st atement on the r ecord provides suff icient
documentation to identify those individual renewal applications that may be acted upon at the
meeting.  Upon q uery by Mayor Kaysen to members of the governing body, there was no objection
voiced to re cognize the rene wal applicants as lis ted on the agenda in lieu of  the Clerk re ading the
individual applicants.  John Rogers, attorney representing HEJE, Inc., advised the applicant will not
be in a position to  be open at their new business location by  April 1, 2009, pursuant to the c urrent
license’s status as a “pa rked” license, a nd requested approval of the renewal with an extension of
the parked status for an additional 6 weeks to allow the applicant time to become operational at its
new business site.  He referenced a recent letter he had submitted (on f ile with office of City Clerk)
explaining the reason for requesting extension of the parked status, and requested consideration of a
2 w eek p ostponement t o p rovide for any  f urther in formation d esired to  b e p resented a t t he n ext
Finance Committee meeting.  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to approve the

question to allow for a  separ ate motion on the microbre wery renewa l permit application for
Shadows Br ewing Co., L LC, seconded by  Mr. C ollins.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve the
microbrewery rene wal perm it continge nt upon the City  Clerk re ceiving verific ation from the
Wyoming Liquor Division no later tha n 5:00 p.m. on March 31, 2009 that the sta tutory minimum
production quantity required by state statutes has been met.  M ayor Kaysen advised the motion to
divide must be acte d upon prior to  the motion to approve.  Upon inquiry , Bob Ma son, Assistant
City Attorney, clarified Shadows Brewing should be able to meet statutory requirements regarding
brewing pr oduction quantity  require ments by  Marc h 31, 2009. Motion to di vide the question
carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.  Dr. Rinne mo ved to further divide the
question to allow for a separate motion on the retail renewal application for HEJE, Inc., seconde d
by Mr. Valde z.  Mot ion carried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning body.  Mr. Valde z
moved to approve Shadows Brewing Co., LLC microbrewery permit renewal application contingent
upon the City  Clerk re ceiving verification from the Wy oming Liquor Divi sion no later than 5:00
p.m. on March 31, 2009 t hat the statutory  minimum production quantity  required by state statutes
has been met, seconde d by Mr. Spik er.  Tim Mangum, Wyoming Liquor Division, confirmed he
had spoken  with the owner  of Shadows B rewing about the production re quirement, and clarified
they should be able to meet the statutory requirement by the deadline of March 31, 2009. He briefly
reviewed state re quirements re garding the  a mount o f p roduct th at mu st b e br ewed a nnually to
qualify for perm it renew al, and noted e quipment brea kdown problems enc ountered by  Shadows
Brewing and their e fforts to meet bre wing requirements.  Motion to approve conting ent upon the
Clerk receiving verification from the L iquor Division carr ied.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the
governing body.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve the renewal application for HEJE, Inc., seconded by
Mr. Collins.  Mr . Collins moved to postpone action on the HEJE, Inc. application for 2 w eeks (to
March 23, 2009), seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Rogers clarified his prior comments
by stating he was requesting approval for continuance of the license’s parked status for 6 weeks,
and stated a 2 week postponement will allow for further discussion if needed at Finance Committee.
Upon inquiry regarding the time require ment in which the license  must be operat ional, Mr. Rogers
explained reasons for the a pplicant’s dela y in opening  a pac kage store a t their new loc ation
involved a 2 month time period in which they  wer e wa iting for  approval from the Boar d of
Adjustment.  Following discussion, Mr. Collins withdrew his motion to postpone with concurrence
by Ms. Broyles as second to the motion. City Clerk Intlekofer noted for  clarification that during a
conversation with Mr. Roge rs s he had sug gested a  2 wee k postponement to allow time for the
Council to receive any additional information on the parked status request.  Mr . Pierson moved to
amend the motion to approve the renewal application for HEJE, Inc. to also extend the parked status
of the lice nse to no  later tha n J uly 1, 2009, seconde d by  Mr. Collins. Dis cussion followed on
progress by the applicant to open the business at its new site, a nd licensee plans to be open a nd
operational in mid-May.  Motion to amend car ried.  Voting  “yes” – a ll members of the g overning
body.  Main motion to approve HEJE, I nc. re tail rene wal application as a mended to extend the
parked status to J uly 1, 2009 car ried.  Voting “ yes” – all members of  the g overning body. Upon
inquiry regarding status of th e pending On the Border restaurant (bar and grill licensee), City Clerk
Intlekofer advised it was he r understanding the license e has ha d some zoning issues re garding the
proposed business loca tion, and provided infor mation on  the par ked status time fra me for the
license.  U pon inquiry , Police Chief B ob Fe cht re viewed c ompliance c riteria f or Applebe es and
clarified they have been in f ull compliance since  an incident that oc curred last year.  Main motion
to renew the remaining renewal licenses “en ma sse” carried.  Voting  “yes” –  a ll members of the
governing body.

justifying cell phone use while driving as a primary offense; use of cellular phones while driving for
emergency, b usiness, me dical, e mployment, e tc. p urposes; c urrent c areless d riving o rdinance in
place; am endment s uggestions; dri vers’ f reedom o f ch oice; support f or t he o rdinance; wri tten
scientific research results by the Psychology Dept., University of Utah, on cell phone usage and risk
factors/accidents (i nformation s ubmitted a nd on  f ile w ith of fice of  C ity C lerk), a nd th e c itation
issuance process.  Upon inquiry, Police Chief Bob Fecht provided his definition of the t erm “legally
parked”, di scussed o fficer di scretionary authority, an d ad vised t he b ond sch edule f or t he o ffense
would b e s et b y t he M unicipal Co urt J udge and fines assessed co uld b e a m aximum o f $750. 00
and/or 6 mos. in jail.  U pon further inquiry, Chief Fecht explained the citizen arrest (witness based)
process a nd p roof of  evidence. D iscussion f ollowed on  th e i ntent of  th e or dinance, w hich i s to
provide saf e dr iving p ractices; pr imary v s. seco ndary o ffense st atus; use o f h ands f ree devi ces;
research o n c ell p hone d river u sage a s a  d istraction; d istractive d riving l egislation, a nd
constitutional issues.  M otion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body
present with the exception o f Mr. Pierson voting “no”.  F or t he reco rd, Mr. Pierson read a l etter
from a citizen opposing the ordinance who was unable to b e present at the meeting.  D iscussion on
the m ain m otion a s a mended i ncluded t he f ollowing t opics: S upport/non-support f or s econdary
offense vs. primary offense status for violations; hindering law enforcement investigative operations
if t he o rdinance passes as  a primary o ffense for v iolations; supp ort for use o f h ands-free devices,
and curr ent carel ess dri ving l aws.  Main m otion as  am ended b y s ubstitute f ailed (Cl erk’s n ote:
Approval of ordinances on final reading require a m inimum of six affirmative votes).  Vo ting “yes”
–  M s. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins and Mr. S piker.  V oting “no” – Ms. Case, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  U nder oth er City business later in the meeting, comments
were made concerning possible reconsideration of action on the ordinance at the next City Council
meeting and amending t he o ffense for violations from a primary t o secondary status.  M r. Brown,
sponsor o f t he o rdinance, stated a s t he p urpose o f t he o rdinance was t o p rovide for safety o f t he
public, he would be against changing a violation from a primary to secondary offense.
Following a brief recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:15 p.m.

MEETING: Cheyenne City Council Special Session | DATE: 2009-04-14 | CLIP_ID: 86 | SOURCE: cheyenne.granicus.com MinutesViewer clip 86

 RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
April 14, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in special session beginning at 12:15 p.m.
in Council Chambers.  Those present were: MAYOR - Richard L. Kaysen; COUNCIL MEMBERS -
Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Valdez.  Absent: Dr.
Rinne and Mr. Spiker. 
Also present were Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
Purpose of the special session was to take action on “Contract Modification No. 1 to Contract
No. 5127 between the City of Cheyenne and Wyoming Department of Transportation to include use
of American Recovery and Reinvestment Act of 2009 (ARRA) Federal Funds for the I-180 Project.”
Following the pledge of allegiance, Mr. Valdez, reporting for Finance Committee, moved to approve,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” - all members of the governing body present.
There b eing n o f urther b usiness prev iously noted for the s pecial s ession, M ayor K aysen
adjourned the meeting at 12:17 p.m.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.
Published: Wyoming Tribune-Eagle
 April 17, 2009

unanticipated pro blems encountered; loan amount requested and repayment t hrough pro gram user
revenues; p rograms p lanned t o be  o ffered t hrough t he f acility a nd affiliated c ost a reas, a nd
estimated t ime f rame f or l oan repay ment.  Upo n i nquiry, Ra y F ertig, F ive-R-Co., pro ject
contractor, rev iewed an ticipated completion time f rame f or P hase I,  s tating a f ew m inor i ssues

Center); p rioritization o f p rojects; u se o f 6  p enny s ales t ax t o a cquire t he p ark p roperty, a ndth

day re serve l evel f or t he C ity.  M s. B royles m oved t o p ostpone for 4 weeks, s econded b y M r.

“A RESOLUTION CERTIFYING THAT THE GOVERNING BODY OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE IS  IN A GREEMENT W ITH TH E LARAMIE CO UNTY BOA RD OF
COMMISSIONERS A S T O A  NEW  REQUES T T HROUGH O UR CO UNTYWIDE
CONSENSUS BLOCK GRANT APPLICATION TO THE STATE LOAN AND INVESTMENT
BOARD”-- referred to Finance Committee (sponsors - Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Spiker and Mr.
Valdez).  M r. Valdez advised the item had been discussed under other items for the agenda at the
most recent Finance Committee and provisions of Title 2 of City Code had been met.  M r. Valdez
moved to adopt, seconded by Ms. Broyles. Gene Burchett spoke in favor of the City’s partnering
with WYDOT to complete the West Lincolnway reconstruction project to specifically i nclude
landscaping median enhancements.  He referenced information contained in a handout he provided
to Council members (on file with office of City Clerk) and explained his reasons why he felt it was
important that the pro ject i nclude t he l andscaping el ements, as  pro posed b y t he M etropolitan
Transportation Organization (MPO), t o improve a nd beautify t he g ateway into t he City from W.
Lincolnway.  A lso speaking in support o f t he landscaping enhancements was Randy Bruns, CEO,
Cheyenne L EADS, w ho em phasized th e i mportance of  th e p roject w ith th e b eautification
enhancements, w hich w ill a ssist w ith o pportunities t o s howcase t he c ommunity fo r e conomic
development purposes.  He referenced federal stimulus funding that is available for the W YDOT
project; m entioned s upport f or t he l andscaping e lements i s a lso p rovided by  t he C heyenne
Chamber of Commerce and the Cheyenne Area Convention and Visitors Center, and expressed his
opinion that if the enhancements were not included in the project at this time it could be a “50 year
lost opportunity” to build and improve the community’s entryway.  C omments followed in support
of t he l andscaping e nhancements as well a s p otential a dditional f uture e nhancements, f unding
sources th at m ay b e a vailable f or project utilization b y th e C ity, a nd oth er p lanned c ommunity
improvement projects.  Ms. Ash moved to strike the phrase “ ... Pioneer and Carey  Reconstruction
Project” from the fifth Whereas clause o f the resolution and replace with the “ ... David Romero
teen center”, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Following a 10 minute recess to review the appropriateness
of the motion, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:00 p.m. and advised  the motion on the
floor was in order.  He clarified, with concurrence by Ms. Ash as maker of the motion, the motion
presented is t o modify t he funding identified for t he W. Lincolnway median enhancement pro ject
to r eflect c onsensus funds w ould g o tow ard th e D avid Romero Y outh Activity Center instead of
the Pioneer and Carey Reconstruction Project if a f unding package for the W. Lincolnway median
project were un able t o be completed. Upo n inquiry as to the purpo se o f t he o riginal 5  Whereasth
clause, Tom Mason, MPO Director, explained when the resolution was being prepared, there was
some un certainty o n the City’s ab ility t o fund t he median en hancements in its en tirety s o it was
determined t hat an alternative q ualifying p roject f or u se o f f unds w ould be  i ncluded i n t he
resolution i n cas e t he f unding s cenario di d no t come together.  He s tated t iming i s cri tical as
WYDOT is opening bids for the W. Lincolnway road project on May 7 .  He f urther advised thatth
since t he res olution was  prese nted, W YDOT h as i nformed t he C ity t hat i t wi ll h ave addi tional
stimulus m oney av ailable an d wi ll b e ab le t o co ntribute $1.0 m illion t oward t he m edian pro ject
with t he City’s portion now set at $800,000, and commented that it is hopeful that bids c ome in
maybe less, and hopefully not more, t han the engineer’s estimates.  M r. Mason discussed funding
sources for t he C ity’s s hare o f t he p roject.  Comment was made t hat t he Whereas c lause be ing
discussed was potentially a “moot” point and may not need to be included in the resolution.  Upon
inquiry, Do ug Vet ter, In terim City E ngineer, adv ised t he Pi oneer/Carey pro ject i s i n t he des ign
phase an d co uld b e un derfunded due t o ab ove gro und el ements, an d cl arified the Carey A ve.
extension pro ject i s di fferent from t he Pi oneer/Carey pro ject. M r. M ason adv ised co mpleted
landscaping in t he W. L incolnway median wo uld b e maintained by t he C ity, an d ex plained s ince
WYDOT is now constructing the fence project on I-180, the City no longer has to fund that project
and the resolution is to request that SLIB (State Loan and Investment Board) transfer the money
that h ad b een dedi cated t o t he C ity f or t he I- 180 pro ject t o t he W . L incolnway M edian
Enhancement P roject.  D iscussion f ollowed o n t he p ossibility o f u sing d edicated f unds f or t he
previous d owntown s kywalk p roject a nd o ther 5  p enny funds fo r t he median project.  I nterimth
Public Works Director Vicki Nemecek reviewed requirements for utilizing 5  penny funds and theth
approval for e xpenditure p rocess for p rojects, w hich must m eet th e p arameters of  th e 1 % b allot
question that voters approved.  Mayor Kaysen noted the timeliness of the resolution relative to

forward.  Pursuant to comment on possible use of SLIB (consensus) monies for the David Romero
Park Y outh Activity C enter p roject i n c onjunction w ith a nother p otential m otion t o a mend, a nd
upon inquiry, C ity At torney D an White r eviewed t he s tatus o f t he a mendment o n t he f loor a nd

order to e nsure d iscussion r emains on  p oint.  M s. A sh w ithdrew her m otion to a mend w ith

suggestion t hat a  C ouncil w ork s ession be  he ld t o r eview C ity p rojects and f unding l evels, a nd
positive co mmunity b enefits t he co mpleted Da vid Ro mero Yo uth A ctivity Ce nter wi ll pro vide.
Ms. Ash moved to amend t he resolution t o make the David R. Ro mero Park t he primary pro ject
outlined in the resolution, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Mr.  Mason advised if the money is redirected
to a nother p roject, l ocal funds r equired to j oin w ith W YDOT’s funding will h ave to c ome from

involved with the r esolution a nd a pproval of  p roject f unding tr ansferral p ursuant to  C ounty
Commissioners’ an d SLIB m eeting s chedules. Ci ndy Sch neider, Ci ty Gran ts M anager, pro vided

meeting the time frame for the upcoming SLIB meeting.  Discussion continued on funding options,

penny f unding av ailability f or i nfrastructure dev elopment, an d M att A shby, Urb an Pl anning,
suggested caution on the funding amount being discussed as WYDOT has not yet opened bids for
the project and it’s conceivable bids could come in higher than estimated.  Ms. Schneider provided
information concerning reallocation of consensus block grant money per SLIB requirements.  M r.
Pierson rai sed a po int o f o rder ques tioning wh at t he pro posed am endment en tailed.  F ollowing
comment on the intent of the motion to amend vs. the actual motion made, Ms. Ash withdrew her
motion to a mend with concurrence by Mr. Valdez as the second to t he motion.  Ms. Ash moved to
amend t he 4  Whe reas c lause i n t he r esolution by  s triking t he p hrase “ . .. I -180 E nhancementth
Bridge Section ...” a nd inserting “ ... David R. Romero South Side Community Park ...”, and by
striking the 5  Whereas clause in its entirety, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Discussion followed on theth
amendment; support for t he W. Lincolnway enhancement p roject as well as t he park project; and
funding concerns and alternatives.  M otion t o amend failed.  Vo ting “yes” – M s. Ash, Ms. Case,
Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr.
Rinne and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Collins moved to amend the 5  Whereas clause to read as follows:th
“Whereas, i f t he Ci ty an d W YDOT ar e A BLE to complete t he f unding p ackage f or t he W est
Lincolnway M edian E nhancement Pr oject T HROUGH O THER SOURC ES, t hen t he C onsensus
Funds would go to the DAVID R. ROMERO YOUTH ACTIVITY CENTER, seconded by M r.
Brown. Upon i nquiry, M r. Vet ter r eviewed t he es timated t ime f rame f or t he Pi oneer/Carey
Reconstruction P roject a nd p otential f unding s hortfall i ssues, a nd M s. S chneider r esponded w ith
information concerning t he p rocess for r eallocating c onsensus money. U pon inquiry, Rick P arish,
Parks an d Re creation Di rector, and M r. M ason adv ised t hey were un aware wh ether f ederal
stimulus dollars could be reallocated.  Mo tion to amend failed (due t o a t ie vote).  Vo ting “yes” –
Ms. Ash, Mr.  Br own, Mr.  Co llins, Mr.  S piker and Mr . Valdez. Voting “no” – Ms.  Br oyles, Ms.
Case, Mr.  P ierson, Dr . Ri nne an d May or K aysen. Ms. A sh m oved t o po stpone f or 4 we eks,
seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Donn Edmunds spoke in opposition to postponement, stating his belief
action should be t aken prior t o WYDOT’s bid o pening.  M r. Mason stated t here is a t ime line in

John F afoutakis co mmented o n t he pro posed po cket park t o b e l ocated i n do wntown
Cheyenne at the f ormer Ma ry’s Ba ke Sh op s ite rel ative t o h is o bservations regardi ng a s imilar
park constructed in downtown Houston, TX, which became attractive to undesirable persons a nd

business with Mayor Kaysen requesting Mr. Fafoutakis stay on point with his comments.  Mr.
Fafoutakis ex pressed opposition t o t he pro posed do wntown po cket park as  i t m ay creat e an

information co mmunicated t hrough t he m edia co ncerning t he s wine f lu, an d di scussed prev ious
history on  th e or iginal s wine flu a nd p roblems w ith p ast v accines.  M r. B rown r aised a  p oint of
order s tating c omments w ere n ot p ertinent.  M ayor K aysen e xplained City-County H ealth D ept.
officials are working closely with State health officials on monitoring the current flu issue.
Donn E dmunds ex pressed co ncerns wi th a d rainage ditch b uilt wes t o f W hitney Ro ad
relative t o s torm sewer d rainage issues and t he t ypes o f materials b eing c arried o r c ontained in
drainage flows.  H e d escribed p roblems w ith d rainage s urface a reas t hat a re c ausing p onds a nd
requested measures be taken to provide for drainage to continue onward in a southerly flow, or be
detained on City property, to avoid health and safety issues for persons living in the area.
 There b eing n o f urther b usiness to come b efore t he go verning b ody, t he m eeting was
officially adjourned at 9:46 p.m.

“A RESOL UTION AUT HORIZING THE MA YOR TO EXECUTE A  L ETTER OF
INTENT WI TH F IELD HOUSE DEV ELOPMENT GROUP, L LC, FOR PURPOS ES OF THE
DEVELOPMENT OF  AN I NDOOR SPORTS C OMPLEX I N THE CI TY OF CHEYENNE .”
Offering pu blic c omments we re John  F afoutakis, M ark Shu bert a nd Er ic Mil ler, wh o s poke in
opposition to t he re solution and urged c aution in p roceeding fo rward wi th a  fi eld h ouse
development.  To pics p resented incl uded: Field h ouse fa cility vs . r ecreation c enter a nd pu blic
sentiment e xpressed th rough a  ba llot e lection qu estion on  the  la tter; fu ture c osts a nd c oncerns,
including use of c ity-owned proper ty; field house proje ct proc ess proposed by  the deve loper;
utilization of current facilities and rental revenues; project financing; current economic conditions,
and pr ioritizing of  City pr ojects.  Da ve Z yzda, D irector of L easing, Lake B arrington (IL) F ield
House, Field House Development Group representative, explained the Group’s letter of intent will
provide them the opportunity to visit with local sports teams/groups to determine whether there is
support th rough c ommitment o f f acility r ental u se/fees to  c onsider d evelopment o f a n indoor
sports complex.  He stated if there is enough interest expressed then the Group may proceed with
discussions with the City with regard to a site involving donated land and land lease arrangements.
He pr ovided in formation o n p otential f ormation o f a  no n-profit c haritable or ganization; f acility
funding mechanism and debt payments; facility management time frame by the Group; property
7
(ground) lease, and eventual ownership of the fa cility by the City.  Dirk Miller, Chey enne Soccer
Association B oard of  Direc tors m ember, spoke in support of the re solution and clarifie d there
currently are not enough places in Cheyenne to rent for player use, noting that several local teams
rent facilities in Col orado f or use.  Comments followed on utiliz ation of City  soccer fields a nd
seasonal us e on ly of  City -owned o utdoor s occer a nd ba seball f ields.  Wa lly Erick son, pa st
president of  Cheyenne Socc er Club and pa rent/coach for soc cer teams, spoke in support of the
resolution to allow the Group an opportunity  to visit  with sports teams to determine the  demand
and feasibility of pursuing a facility.  He advised several facilities of this type are located in the Ft.
Collins, CO area, where competitive soccer programs lease the facilities to enable year round play
(i.e. in door tur f), e xplained te am ne eds a nd vo lumes a nd the  un availability of  fa cilities lo cally
through the community college and schools affecting player use.  Major Dorr spoke in support of
the letter of intent and giving youth a chance to compete and prove themselves.  Du ring public
comments, points of order were raised with Mayor Kaysen requesting speakers to remain on topic
with their comments, maintain respectfulness, and to o ffer comments, not enter into debate, with
other speakers.  Upon inquiry, Mayor Kaysen clarified the letter of intent authorizes the Group to
visit with sports teams and if, based on their findings, the Group determines a facility is f easible
for the community, then discussions will be held involving a definitive development agreement.
He state d any  final ag reement proposed will come be fore the governing body for r eview a nd
approval.  Mr. Valdez re ported the re was no re commendation fr om Financ e Committ ee on the
item.  Mr. Collins moved to adopt, seconded by Dr. Rinne.  Comments followed and included the
following topics: Shortage of space for sports leagues to practice; providing athletes with a facility
for y ear round use to build and enha nce a thletic skills and ma intaining a  competitive training
environment for players; aspects of partnering with the private sector to b uild a community field
house; lo cal c ommunity c ollege a nd sc hool polic ies a ffecting facility rental a vailability; e -mails
received by Council members in s upport of the letter of intent; possible land lease with the City
for a nominal amount, and non-commitment on the part of the City at this time.  During comments
and inquiries, Mr. Z yzda provided infor mation conce rning potential g round lea se/sales options
and financial bonding, and confirmed the letter of intent involves no obligation by the City.  Upon
inquiry reg arding potential parc els of City  land that could be utilized fo r a facility, Parks and
Recreation Direc tor Rick Parish stated par cels of la nd would need to be  revie wed f or
requirements, optio ns and availability  if it is decided the f easibility study  war rants moving
forward. Concerns were expressed with budg etary issues, other ong oing city projects, and contact
with ci tizens who have expressed a desire for a recreation center in the future instead of a field
house.  Motion car ried.  Voting  “y es” – a ll members of the g overning body present with the
exception of Ms. Broyles voting “no”. (#5135)

John F afoutakis c ommented on Council m embers de claring c onflicts o f i nterest r elative to

Eric Miller discussed adoption of the re solution earlier in the me eting pertaining to the letter
of intent with Field House  Deve lopment Group and, if a  fac ility is built , resp onsibilities by  the
parties if f inancial c ommitments could not  be met.  Pursuant to Mr. Miller’s c omment on

Mark Shubert commented on the city deficit, employee salary expenses and public vs. private
employment benefits.  May or Kaysen advised comparing salaries and employee benefits packages
between public and private sectors was not “apples to apples”, and expressed his appreciation of City
employees.
  
Mr. Collins, refer encing a memora ndum issued from the Metropolitan Planning  Office, and
indicating he had vote d with the preva iling side, m oved to re consider the Cooperative A greement
with the Wyoming Department of Transportation and the City for the West Lincolnway enhancement
project as approved at the May  11, 2009 City  Council me eting, seconded by Mr. Spiker. City  Clerk
Carol I ntlekofer r ead th e e ntitlement o f t he ite m pr oposed f or re consideration a s f ollows:
Cooperative A greement betwee n the Wyoming D epartment of Tra nsportation and the City  of
Cheyenne f or West L incolnway Enhanc ements betwe en Westland Road a nd Interstate 80 (2007-
2010 1% Sales Tax, SLIB Funding, and ARRA Funding ).  Comments followed on the purpose  of
reconsideration as, since approval of the item at the May 11, 2009 meeting, WYDOT has advised
they provided the City  with an incorr ect agreement and Exhibi t B whic h con tained an error  in the
amount of America Recovery and Reinvestment Act (ARRA federal economic stimulus) funds being
provided resulting in a $5,000 incr ease (from $16,574.00 to $21,574.00) to the City ’s contribution
toward the project.  Doug  Vetter, City Engineer, concurred the cost to the city  will be $21,574.00
pursuant to lim ited federal ARRA funding a llowed, and state d a minor cha nge to rig ht-of-way
wording within the ag reement is also included in the cor rection.  Moti on to rec onsider ca rried.
Voting “ yes” – a ll members of the g overning body  prese nt. Mr. Colli ns moved to approve  the
Cooperative Agreement, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Collins moved to amend by  su bstitute
agreement, including a  substitut e Exhibi t B, sec onded by Mr. Spiker.  Dr. Rinne r aised a point of
order on whether  a past ordina nce be fore the g overning body  which  pertained to rec onsideration
procedures ha d been a pproved.  U pon reque st, Ci ty Clerk Carol I ntlekofer advised the pr evious
ordinance, wh ich would have c hanged City Code reg arding the re consideration process to provide
that items brought up and appr oved for reconsideration at the next reg ular meeting of the governing
body would be postponed with the item re ferred back to a Council standing committee for r eview
prior to a ction, had failed.  She  stated although the ordinance received an affirmative majority vote
of those pre sent, it failed as the r equired six votes to approve  an ordina nce on 3  and fina l readingrd
was not attaine d.  Upon inqu iry, Mr. Vetter  provided infor mation contained within the Coopera tive
Agreement involving the City’s maintenance responsibilities.  Com ment was made in opposition to
the process being followed to reconsider and amend previously approved agenda items without prior
notification to th e public.  Motion to amend ca rried.  V oting “yes” – a ll members of the g overning
body present with the exception of Ms. C ase voting “no”.  Ma in motion as amended by  substitute,
including a substitute Exhibit B, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the g overning body present
13
with the exception of Ms. Case voting  “no”.  U pon request, Ms. Intlekofer provided information on
votes ca st on the ordinance  that fa iled pursuant to Dr. Rinne’ s point of order r aised during
discussion.  (#5318)

matters involving pe nding litig ation (Swan Ranc h annexation and King ston vs. the Cit y and
NextMedia) and another potential litigation matter.  Mr. Collins moved to meet in executive session,
seconded by  Mr. B rown.  Motion car ried.  Voting  “y es” – a ll members  of t he g overning body
present.  Mayor Kaysen announced members would meet in executive session in Council Chambers,
not open t o the public; requested the City  Attorney, City Clerk, and other  staff a s requested by the
City Attorney, be pre sent at the executive se ssion, and stated there  would be no ac tion taken by the
governing body as a result of the executive session, and that the regular City Council meeting would
officially conclude at the close  of the e xecutive session.  (Clerk’s Note: Executive session conve ned
at 9:15 p.m.)
 There being  no further  business to come bef ore the  g overning body  prior to executive
session, the meeting  was o fficially adjourne d at 10:26 p.m. following c losure of the  executive
session.
 Submitted by,
 
 Carol Intlekofer, MMC
 City Clerk
This notice is available in alternative, accessible formats upon request.

The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning

Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments on a pro posed trade of real
property owned by the City of Cheyenne to Cheyenne Hotel Investors, LLC, and Cheyenne Hotel
Investors II, LLC, described as a parcel of land situated in a portion of the Replat of Westland
Subdivision, said subdivision being further described as a portion of Lot 1 of Block One, a portion
of Lot 1 of Block Two and a portion of vacated County Road 51, consisting of approximately 51,423
square feet (estimated appraised value $97,703.70), for real property owned by Cheyenne Hotel
Investors, LLC, and Cheyenne Hotel Investors II, LLC, to the City of Cheyenne, described as a
parcel of land situated in a portion of Lot 4, Block Two, Griffin Addition; and an adjoining portion
of Government Lot 5 of Section 36, T. 14 N., R. 67 W., 6  P.M., Laramie County, lying generallyth
south and west of said Lot 4, all consisting of approximately 75,682 square feet (estimated appraised
value $75,901.45), plus the sum of $21,802.25 which represents the difference between the value
of the land to be exchanged between the parties, said funds to be used for design and construction
of amenities for Pat Griffin Park, (generally located west of Westland Road and east of I-25).  Rick
Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, stated the property involves a portion of land previously
approved for trade between the parties by the governing body, but that the amount of land being
traded has changed since it was last reviewed.  He explained the location of parcels involved in the
proposed t rade and indicated development o f t he pro perty will allow for a f uture gr een s pace
corridor a long Crow Creek, a nd ho tel p roject for t he Cheyenne Hotel I nvestors g roup.  D onn
Edmunds questioned whether a public park would be built at the location with Mr. Parish responding
that the project will allow for future potential development of a park and Greenway access.  Upon
inquiry regarding deed restrictions on the property currently owned by the City, Mr. Parish advised
city staff has spoken with a representative of the previous owners who deeded the property to the
City and that the City has received written agreement to the proposed land trade.  There being no
further comments or discussion and purpose of the hearing having been met, Mayor Kaysen closed
the hearing.  
Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments on a bar and grill liquor license

Mayor Kaysen opened a public hearing to receive comments on a winery permit application
