“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CERTAIN CHAPTERS AND SECTIONS OF TITLE 17,
ZONING, OF THE CHEYENNE CITY CODE BY CHANGING THE DEFINITION OF OFF-
PREMISE SI GN IN SE CTION 17.04. 040, DEF INITIONS, SI GNAGE, A ND B Y A DDING
ADDITIONAL LANGUAGE TO SECTION 17.128.090 G., OFF-PREMISE SIGNS.”  Mr. Collins
and M r. V aldez e ach d eclared a  c onflict of  i nterest ( property ow nership i nvolving l easing of
billboards) for this as well as the next agenda item, and vacated Council Chambers. Frank Cole,
Cole Shopping Center, discussed a 2006 amendment to the zoning code resulting in the proposed
ordinance, C ity c ontrol of  p lacement a nd n umber of  b illboards a nd e ffect on  c ertain p roperty
owners, a nd d istance o f b illboards fr om r esidential a reas, in cluding mixed uses, a nd s poke in
opposition to billboards on developed lots.  Pat Crank, attorney with Speight, McCue and Crank law
firm, representing NextMedia, spoke in favor of the proposed ordinance, which is intended to clarify
an inadvertent error/effect in the zoning code, due to the 2006 amendment, by specifying that off-
premise signs are not billboards, and discussed t he City’s “cap and replace” billboard program.
Troy H ammond, g eneral manager, NextMedia O utdoor Advertising, a lso spoke in favor o f t he
ordinance and the value of billboards to c ommunity businesses and organizations, and referenced
letters of support that had been submitted.  Louise Waters and Donn Edmunds spoke in opposition
to b illboard signs b eing located n ear residential n eighborhoods.  M s. W aters further ex pressed
concern with lighted billboards relative to driver distraction, and effect on community beautification.
Mr. Brown reported there was no recommendation from Public Services Committee on the item.
Mr. Brown moved t o a pprove o n second r eading, seconded by Mr. P ierson.  I t w as no ted t he
ordinance will define an off-premise sign, correcting language that inadvertently changed the intent
of the 2006 amendment.  Discussion followed regarding the 2006 zoning amendment pertaining to
billboards, cap and replace program, and billboards placed since the 2006 amendment and effect of
a recent lawsuit settlement involving NextMedia.  Upon inquiry, Mr. Crank provided information
on the number of NextMedia billboards that have been properly permitted through the City since
the 2006 am endment wen t i nto ef fect, and stated t he rece nt l awsuit i nvolves a co nfidential
settlement.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council
Chambers with the exception of Ms. Case voting “no”.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTERS 17.04 INTRODUCTORY PROVISIONS
AND DEFINITIONS AND 17.128 SIGNS OF TITLE 17, ZONING, OF THE CHEYENNE CITY
CODE 2002.”  Following a ten minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 7:30 p.m.
Pat Crank, attorney with Speight, McCue and Crank law firm, representing NextMedia, stated the
ordinance will authorize digital billboards within City limits.  He reviewed various digital billboard
terminology and definitions contained in the proposed ordinance; stated electronic billboards will
not flash, blink or contain moving messages that may be distractive to drivers, and explained the cap
and replace system, which means the net number of NextMedia digital billboards will not increase
from the 1995 level.  If the ordinance is approved, in order to construct a digital billboard Mr. Crank
expressed that the company must remove 3 b illboards for each (1) digital billboard. He a dvised,
under the City’s cap and replace program, NextMedia currently has 8 credits and, if the ordinance
is approved, NextMedia is prepared to waive those credits.  Mr. Crank  provided information on the
number of proposed digital billboards that may be constructed, removal of other billboards, and an
ordinance provision stating t hat t he support column of a new o r relocated billboard will not be
constructed within 150 linear feet of the property line of a single family dwelling located in a zoned
district in which billboards a re pr ohibited.  He f urther discussed co mmunity b enefits o f digital
billboards involving the ability to timely broadcast public safety messages involving l aw
enforcement announcements and weather alerts, advising that Police Chief Bob Fecht has agreed
to be the “entry point” person for all public safety and law enforcement messages.  Troy Hammond,
general manager, NextMedia, provided information on the reduction percentage of billboard signs
within the community with implementation of the ordinance, and commented on modern technology
enabling b usinesses t o di sseminate adv ertising m essages i n a t imely m anner.  J eff E verson,
Daktronics, I nc., p rovided a  p resentation regarding d igital billboards; LED d isplay a nd lighting
technology; d igital b illboard f eatures; c omputerized m onitoring a nd o versight o f b illboard
operations; timely, efficient and reliable messaging capabilities; different types of message boards,
and community and business benefits.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on second reading, seconded
by Mr. Pierson. Mr. Brown moved to amend by substitute, seconded by Mr. Pierson. Upon request,
City At torney D an W hite e xplained t he d ifferent s ubstitute v ersions c ontained in  t he C ouncil
meeting p ackets a nd c larified t he p roposed substitute o rdinance d ated J une 5 , 2 009 w as t he
appropriate substitute pursuant to the motion to amend.  It was advised the substitute includes the
addition of paragraph G. referencing billboards not being constructed within 150 linear feet of single
family dwellings.  F rank Cole spoke in opposition to the proposed language in paragraph G. as
written, expressing his belief that wording should include any zones that involve residential uses,
such a s M UB a nd M UR z ones.  U pon inquiry, M r. C rank reviewed t he impact t o N extMedia
regarding any extension of the buffering distance.  Motion to amend by substitute carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Mr. Brown moved to
amend paragraph G. of the substitute ordinance by adding the word “residential” after the phrase
“single family”, and changing the word “prohibited” to “permitted”.  Motion died due to lack of a
second.  Discussion followed on the benefits of digital billboards, including the ability to provide
emergency an d saf ety r elated co mmunity m essages; r eduction/elimination o f static b illboards;
quality a nd a esthetic a ppearance o f d igital billboards, a nd p hysical c ondition o f c ertain existing
billboards.  D uring d iscussion a nd u pon i nquiry, M r. H ammond p rovided i nformation o n t he
industry’s move towards digital billboards and company contact information relative to
maintenance/repair issues involving existing billboards, and indicated current billboards that w ill
be removed have yet to be completely identified.  Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes”
– all members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.
Following a 10 minute recess, Mayor Kaysen reconvened the meeting at 8:29 p.m. at which
time Mr. Collins and Mr. Valdez returned to Council Chambers.
“AN ORDINANCE APPROPRIATING MONIES FOR THE CITY OF CHEYENNE, WY
FOR THE VARIOUS PURPOSES IN CONDUCTING THE MUNICIPAL GOVERNMENT OF
SAID CITY AND FIXING THE AMOUNT OF GENERAL AND SPECIAL TAXES AS PART OF
THE REVENUE REQUIRED TO MEET THE SAID APPROPRIATION, ALL FOR THE FISCAL
YEAR BEGINNING JULY 1, 2009, AND ENDING JUNE 30, 2010.”  Barb Dorr, City Treasurer,
advised that since the previous Finance Committee meeting, communication had been received that
the governor would be reducing the state’s direct distribution funding amount to cities and towns
in F Y 2010 b y 5% i nstead o f 10% as i nitially anticipated, an d rev iewed s everal b udget areas
proposed for amendment changes based upon the 5% reduction and recommendations approved by
Finance C ommittee.  J ack Q uirk, M ark S hubert a nd J ohn F afoutakis provided c omments on
suggested cost savings relative to employee salaries and benefits; national unemployment rates, and
comparison o f private vs . p ublic s alary c ompensation a nd be nefit p ackages.  C laus J ohnson,
President, Cheyenne Public Employees Association (CPEA), expressed appreciation for the work
provided by and dedication of City employees, stating many positions are underpaid and the benefit
package helps off set salary levels.  Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second reading, seconded by
Mr. Spiker.  M r. Valdez moved to amend by implementing a 2% pension increase for all eligible
non-uniformed City employees and uniformed Police Department employees effective January 1,
2010, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Mr. Fafoutakis spoke in opposition to increasing the City’s pension
contribution fo r e mployees, e xpressing h is c oncerns w ith the current s tate o f t he e conomy.
Comments followed on  budget s avings to b e r ealized if th e a mendment were approved d ue to
implementation of a 2% i ncrease in January 2010 vs. a 1.5%  increase in July 2009.  M r. Brown
raised a point of clarification questioning if the amendment failed, would a proposed 1.5% pension
increase to be effective July 1, 2009 still be considered as referenced previously during Ms. Dorr’s
comments.  Information was provided relative to the proposed 1.5% pension increase to be effective
July 1 .  It was noted the pension increase is to fairly compensate eligible employees with the samest
pension contribution amount that uniformed members of the Fire Dept. will be receiving due to
FY2010 union contract negotiations.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr clarified any future proposal to change
fire c ontract p rovisions t o re move the City’s i ncreased p ension c ontribution w ould re quire
discussion and approval involving contract negotiations for FY2011.  She further advised uniformed
Police and non-uniformed City employees do not have negotiation rights that union fire employees
have, and provided information on pension amounts paid by the City.  Mr. Pierson moved to amend
the p roposed a mendment by a dding language t o c larify t hat t he 2 % p ension increase would be
effective January 1, 2010 and end June 30, 2010, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Ms. Dorr suggested the
amending language might be be tter s uited for inclusion in t he o rdinances, a lso o n t he meeting
agenda, relative to terms and conditions of employment rather than the budget ordinance.  It was
clarified the motion on the floor was to amend the previous motion to amend by providing for an
effective term of the 2% pension increase from 1-1-2010 to 6-30-2010.  Discussion included future
budget concerns and the option of re-evaluating the pension increase during the FY2011 budget
process.  Motion to amend the proposed amendment carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Broyles,
Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins and
Mayor Kaysen.  Discussion followed on the proposed amendment as amended relative to a 2% vs.
1.5% p ension i ncrease a nd e ffective ti me f rames.  M r. B rown r aised a  p oint of  c larification
regarding the amendment, as amended, with Ms. Dorr confirming if the amendment fails then the
proposal is that eligible employees would receive a 1.5% pension increase in July, which is the same
time frame union Fire Dept. employees would receive the increase.  C omments continued on the
proposed p ension i ncrease and effective ti me f rames, C ity’s f inancial s ituation, s crutiny of
department expenditures, and pension increase uniformity among employees.  D uring comments
regarding fair and equitable treatment for employees and upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr e xplained
employees receiving the 2% pension increase for half a year would receive a less net amount than
those who will receive the 1.5% pension increase for the entire fiscal year.  M otion to a mend to
provide a 2% pension increase for non-uniformed employees and uniformed Police Dept. employees
effective Jan. 1, 2010, as amended to specify the term of the pension increase would be from Jan.
1, 2010 to June 30, 2010, failed.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.
Voting “no” – Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Mr. Valdez
moved to a mend by requesting the Mayor and City Treasurer re-evaluate the proposed budget to
fund the $1 million dollar annual payment to the Board of Public Utilities through other cost savings
allocations, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  C omments followed with some governing body members
expressing their belief that it is the City’s responsibility, as represented to voters, that the City would
help BOPU with the $1 million dollar annual bond payment.  Mayor Kaysen provided information
on City assistance with the bond payment, previous proposal to assess in November the amount of
payment the City may need to contribute, and budget adjustments that would be needed if the $1
million dollar payment is to be fully funded.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Ash,
Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Mr. Collins, Mr. Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  Voting “no” – Ms. Case, Mr.
Pierson and Mayor Kaysen. Mr. Valdez  m oved t o amend t o r emove the GIS budget line item
($10,000.00) from the City Council’s budget and include it in the Engineering Department budget,
seconded by Ms. Broyles. Discussion followed on the initial purpose of placing the GIS funding in
the City Council’s budget, support for retaining the line item in the Council’s budget, and procedure
and time frame for any further amendments to the budget ordinance.  During discussion and upon
inquiry, Ms. D orr explained t o move t he $ 10,000 from t he Council budget t o t he E ngineering
budget, the amendment would be needed.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – Ms. Broyles,
Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Ms.
Case and Mr. Brown.  Upon inquiry, Ms. Dorr advised due to the amendment appropriating funds
for the BOPU payment in the full amount of $1 million dollars, other funding levels will need to be
reduced to compensate for the $300,000 difference.  M r. Pierson moved to reduce City Council
contingency funds by $80,000, leaving $10,000 for each Ward, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Discussion
followed on past use of Council contingency funds.  Motion to amend failed. Voting “yes” – Ms.
Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.  Voting “no” – Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Mr. Collins,
Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Mayor Kaysen suggested that he and the City Treasurer further
review the budget and submit other proposed funding allocation recommendations to members of
the governing body within the next few days.  Upon query, Ms. Dorr provided information on past
funding f or gr affiti rem oval an d Ci ty Co uncil line i tems t hat co uld b e ut ilized f or F Y2010.
Discussion followed on the time frame for further budget ordinance revisions, general fund reserve
levels and past budgets.  M s. Ash moved to a mend to a dd the condition that th e 1.5% pension
increase for non-uniformed employees and uniformed Police Dept. employees would be effective
July 1, 2009 through June 30, 2010, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Ms. Dorr and City Attorney Dan
White each suggested that the amendment language would be more appropriate in the employee
terms and conditions ordinances instead of the budget ordinance.  Ms. Ash withdrew her motion
with concurrence by Mr. Pierson as the second to the motion.  Mr. Collins called for the question
to l imit d ebate.  M ain m otion a s twice amended c arried. V oting “y es” –  a ll m embers o f t he
governing body present.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3814 DEFINING THE TERMS AND
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR NON-UNIFORMED EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE.”  Mr. Valdez moved to approve on second reading, seconded by Mr. Spiker.  Ms. Ash
moved to amend to include language specifying that the term for a 1.5% pension increase would be
effective July 1, 2009 through June 30, 2010, seconded by Mr. Spiker. Rich Wiederspahn, Human
Resources Director, clarified that currently ordinance language does not address the amount of the
City’s contribution toward employees’ pensions, and recommended that the proposed amendment
language be inserted in Section 4, Compensation, of the ordinance.  Mr. Spiker offered a friendly
amendment, accepted by Ms. Ash as maker of the motion, to identify the language would be placed
in Section 4, Compensation, of the ordinance. Upon inquiry, City Attorney Dan White clarified the
City is required by state law to make a certain percentage of employee pension contribution, and that
the C ity c an e lect t o increase it s p ercentage a mount. M otion t o a mend, including t he fr iendly
amendment, carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present with the exception

ANNOUNCEMENTS/REPORTS/MOTIONS:
Announcement was made of a Public Hearing to be held Tuesday, June 16, 2009 at 5:00 p.m.
in City Council Chambers for p roposed budgets for t he City of Cheyenne a nd Board o f Public
Utilities for fiscal year 2010.
Announcement was made of a Special Session of the Governing Body to be held Tuesday,
June 16, 2009 at 5:15 p.m. in City Council Chambers for proposed budgets for the City of Cheyenne
and Board of Public Utilities for fiscal year 2010.
Announcement was made of a calcutta to be held Saturday, July 25, 2009 beginning at 5:30
p.m. a t t he V IP/Sponsor T ent located in Frontier P ark in c onjunction with t he 2 009 Cheyenne
Frontier Days Rodeo.  Proceeds to benefit Cheyenne area charities.
OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
Rick Parish, Director of Parks and Recreation, announced due to pending inclement weather
the first Movies in the Park series scheduled for June 9th in Lions Park at the Amphitheater would
be moved to the Taco John’s Events Center (TJEC). He noted TJEC will be the site for future
movies to be shown during this year’s series based upon inclement weather.
There b eing n o f urther b usiness t o co me b efore t he go verning b ody, t he m eeting was
officially adjourned at 11:02 p.m.

RECORD OF PROCEEDINGS FOR THE GOVERNING BODY
OF THE CITY OF CHEYENNE
Office of City Clerk
June 22, 2009
The Governing Body of the City of Cheyenne met in regular session on this date beginning
at 6 :00 p .m. i n C ouncil Chambers.  P resent w ere: M AYOR -  R ichard L.  K aysen; C OUNCIL
MEMBERS - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Mr. Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr.
Spiker and Mr. Valdez.  The pledge of allegiance was recited.
 
Also present: Carol Intlekofer, City Clerk; Barb Dorr, City Treasurer, and Dan White, City
Attorney.
In c onjunction w ith the  recent a nnual Wyoming As sociation o f M unicipalities (WA M)
convention, M ayor Kaysen r ecognized a nd pr esented 1 5 WA M I nnovation A wards to
representatives of the f ollowing City departments: Planning, Parks and Rec reation, Engineering,
Metropolitan Planning Organization, and Public Works.
Consent Agenda. (All agenda items listed with the designation of [CA]  are considered to be
routine items by the governing body and will be enacted by one motion.  There will be no separate
discussion on these items unless a member of the governing body so requests and support by two
other members is received.  Any item removed from the Consent Agenda will be considered in its
normal sequence on the agenda.)  Upon request by Ms. Case, and pursuant to support received by
Mr.  Collins and Mr. Pierson, agenda item #7 was removed from the Consent Agenda. Mr. Collins
moved to approve  remaining Consent Agenda items, seconded by Mr. B rown.  Motion car ried.
Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM REGULAR MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 8,
2009.  Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
 MINUTES FROM VOUCHERS MEETING OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 15,
2009.  Mr. Spiker moved to approve, seconded by Mr. Valdez.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body.
[CA] MINUTES FROM SPECIAL SESSION OF THE GOVERNING BODY ON JUNE 16, 2009.
Motion (per Consent Agenda) carried.
Mr. Valdez  moved to approve payment of vouchers as presented, seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Motion carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CERTAIN CHAPTERS AND SECTIONS OF TITLE 17,
ZONING, OF THE CHEYENNE CITY CODE BY C HANGING THE DEFINITION OF OFF-
PREMISE S IGN IN SECTI ON 17.04.040, DEF INITIONS, SI GNAGE, AND B Y ADDI NG
ADDITIONAL LANGUAGE TO SECTION 17.128.090 G., OFF-PREMISE SIGNS.”  Mr. Collins
and Mr. Va ldez each de clared a  conflict of inter est (prope rty owner ship involvi ng leasing  of
billboards) for this as well as the next agenda item, and vacated Council Chambers. Ed Strader spoke
in oppositi on to the ordinance, expressed c oncerns with a billboard locate d near an apa rtment
dwelling, commenting on light pollution and effect on tenants due to the lighted billboard; distance
of billboards from streets, rights-of-way and greenway paths, and suggested removal of certain
billboards.  Frank Cole expressed his opinion that street rig hts-of-way should be excluded in the
distance measurement for placement of billboards, and commented on concerns with billboards near
any type of residential use zones.  Reporting for Public Services Committee, Mr. Brown stated there
was no recommendation on the item from Committee.  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and
final reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson.  Upon inquiry , Matt Ashby, Planning Services Director,
stated the current draft of the proposed Unified Development Code does not address a section on
billboards and that whatever is decided by the governing body will be incorporated, and Pat Crank,
attorney with Speight, McCue and Crank law firm, representing NextMedia, advised decisions have
not yet been made regarding placement sites of digital vs. paper billboards. Discussion followed
regarding placement of billboards in proximity to apartments/residential use areas, and purpose of
the ordinance amendment, which is to correct language pertaining to the definition of off-premise
signs due to a 2006 amendment.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body
present in Council Chambers with the exception of Ms. Case and Dr. Rinne voting “no”. (#3858)
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING CHAPTERS 17.04 INTRODUCTORY PROVISIONS
AND DEFINITIONS AND 17.128 SIGNS OF TITLE 17, ZONING, OF THE CHEYENNE CITY
CODE 2002.”  Mr. Brown moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Mr. Pierson.
Upon inquiry, Martin Matsen, Development Director, provided information on the 150 linear ft.
distance for placement of billboards from single family residential properties as proposed in Sec.
17.128.010, G., stating if someone applied for a sign permit a plot plan would be required to reflect
the distance from the property line of the closest residence to the proposed sign pillar, and explained
the use is different than for a multi-family residence.  He confirmed that duplexes and townhomes
are not considered single family residences and if a single family residence is located within a CB
(commercial business) zone and billboards are allowed, the residence would not be afforded the 150
ft. distance protection.  Upon inquiry, Pat Crank, attorney with Speight, McCue and Crank law firm,
representing Ne xtMedia, a dvised N extMedia wo uld c onsider a nd a nalyze a ny re quest f rom a
neighbor involving night time light pollution.  He stated digital billboards are less likely to present
light pollution issues.  Comments followed on neighborhood light pollution concerns, and extending
the 150 ft. distance protection to those zones containing residential housing uses.  Dr. Rinne moved
to amend Sec. 17.128.010, G. to include duplexes and townhouses, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Upon
inquiry reg arding the amendme nt, Mr. Crank cla rified his client would be  ac ceptable to t he
amendment, a nd stated that duplexes and townhouses ar e spec ifically defined in city  code.
Comments followed in support of the amendment.  Motion to amend carried.  Voting “yes” – all
members of the governing body present in Council Chambers.  Upon inquiry regarding placement
of billboards near apartment buildings, Mr. Crank responded with information concerning limited
viable commercial billboard locations relative to the 150 ft. buffer zone restriction and desired high
volume visibility of signs for the benefit of sign advertisers and for public safety messages.  He also
discussed the net reduction in the number of his client’s billboards if the ordina nce is approved.
Main motion as amended carried. Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body present in
Council Chambers.  Following announcement of the vote, Mr. Collins and Mr. Valdez returned to
Council Chambers. (#3859)
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3814 DEFINING THE TERMS AND
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR NON-UNIFORMED EMPLOYEES OF THE CITY OF
CHEYENNE.”  Reporting for Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez moved to approve on third and final
reading, seconded by Ms. Ash.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.
(#3860)
“AN ORDINANCE AMENDING ORDINANCE NO. 3815 DEFINING THE TERMS AND
CONDITIONS OF EMPLOYMENT FOR SWORN OFFICERS OF THE CHEYENNE POLICE
DEPARTMENT.”  Mr . Valdez moved to approve on third and final reading, seconded by Ms.
Broyles.  Motion carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body. (#3861)

“A RESOLUTION RE-APPROPRIATING THE FISCAL YEAR 2009 BUDGET OF THE
CITY OF CHEYENNE ACCORDING TO THE PROVISIONS OF W.S. §16-4-112 AND §16-4-
113.”  Mr. Va ldez moved to adopt, seconde d by Ms. Broyles.  Mr. Valdez moved to amend by
allocating $25,000.00 from FY2009 City Council professional services fund to FY2010 Art in Public
Places fund, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon inquiry, City Treasurer Barb Dorr advised as of May
31, 2009, the balance in t he Art in Public Places (AIPP) account was $51,312.00.  Pur suant to
discussion on the remaining balance in the AIPP fund, Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director,
clarified the most recent piece of art has been commissioned and the funding will come out of the
remaining AIPP fund balance.  Comments included FY2010 budget reduction areas; other types of
art media ns that could be utiliz ed in the community , other than sc ulptures, which ar e not as
expensive; lack of  FY2010 f unding f or AIPP and effe ct on the AI PP C ommittee, a nd the re -
appropriation of funding from the current fiscal year to the next budget year.  Upon inquiry, Ms.
Dorr a dvised as of Ma y 31, 2009, t he Council’s profe ssional service s fund balanc e wa s
approximately $6,765.00, and Kris Jones, Executive Assistant to City Council, advised that a recent
communication with Acc ounting reflected an estimated bal ance of $39,000.00 in the Council’s
professional services line item.  Comm ents followed on the difference in balance amounts in the
professional services line item as reported by Ms. Dorr and Ms. Jones.  During ensuing discussion,
Ms. Dorr offered to provide a review of the line item to ascertain the correct amount of the fund’s
balance prior to the end of the evening’s meeting.  She suggested the funding source could instead
come from the FY2009 general contingency fund.  Mr. Collins moved to amend the amendment to
allocate $2 5,000.00 ou t of  F Y2009 g eneral c ontingency fu nds in stead o f t he City Cou ncil
professional services account, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Upon request, Ms. Dorr advised estimated
funds to be submitted by  departments at budget year-end will be minim al, and r eviewed the re -
appropriation process.  In conjunction with comments regarding the status of the AIPP Committee
if funding is n ot received for the next fi scal year, Mayor Kaysen clarified the Committee will
continue to meet to plan for artwork contributions in the community, and stated he had visited with
the Committee chair regarding lack of funding for the next fiscal year due to budgetary restrictions.
Further discussion followed.  Motion to amend the amendment carried.  Voting “yes” – Mr. Brown,
Ms. Broyles, Ms. Case, Mr. Collins, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Voting “no” – Ms.
Ash, Mr. Pierson and Mr. Spiker.  Discussion continued on support for the AIPP program, funding
cut-backs made during FY2010 budget preparation, and the City’s overall financial picture.   During
comments, Ms. Dorr clarified that during the FY2010 budget preparation, the following areas were
affected by the elimination of funding: Allocation to Parks infrastructure fund; Jeff Ziebart contract;
employee bonuses, and the AIPP.  Ms. Broyles raised a point of clarification questioning the motion
on the floor with Mayor Kaysen advising the pending motion is to approve the initial amendment
as amended to reflect allocation of $25,000.00 from the FY2009 general contingency fund, instead
of from the Council’s professional services line item, to FY 2010 Art in Public Places line item.  Ms.
Broyles moved to amend to allocate $25,000.00 from Council’s FY2009 professional services line
item and requested verification of an accurate balance amount in the Council’s professional services
fund.  To allow Ms. Dorr and Ms. Jones time to research and determine the actual balance amount,
Mayor Kaysen advised the agenda item would be tabled until the end of the meeting agenda, prior
to other City business.  Mayor Kaysen removed the item from the ta ble following reading of the
announcements section on the meeting agenda.  Ms. Dorr clarified there is $6,000.00 currently in
Council’s professional services account and that the balance amount recently provided to Ms. Jones
was in error.  Ms. Broyles withdrew her motion to amend.  Motion to amend as ame nded failed.
Voting “yes” – Ms. Broyles and Mr. Collins.  Voting “no” - Ms. Ash, Mr. Brown, Ms. Case, Mr.
Pierson, Dr. Rinne, Mr. Spiker, Mr. Valdez and Mayor Kaysen.  Upon further query on notable
items contained in the re -appropriation, Ms . Dorr advised a r ecently rec eived invoice  for the
Downtown De velopment Author ity’s 2008 TI F ( tax i ncrement financ ing) in the amount of
$477,156.00 was included, which will leave a general fund reserve balance of about $7,000,000.00,
and provided an explanation of how the TIF payment affects reserves.  Main motion carried.  Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body.  (#5142)

(per Consent Agenda) carried (amount not to exceed $207,448.00).
 Consideration of Bid #R-31-09 for furnishing three (3) new medium/heavy duty body on
chassis transit busses from Intermountain Coach Leasing, Inc., Colorado Springs, CO, for the Transit
Department (American Recovery & Reinvestment Act Funding).  Mr. Valdez moved to accept the
bid submitted by Intermountain Coach Leasing, Inc., Colorado Springs, CO, in an amount not to
exceed $225,869.00, seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Comments followed regarding source of funds, and
future bus replacements relative to options involving emergency evacuation and whe elchair
accessibility areas.  Joe Doughe rty, Transit Dire ctor, advised  the buses a re being fully funded
through Federal stimulus (ARRA) funds; explained wheelchair accessibility for the buses, and noted
that Transit drivers receive bus passenger emergency evacuation training.  Motion carried. Voting
“yes” – all members of the governing body. 
Consideration of Bid #R-33-09 for furnishing three (3) new 2009 Harley Davidson FLHP
motorcycles from Maverick Motorsports, Chey enne, for the Po lice Department (2007-2010 1%
Sales Tax).  Pursuant to questions raised at Finance Committee on the item, Police Chief Bob Fecht
briefly revie wed discontinuation of a  past leasing  prog ram off ered by  the manufa cturer, and
explained the curr ent prog ram a llows for a stra ight t rade-in on the motorcy cles, a dding tha t
equipment upg rades are  additional costs.  He noted t hat due to an invoicing e rror, Maver ick
Motorsports has advised they will waive the amount involved in the error.  Mr. Valdez moved to
accept the bid from Ma verick Motorsports, Chey enne, in a n amou nt not to exceed $2,385.00,
seconded by Ms. Broyles.  Noting that the information provided by Chief Fecht addressed the
questions raised at Finance Committee, Mr. Valdez, pursuant to the Committee’s recommendation,
moved to postpone for two weeks (to July 13, 2009).  Motion died due to lack of a second.  Upon
inquiry, Chief Fecht confirmed the motorcycles are available to the Department at this time.  Motion
to approve carried.  Voting “yes” – all members of the governing body.

OTHER CITY BUSINESS:
John Fafoutakis expressed his concern with potential implementation of a tolling fee on I-80
by WYDOT rela tive to effe cts on local us ers, state residents and truc kers, and encouraged the
governing body to consider official, collective opposition to a tolling fee. 
Rick Parish, Parks and Recreation Director, provided information on the annual SuperDay
event to be held June 27 , advising hours were being extended this year and that a free publicth
concert  will be held that evening at 7:00 p.m. at the amphitheatre (Lions Park).
Ms. Ash announced that Richard Leslie, Executive Director, Wyoming Epilepsy Association,
has bee n named one  of ten nat ional finalists for the 2009 Robert Wood J ohnson Founda tion
Community Health Leaders award, noting this is the first time someone f rom Wyoming has been
named as a finalist.
Ms. Broyles advised Habitat for  Humanity Women Build Project locate d on Messeng er
Court is looking for volunteers to assist with the housing project.
There being no further business to come before the governing body, the meeting  was
officially adjourned at 7:40 p.m.
